## Initializing Catheter Visualization

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/curve\_visualization\_initializing\_3-2\_EN.xml

Before starting initialization, verify the following:

* The ablation patch (if required) and ablation generator are connected to the system.
* The ablation generator is switched on.
* Device for real-time verification of catheter locations is ready for use.

If a fluoroscopy device is used for real-time verification, it is positioned according to guidelines.

Ablation and MAP 1-2 stimulation routing should be avoided during the initialization process. If initialization is started while stimulation to MAP 1-2 is active, the initialization process is postponed until the routing is changed. Once the stimulation routing is changed, initialization starts automatically.

If initialization is started while there is an alert message regarding metal interference, the initialization process is postponed until the alert message disappears. When the message disappears, initialization starts automatically.

14.2.4. V1

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteCaution.png](data:image/png;base64...) | *CAUTION*  Fluoroscopy devices that are too close to the patient might affect location accuracy and, as a result, catheter visualization. |

During initialization of catheter visualization:

* An alert message appears. The message will disappear when initialization ends.
* During initialization of catheter visualization, delivery of energy for ablation will be disabled for a short period. When energy for ablation is available, the impedance reading appears on the RF generator.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Do not switch the ablation generator off during initialization. |

## Exiting a Study

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/study\_exit\_v7p4\_EN.xml

When you exit a Study (Study > Exit) you will be prompted to record the primary arrhythmia. The secondary arrhythmia is optional.

To export study summary data from the CARTO™ 3 System to other applications, select Statistics.

It is recommended to back up all new patient studies at the end of each procedure or day. To facilitate this, you can create a backup when exiting a study (see ["When to Back Up Studies"](#target9000040)).

If an issue occurred during the study and you intend to open a complaint, select the Save Study debug files for further investigation option. This ensures that log files and other debugging information for the Study is not removed during routine Workstation maintenance. Study backup is not affected.

## Review Mode

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/reviewMode\_using\_C3\_EN.xml

Review Mode enables you to review and edit data collected during a CARTO™ 3 Study. You can review a single map in a study stored on the CARTO™ 3 Workstation or a disk. Certain options that cannot be performed in Acquisition mode are accessible in Review Mode.

Review Mode is accessed from the Opening Screen.

When you select a point on the map or in the Point List, the following data is available for review:

* Point data
* Point stability
* Cycle length
* LAT
* Voltage values
* Reference and intracardiac electrograms
* Time at which a point was acquired

All changes made to the study in Review Mode are automatically saved.

![mainScreen_reviewMode.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 31: Main Screen in Review Mode

## Loading Maps from a Previous Study

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/LoadingMap\_prevStudy\_v6\_EN.xml

You can load maps from a previous study for viewing in acquisition mode. The maps must be loaded during the Study Setup phase, before initialization.

The maps are loaded into the Additional Map Viewer. The background is a different color, to distinguish these maps from the current study.

![loadMaps_loaded_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 32: Viewing Maps from Previous Study

## Exiting the CARTO™ 3 System

## Workstation Maintenance and Optimization

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/performance\_optimization\_v8p2\_EN.xml

For consistent response time, the CARTO™ 3 System will regularly perform maintenance and optimization tasks at time of shutdown. These tasks include: defragmentation on the workstation hard disk and updating of antivirus definition files.

If the defragmentation process is scheduled to occur, it starts the next time the workstation is shut down.

The optimization time depends on the number of studies. If the process will take more than a few minutes, the option to cancel appears. Click Cancel to stop the process.

If you cancel the maintenance and optimization process, and the disk fragmentation is more than 30% complete, a message appears with the option to continue the process. You must click Yes to cancel the maintenance and continue shutting down the workstation. Click No to continue with the process.

# File Management

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/FileManagement\_EN.xml

CARTO™ 3 Studies can be backed up to external storage media or a network, and then restored to a CARTO™ 3 Workstation for review.

In addition, the Export Data Tool can be used to export several types of data collected during the study.

These options are accessed through the System Tools dialog box.

The Study Archiver is used for backing up, restoring, and deleting Studies. Studies and Exported Data can be saved to CD, DVD, USB storage device, or the hospital network.

Data that is backed up to external media is encrypted. Data that is backed up to the hospital network is not encrypted.

Studies can be restored from CARTO™ 3 System V3.1.1 and higher.

## When to Back Up Studies

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/backup\_when\_V7\_EN.xml

It is recommended to back up all new patient studies at the end of each procedure or day. To facilitate this, you are prompted to create a backup when exiting a study. In addition, you will be prompted to record the type of arrhythmia.

To protect the system, if the hard disk is more than 95% full, features that use space on the hard disk become unavailable (for example, **Continue Study, New Study, Save As, Restore**). When this situation occurs, an alert or error message appears. To create free space on the hard disk, back up patient data, and then delete it from the hard disk drive.

## Exporting Data

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ExportingData\_v7\_EN.xml

The following types of data can be created during a study:

* Screen captures (\*.jpg)
* Video clips (\*.mp4)
* Log files

Raw data can be exported as text files from Review mode (Study > Export Study Data).

**Exporting During a Study:**

Screen captures and video clips can be exported during a Study or from Review Mode (Study > Media Export. All media files associated with the Study are exported to the selected USB storage device.

The data is encrypted and password-protected. You must enter a valid password in order to export the data. A valid password must be between 8 and 15 characters and contain at least 1 number or non-alphanumeric character.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  You cannot perform any other activities while the export is active. |

**Exporting After a Study:**

After a Study, you can use the Export Data Tool to export additional files that are associated with the Study.

By default, the data is encrypted and password-protected. However, if you are exporting only images and/or video clips, you have the option of exporting without encryption.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteDanger.png](data:image/png;base64...) | **WARNING**  If you choose to export the data without this protection, you must acknowledge that you may be liable for failing to appropriately safeguard this data (e.g., if your device is lost, stolen, or hacked) or if you fail to comply with applicable laws and regulations relating to the privacy of medical records and personal information. |

Be sure to save the password securely. If the password is lost, export the data again. **If the study is no longer available, there is no way to recover the content.**

# Security

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/security\_intro\_7p4\_EN.xml

The CARTO™ 3 System is designed to connect securely to a variety of networks. CARTO™ 3 System security is in accordance with the IEC 80001.1 standard.

CARTO™ 3 System security includes:

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Operating system security | Microsoft® Windows® 10 |
| Firewall protection | Microsoft® Windows® 10 firewall application  Enabled by default. |
| Secure Login | Prevents unauthorized access  Disabled by default. |
| Antivirus protection | Trellix antivirus application  Enabled by default. |

To ensure security and patient privacy, follow these recommendations:

* Enable the secure login feature for password-protected access
* Enforce audit policies
* Enforce use of user management system
* Use strong passwords
* Change passwords regularly
* Ensure that the CARTO™ 3 System is operated only in a secured hospital environment

REP4611 security risks V3 tab Sec.14

If communication to the hospital network is lost during emergency conditions due to failure of the network or physical disconnection, you can use external media to back up or restore data in CARTO™ 3 System.

## Secure Login

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/HIPAA\_intro\_V8p3\_EN.xml

The CARTO™ 3 System complies with all health regulations regarding privacy and integrity by providing a secure login to the system. When enabled, users are required to sign into the CARTO™ 3 System with a username and password. Secure Login records an audit trail file containing the username, time and date of logins, and system event descriptions. When using Secure Login, the system will logoff automatically and return the user to the Login dialogue box after a user-defined idle time is reached.

The audit trail file of system events is available for export by the hospital CARTO™ 3 System Administrator (OR Admin). The audit trail lists system events, identifying them with the following information:

* Time and date
* Username
* Event description.

Table 34: User Types and Access Levels

| User Type | Role Associated with User Type | Access Level |
| --- | --- | --- |
| OR Admin | The Operating Room Admin (OR Admin) is a hospital administrator for the CARTO™ 3 System | User Management for the local Workstation with access to the CARTO™ 3 System and no access to Technician Tools |
| OR User | The Operating Room User (OR User) is an EP Doctor, or other EP staff | Access to the CARTO™ 3 System only, with no access to Technician Tools or User Management |
| CAS | BW Clinical Account Specialist (CAS) | Access to the CARTO™ 3 System, User Management, and partial access to Technician Tools (Upgrade and CAS Tools tabs only) |
| FSE | BW Field System Engineer (FSE) | Access to the CARTO™ 3 System, User Management, and Technician Tools for system configuration and software upgrades |

## Antivirus

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/antivirus\_intro\_EN.xml

The System is protected against security threats and attacks from infected external sources by both an antivirus application and an Allow List. The antivirus application is updated automatically or manually, using antivirus definition files. The CARTO™ 3 Allow List mechanism ensures only approved applications can execute on the CARTO™ 3 Workstation. By default, all other applications are denied execution.

The antivirus definition files can be upgraded automatically or manually. For antivirus definition files to be updated automatically the hospital must configure their network to securely allow communication with Trellix, the antivirus application provider.

* Configure the System for the antivirus application to run automatically. Antivirus definition files are accessed through the hospital network using DICOM connectivity configurations.
* Update manually with updated antivirus definition files via a USB storage device.

You can test connectivity from the System to the Trellix antivirus definition update website using the Test button on the DICOM Network Configuration page, the result appears to the right of the button.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DICOM_AVTest_success.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Indicates connectivity is present. |
| ![DICOM_AVTest_fail.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Indicates no connectivity. |
| ![DICOM_AVTest_notdone.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Indicates a test has not been performed. |

To prevent a negative impact on performance, the System does not start scheduled scans while a study is in progress. If a study is started while a scan is in progress, the scan will stop.

### Virus Scan upon Exiting

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Scan\_and\_shutDown\_EN.xml

When the antivirus application is enabled, the Exit dialog box includes an additional option: Scan & Shut Down.

When Scan & Shut Down is selected, a scan for viruses is performed. A progress bar is displayed, showing the progress of the scan.

After completion of the scan, the workstation shuts down.

### Unremovable Viruses

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/virusDetectedWarning\_3.2\_EN.xml

If the antivirus application detects a virus during a scan of the System, it automatically attempts to remove the virus and repair the infected file without prompting the user for action.

However, if the antivirus application cannot remove a virus, additional action is required. The opening screen, when it next appears, displays a message recommending not to use the System until Biosense Webster Service and Support Department resolves the issue. The New Study, Image Integration, and Review Study buttons are disabled when the message appears.

To continue using the System without resolving the issue, the user must select the check box in the message. All buttons are then enabled.

The message is displayed each time the opening screen appears until the issue is resolved.

### Viruses on USB Storage Devices

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/virusDetectedWarning\_USB\_EN.xml

Connected USB storage devices are scanned only when files are being accessed. When a file with a virus that cannot be removed is detected, a message appears, notifying the user that the file cannot be accessed. The antivirus blocks access to the infected file to protect the operating system.

To continue working, click OK.

## Firewall

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/firewall\_intro\_EN.xml

When enabled, the firewall application blocks unauthorized network access to the CARTO™ 3 System. The firewall application does not affect user workflow.

## Security Maintenance

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/CARTO\_SUG\_Software\_Security\_Maintenance\_EN.xml

The security level of the CARTO™ 3 System software components is regularly maintained by the periodical deployment of **Security Service Packs** according to the Biosense Webster Security for Product policy. Security Service Packs may also be deployed to remedy emerging high-level risk security vulnerabilities.

When enabled, Security Service Packs can be pushed to the CARTO™ System. Indication appears in the opening screen when a new service pack is ready to install.

# ACCURESP™ Module

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/About\_RER\_EN.xml

The ACCURESP™ Module for Respiration Gating enables the CARTO™ 3 System to learn the patient's respiratory motion and train for it. This training allows the user to set a threshold for selective acquisition of location data. When EA points and FAM data are acquired within this threshold (from the end expiration period), heart movement due to respiration is minimal.

The ACCURESP™ Module can be activated or deactivated before or during a clinical procedure as desired.

## Supported Catheters

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/RER\_catheters\_v7p5\_EN.xml

Respiration Gating Training can be performed with any magnetic sensor based catheter.[[16]](#footnote-16)

See also:

* ["Respiration Gating Training"](#target9000041)

## Respiration Gating Options in the CARTO™ 3 System Interface

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/changes\_toGUI\_v6\_EN.xml

The following features and options in the CARTO™ 3 System Interface relate to Respiration Gating:

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ACCURESP™ Module Menu | The ACCURESP™ Module menu command appears in the Tools menu. It opens a submenu with options relating to Respiration Gating. |
| Respiration Gating Window | Initially, the Respiration Gating Window is used to set the training parameters and start respiration training. During training, the window displays a progress bar. After training, the window displays a graph showing respiration amplitude change over time. The graph is color-coded to indicate when the respiratory cycle is within the Respiration Gating Threshold (orange is within the gating threshold; yellow is above the gating threshold). |
| FAM Toolbar | The Respiration Filter option enables or disables the Respiration-Filtered FAM acquisition mode. When this acquisition mode is enabled, FAM location data (volume) is not acquired for data above the Respiration Gating Threshold. When the mode is disabled, all location data is acquired. However, you can choose to hide or show data acquired above the Respiration Gating Threshold. |
| Map Shortcut Menu | The Exclude respiration data option in the Map shortcut (right-click) menu hides or shows FAM data that was acquired above the Respiration Gating Threshold. When enabled, FAM location data that was acquired above the Respiration Gating Threshold is not displayed in the FAM volume or reconstruction.  This option is selected by default. |
| Acquisition Filters | Respiration Gated Acquisition mode can be enabled through the Acquisition Filters tab of the Preferences dialog box. When this option is enabled, the System acquires EA points only when they are annotated and within the Respiration Gating Threshold. |
| Point List | Electroanatomical (EA) Points are labeled ![PtList_inThreshold.png](data:image/png;base64...) (acquired when the respiratory cycle was within the Respiration Gating Threshold) or ![PtList_outThreshold.png](data:image/png;base64...) (acquired when the respiratory cycle was above the Respiration Gating Threshold) in the Respiration (![PtList_RespColumn.png](data:image/png;base64...)) column of the Point List. |
| Beat Buffer | The Beat Buffer is color-coded to indicate whether the data was acquired within or above the Respiration Gating Threshold (orange is within the gating threshold; yellow is above the gating threshold). |
| CARTOSOUND™ Module | Gating status is displayed for frames and contours.[[17]](#footnote-17) |

## Preparing for a Study with Respiration Gating

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/preparations\_EN.xml

### Respiration Gating Training

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/respiration\_training\_v6\_EN.xml

REP4611 A-V1-1.30.4

During the training phase (up to 40 seconds), a supported catheter is placed in the heart. The catheter should be in a fixed location on the endocardium, in an area with a large respiratory swing.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  When using a SOUNDSTAR™ Catheter for Respiration Gating Training, place the catheter tip on the coronary sinus ostium (CS Os) or slightly inside the proximal part of the CS. |

For a ventilated patient, you can select Mechanical ventilation before training. When this option is active, point acquisition in Respiration Gated Acquisition Mode is faster. (For information regarding this mode, see ["EA Points and Respiration Gating"](#target9000042)).

After the training period, the Respiration Graph is displayed in the Respiration Gating window (see ["Respiration Graph"](#target9000043)).

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  REP4611 v3 tab, 1.1.1.7   * Do not touch the patches during Respiration Gating training.   If patches must be touched during training to fix patch adherence, restart training after adjusting the patches.   * Respiration Gating training will fail if the training catheter is disconnected during training. |

Retraining is required in the following situations:

* After System initialization is performed
* After resetting catheter visualization
* When a patch error occurs
* When an alert and/or system message appears, indicating that training is required
* When Respiration Gating Training fails

If a chest or back patch is disconnected during a Study with Respiration Gating, the displayed gating status will be incorrect.

If this occurs:

1. Reconnect the patch.
2. Initialize catheter visualization.
3. Repeat Respiration Gating Training.

### Respiration Gating Threshold

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/set\_gatting\_v6\_EN.xml

The Respiration Gating Threshold marks the preferred acquisition phase of the respiratory cycle. FAM data and EA Points are labeled according to whether they were acquired within the threshold or above this threshold. The threshold is set by the System.

REP4611 v3 tab, 1.1.1.5, v2 tab 1.3.1.5

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteCaution.png](data:image/png;base64...) | *CAUTION*  If the patient's respiration rate rises to 30 breaths per minute or higher, the ACCURESP™ Module will not function correctly. If this occurs, continue to work according to current practice, without using the ACCURESP™ Module. |

REP4611 D-V2-1.3.1.1

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  A temporary change to the respiration pattern might lead to incorrect gating. If such a temporary change in respiration is noted, treat automatically-acquired points or volume according to current practice. |

### Respiration Graph

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/view\_realTimeGraph\_v6\_EN.xml

The Respiration Gating window displays a real-time graph showing the respiration amplitude change over time. Time scale and amplitudes can be adjusted by using the arrow keys.

If the window is closed, click Tools > ACCURESP™ Module > Graphs Viewer Respiration Graph

The graph is color coded:

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Yellow | Above the gating threshold |
| Orange | Within the gating threshold |
| Gray | The current respiratory cycle has not ended, so the calculation to determine if this data is above or below threshold has not been performed. The status will be updated at the end of the respiratory cycle. |

The Respiration Graph can be used as an indicator for acquiring points within the gating threshold.

VSTS 36415: NTF

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Ensure that the Respiration Graph is not positioned over a toolbar. If the Respiration Graph is positioned over a toolbar, the graph display might be distorted. |

## Mapping with Respiration Gating

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/rer\_mapping\_EN.xml

Respiration Gating is based on data measured during a full respiratory cycle. Determining whether data is within the threshold or above it requires that the respiratory cycle has finished.

The FAM reconstruction is colored and EA point data are labeled according to their gating status, so data above the threshold can be excluded or included as desired.

This respiration gating status is retained when performing Save Map As or Remap.

### FAM Reconstructions and Respiration Gating

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/rer\_famReconstructions\_v6p5\_EN.xml

During FAM acquisition, all location data is acquired, even if it is above the Respiration Gating Threshold. You can control the display of the data above the threshold.

* Manual exclusion mode (default)

The volume that was acquired within the gating threshold is colored orange and volume that was acquired above the threshold is colored yellow. The yellow volume can be included in the map, or permanently deleted with a special mode of the FAM Eraser Tool.

* Automatic exclusion mode

Volume that was acquired above the threshold is excluded from the map display.

You can switch between the manual and automatic FAM exclusion modes by selecting Exclude respiration data in the Map shortcut menu. Changing modes clears the FAM regional resolution level.

If Exclude respiration data is enabled, it is indicated in the Map List.

### EA Points and Respiration Gating

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/rer\_eaPoints\_6-3\_EN.xml

When EA points are acquired with the ACCURESP™ Module active, the Respiration (![PtList_RespColumn.png](data:image/png;base64...)) column of the Point List indicates the gating status of each point.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![PtList_inThreshold.png](data:image/png;base64...) | The point was acquired within the Respiration Gating threshold |
| ![PtList_outThreshold.png](data:image/png;base64...) | The point was acquired above the Respiration Gating threshold |
| ![PtList_NA.png](data:image/png;base64...) | The ACCURESP™ Module was not active when the point was acquired, or the Respiration Gating status calculation has not ended. The status will be updated at the end of the calculation. |

If the Respiration column is hidden, click ![PtListArrow.png](data:image/png;base64...) and select Respiration from the list.

When points are copied or moved to another map, they retain the ![PtList_inThreshold.png](data:image/png;base64...) or ![PtList_outThreshold.png](data:image/png;base64...) labels.

The Beat Buffer also indicates the gating status. If the calculation has not ended, beats are white.

In Respiration Gated Acquisition Mode, the System acquires EA points only when they are within the Respiration Gating Threshold. This option is enabled through the Acquisition Filters tab of the Preferences dialog box. When this option is enabled, the System waits for the first annotation that is also within the Respiration Gating Threshold every time the EA acquisition button is clicked. You can temporarily override the feature, if needed, by clicking the button again.

For a ventilated patient, you can select Mechanical ventilation before training. When this option is active, point acquisition in Respiration Gated Acquisition Mode is faster.

1.3.1.1. V1

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteCaution.png](data:image/png;base64...) | *CAUTION*  When Mechanical ventilation was selected during training and Respiration Gated Mode is active, points are acquired within one respiratory cycle based on a preliminary gating value. After a second respiratory cycle, the gating status is confirmed.  If Mechanical ventilation Mode is used for a non-ventilated patient, the gating status for points acquired may change after the confirmation process. Points that were marked with ![PtList_inThreshold.png](data:image/png;base64...) change to ![PtList_outThreshold.png](data:image/png;base64...) in the Point List. Evaluate these points carefully. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Respiration Gating features also apply to points acquired with a multi-electrode mapping catheter. |

### Respiration Gating and the CARTOSOUND™ Module

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/rer\_cartoSound\_EN.xml

Respiration Gating Status can be displayed in the CARTOSOUND™ Module.[[18]](#footnote-18) For availability, contact the Biosense Webster Service and Support Department.

After a clip is stopped, the respiration gating status for each frame is displayed, indicating whether the frame was taken inside the respiration gate (above the threshold) or outside the gate (below the threshold).

After a contour is assigned, the Respiration Gating status of the contour (that is, whether it was drawn over a gated frame or not) is displayed in the Point List.

The Respiration Gating status for frames and contours is also available in Review Mode.

# Advanced Reference Annotation (ARA)

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/signalAnotation\_title\_v7\_EN.xml

## ARA Overview

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/signalAnotation\_AdvancedReferenceIntro\_v7\_EN.xml

**ARA** (Advanced Reference Annotation) is a reference annotation algorithm that uses up to 5 intracardiac bipolar channels of the reference catheter and body surface ECG channels to define the reference timing. The algorithm, which uses the signal's center of energy for reference annotation calculation, dynamically evaluates the ECG channels to eliminate channels with noise and automatically determines both atrial activation time and ventricular activation time.

The dynamic channel selection and differentiation between atrial and ventricular activity allows for detection and repeatability of reference annotations in complex arrhythmias.

The ARA algorithm has the following modes for calculating reference annotations:

* Automatically selects multiple bipolar intracardiac (IC) channels (up to 5 of the most distal) of the selected reference catheter for atrial cases
* Any single user-defined IC bipolar channel of the selected reference catheter for atrial cases
* Body surface ECG precordial channels (V1-V6)

REP9626 1.1.7, V7 ARA

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE   * Using the ARA algorithm for the following clinical scenarios may result in decreased performance:   + Atrial Fibrillation   + AVNRT or accessory pathway arrhythmias (WPW) with an IC reference   + An IC catheter in the ventricle as a ventricular reference   + When atrial activation constantly coincides with the QRS complex with an IC reference * In these cases, it is recommended to use one of the alternative Reference Criteria provided by the CARTO™ 3 System. * The CARTOUNIVU™ Module Cine mode cannot be used with ARA. |

## Performing a Study with ARA

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ARAFeature\_PerformingStudyARA-TopLevelHeading\_EN.xml

Verify the following before activating ARA:

* The body surface ECG patches (precordial leads) are placed on the patient and connected to the CARTO™ 3 System.

This is necessary because all IC annotations are checked against the precordial leads to filter out ventricular activations from an atrial map.

* When working with IC ARA, before acquiring an ULS contour or EA points (either continuous or manual), ensure that a physically stable catheter (desired to be the reference catheter) is connected to the PIU and selected in the Map Setup screen (for example, CS).

REP9626 1.1.4, V7 ARA

* There must not be significant noise on the reference catheter signals. The ARA algorithm only uses signals with good signal-to-noise ratio for reference annotation calculation.

### Activating ARA

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ARAFeature\_ActivatingARA\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | In the Map Setup screen, select the Mapping Catheter from the drop-down list. |
| 2. | Select the Chamber of Interest from the drop-down list. |
| 3. | Set the Reference Criterion to Automatic.  This selection ensures that ARA is the reference annotation algorithm used in the map.   |  |  | | --- | --- | | ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Automatic is the default Reference Criterion for the following Study Templates: Atrial Flutter, Focal Atrial Tachycardia, Ventricular Tachycardia, and Other. | |
| 4. | Select the proper Reference Channels according to the Chamber of Interest.  You can select channels for any catheter defined by the template, whether connected or disconnected (shadowed), except for the mapping catheter.   |  |  | | --- | --- | | Atrium | Multi-channel: Select REF All for the Channels of the IC reference catheter, or select BS V1 - V6.  ![ARAFeature_MapSetup_Multi-channel_Atrium.png](data:image/png;base64...)  For IC reference: The 5 most distal paired bipolar IC channels of the reference catheter are evaluated by the algorithm for use as references. (In the example above: REF1-2, REF3-4, REF5-6, REF7-8, REF9-10.) | | Atrium | Single channel: Select a single bipolar IC channel from the selected reference catheter's available channels.  ![ARAFeature_MapSetup_SingleChannel_Atrium.png](data:image/png;base64...) | | Ventricle | Multi-channel: BS V1-V6 is automatically selected and displayed in gray as Channels to be used as the reference.  ![ARA_MapSetup_Ventricle.png](data:image/png;base64...) | |
| 5. | (Optional) Select the desired color for the reference annotations. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  There is an annotation latency when using the ARA algorithm due to annotation calculation time. The dashed line of the ARA reference annotation in the Monitor pane may be displayed up to 850 ms after signal display in the Monitor pane. |

### ARA Algorithm Training Period

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ARAFeature\_AlgorithmLearning\_EN.xml

A training period is required for the ARA algorithm to evaluate ECG data and to analyze cardiac activity and environmental noise on the signals. During the training period, the ARA algorithm selects the channels to be used for annotation calculations, an alert appears, and continuous acquisition is disabled. After the training period, the channels to be used for annotation calculations are selected, reference annotations appear, and acquisition is enabled.

The ARA algorithm training period differs, depending on the Reference Channels used in the study:

* Body surface- 9 seconds
* IC- 18 seconds

If this ECG data is not available (for example, if the catheter was disconnected and reconnected), the system waits until the data is accumulated. An alert appears and continuous acquisition is disabled.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Reconnecting the reference catheter during the training period may trigger a re-training period. |

A training period is automatically activated in the following situations:

* Setting the Reference Criterion to Automatic, such as when creating a new map or during remap

The training is repeated every 5 seconds until points or contours are acquired.

* Changing the Reference Channels in an empty map (without points or contours)
* Starting continuous acquisition in an empty map (without points or contours)
* Manually acquiring the first electroanatomical point in a map
  + The point will be on the second annotated beat after clicking the acquisition button (due to training)
  + The beat buffer will be cleared, due to training. (Beats will start to accumulate after clicking the acquisition button, so 10 beats after clicking the acquisition button, the beat buffer will be full.)

When an EA point or an ULS contour is acquired, the selected IC channels used for ARA calculation for that map are fixed.

### User Interface

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ARAFeature\_UserInterface\_EN.xml

ARA data is displayed in the following ECG viewers:

* Annotation Viewer
* Baseline Point Viewer
* Monitor
* Pattern Bank
* Selected Point Viewer

When single-channel ARA is used:

* The reference annotations are displayed as single dots, colored according to the Reference Color setting in the Map Setup screen, in all relevant ECG viewers.
* The selected bipolar channel is displayed with an R label in all ECG viewers.
* The reference channel is displayed by default in all ECG viewers.

![ARA_Single-channel_Atrium_SPV_Dot.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 33: Single-channel ARA Reference Annotations- Selected Point Viewer

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  When using ARA, the algorithm uses the signal's center of energy for reference annotation calculation. Therefore, the reference annotation will not always be in a consistent fixed position, such as the maximum value. If you suspect that a reference annotation is inaccurate, edit the reference annotation or delete the corresponding point or points on the map. If you suspect that many reference annotations are inaccurate, start a new map. |

When multi-channel ARA is used:

* The reference annotations are displayed as dashed lines in the ECG viewers, colored according to the Reference Color setting in the Map Setup screen, in all relevant ECG viewers.
* The channels that are used for reference annotation calculation are displayed with an R label in the ECG viewers.
  + In atrial studies, the 5 most distal bipolar channels of the selected catheter are evaluated by the algorithm. The channels that are selected for reference annotation calculation are displayed with R labels and may be a subset of the 5, depending on their quality to serve as good reference channels. When an EA point or an ultrasound contour is acquired, the selected IC channels used for ARA calculation for that map are fixed.
  + When using body surface ECG reference channels, BS V1-V6 channels are displayed with R labels and evaluated by the algorithm with each annotation.
* The system doesn't automatically add reference signals to the display in ECG viewers (to prevent overcrowding of the signal display).

You can manually add channels to the display.

![ARA_Multi-channel_Atrium_SPV.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 34: Multi-channel ARA in the Atrium- Selected Point Viewer

![ARA_VENTRICLE_MAPPING_SPV.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 35: Multi-channel ARA in the Ventricle- Selected Point Viewer

When ARA is selected, the Ultrasound window and pattern viewers (Pattern Matching Viewer, Pattern Bank, etc.) display the following:

* Body surface ECG reference channel: V5 precordial lead
* Multi-channel IC reference: The most distal bipolar channel evaluated for reference annotation calculation for that map (i.e., the most distal of the map's fixed channels)
* Single-channel IC reference: Selected bipolar reference channel

![ARA_ULSViewer_Ventricle.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 36: Ultrasound Window: Body Surface ECG Reference Channel

![ARA_ULSViewer_Atrium.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 37: Ultrasound Window: Atrial Mapping

![ara_pattern_viewer.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 38: Pattern Matching Viewer with ARA

### Functionality When No Reference Annotations are Detected

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ARAFeature\_NoReferenceAnnotations\_EN.xml

There are several conditions where reference annotations are not detected. If an alert appears that no reference annotations are detected, continuous acquisition is disabled.

The system adds a visual indication by the Acquisition Panel.

![ARAFeature_AcquisitionPanel_NoRefAnnotation.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 39: No Reference Annotation Indication

Manual point acquisition is still possible.

* The acquired point type is Location Only.
* The acquired point does not have a LAT value. Edit the point manually to set a LAT value.
* The acquired point has a comment in the Point List: "No Reference Annotation Point".
* If the point is acquired during ablation, it receives the appropriate ablation tag (according to the ablation mode).

Annotation Viewer display is continuously updated, but without any reference or Window of Interest (WOI) lines.

CARTOSOUND™ Module: The ultrasound fan is not displayed in the Ultrasound window. The SOUNDSTAR™ Catheter visualization is not displayed in the map viewer.

### Copying or Moving Points between Maps

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ARAFeature\_CopyMovingPoints\_EN.xml

You can copy or move points from maps with ARA (all types) to maps with any other reference annotation, including ARA. You cannot copy points from maps with a different reference annotation algorithm to maps with ARA.

ARA to ARA copy or move must be between maps with the same ARA type:

* Multi-channel BS to multi-channel BS
* Single-channel IC to single-channel IC (same channel)
* Multi-channel IC to multi-channel IC (same reference catheter)

When copying or moving points from maps with ARA to maps with a different reference annotation algorithm or from maps with ARA to maps with ARA, the system automatically adjusts the reference annotations according to the target map's reference annotation algorithm. This adjustment also occurs when using the CARTOREPLAY™ Module to retroactively acquire ARA reference points.

In maps with multi-channel IC ARA, the algorithm selects which channels to take into consideration and requires a shared channel or channels used for reference detection between maps. When copying or moving from multi-channel IC ARA to multi-channel IC ARA, a point will not be copied or moved if the destination map does not share any reference channel with the target map.

### ARA Behavior when Pacing is Active

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ARAFeature\_PacingBehavior\_EN.xml

The ARA reference annotation algorithm detects when pacing is performed via a catheter connected to the CARTO™ 3 System. ARA differentiates between pacing from the Reference Channels and pacing from catheters whose channels are not Reference Channels:

* For pacing from a Reference Channel, the reference annotation is located on the pacing spike.

The pacing spike is followed by a blanking period of 175 ms on the reference channels. No annotations are issued during the blanking period.

* For pacing from a catheter connected to the CARTO™ 3 System, whose channels are not Reference Channels, possible pacing artifacts are filtered out since they may conflict with chamber activation signals on the Reference Channels.

The pacing spike is filtered out by replacing it with a linear function, 7 ms before the spike and up to 35 ms after the spike.

* For pacing from a catheter that is not connected to the CARTO™ 3 System, such as a pacemaker or a catheter connected to the EP recording system, the pacing artifact is not removed by the algorithm, and it may lead to an inconsistent reference annotation.
* During pacing, a pacing artifact appears on the signals shortly after the last pacing spike (around 2 seconds). In rare cases, when using high power pacing, the presence of the pacing artifact may cause the ARA algorithm to temporarily miss annotations for a few beats following the pacing artifact.

REP10631\_1.0, Section 5.1.4.2

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  When multi-channel ARA is used, ensure that all pacing stimulation is routed through the CARTO™ 3 System. Stimulation that is not routed through the CARTO™ 3 System might cause inconsistency in the reference annotation and inaccuracies in the map. |

# CARTOMERGE™ Image Integration Module

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/CartoMergePlus\_containter\_c3\_EN.xml

## CARTOMERGE™ Module Overview

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/About\_CartoMerge\_v6\_EN.xml

The CARTOMERGE™ Image Integration Module[[19]](#footnote-19) enables import of segmented data of CT or MRI image studies into three-dimensional (3D) segmented surface images (referred to as *surface images*) of the desired cardiac chamber or structure.

After import to the CARTO™ 3 System, you can register the surface images to real-time map point data.

See also:

* ["Image Processing Package"](#target9000044)
* ["CARTOSEG™ Segmentation Modules"](#target9000045)

## Importing CT and MRI Images

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Importing CT and MRI Images\_EN.xml

You can import CT or MRI studies to the CARTO™ 3 System via CD or network connection. Studies can be original (raw data sent in 2D slices and 3D consistent) or processed on a radiology workstation, as long as they are 3D-consistent and saved in DICOM 3 format. Derived or reformatted series **must not** include a localizer slice.

The CARTOMERGE™ Module supports use of derived or imported series from the General Electric AW Workstation, Siemens Leonardo Workstation, and Philips Extended Brilliance Workspace (EBW). It also supports import of 3D models (single segmented structure) created on the CardEP Module of the AW Workstation. Importable data is defined as follows:

* **Original 3D Consistent (raw data):** An original volume of data provided in bulk as 2D images which compose a 3D consistent volume, typically provided from a CT or MRI scanner.
* **Derived/Reformatted 3D Consistent:** The same as raw data, except that the volume is derived on the radiology workstation. The voxels of the segmented volume retain their original values, while other voxels receive an intensity value of zero (0). Data is saved in standard DICOM 3 format and should be 3D consistent.
* **3D Model:** A 3D model is reformatted on the CardEP module of the AW workstation. The voxels of the segmented volume retain their original values, while other voxels receive an intensity value of zero (0). Data is saved in a proprietary Biosense Webster format supported by major CT/MRI system vendors such as Siemens, Philips and GE.

When a study is imported, each valid series is saved to the system hard disk as a unique 3D Image Study. The name of the imported study is based on the numbers of the original image study and series. For example, Image Study 6, series1 is called "Study6.1" in the image integration module. All relevant DICOM attributes of the study are also imported.

Defect 11725

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Image Studies that are not associated with a CARTO™ 3 Study do not appear in study selection lists when studies are sorted by date. |

In addition, when importing a derived/reformatted series or 3D model, the surface of the segmented object is extracted.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  You cannot import data from derived or reformatted 3D consistent images without a localizer slice. |

### CARTOMERGE™ Module Enhancements

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoMerge\_advanced\_intro\_c3\_EN.xml

The CARTOMERGE™ Module Enhancements[[20]](#footnote-20) include the following options:

* Importing segmented colored surface images
* Loading segmented images directly from the DICOM network

These features are described below.

#### Importing Colored Surface Images

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cm\_coloredImages\_import\_V7\_EN.xml

Color images can be used in a CARTOMERGE™ Study, if the images are in the supported format. You can either show the original image color, or you can use the CARTOMERGE™ Module anatomical coloring option.

If the images are corrupted or not in the supported format, an error message appears. Only triangles and quadrilaterals are supported.

For details on the correct format, contact the Biosense Webster Service and Support department.

#### Loading Segmented Images

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/LoadSegmentedImage\_EN.xml

Medical imaging systems often perform segmentation on the images they produce. If such segmented images are saved on the DICOM network using the Biosense Webster proprietary format, the CARTOMERGE™ Module can load them directly from the network. You do not need to prepare and import these images with the Image Processing Package, so the workflow of the procedure can continue without interruption. In addition, this feature facilitates immediate use of images produced during an ongoing procedure.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  To enable loading segmented images directly from the DICOM network, ensure that the DICOM network is configured on the CARTO™ 3 System. |

## Registering Surface Images to CARTO™ 3 Maps

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Registering Surface Images to Carto XP Maps\_EN.xml

Before beginning the process of registration, you must begin a new study (or open a previous study), start a CARTOMERGE™ Study, and have a surface image displayed in the Main Map Viewer. It is important to understand how a surface image is displayed, and what the various options are for manipulating it. You should also be familiar with the concept of *Landmark Pairs* and how they are applied. Finally, you need to understand the different types of registration, and other options available to assist in registration.

### Landmark Pairs

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Understanding Working with Landmark Pairs\_EN.xml

A *landmark* is a named flag that is attached to a specific site on a surface image, contour, or a point on a map. For best results, landmarks should be placed as far apart as possible within the target registration area.

A *landmark pair* is a set of two identical flags (name and color) that are attached to both a surface image and a point on the map or a contour. Landmark pairs are used to register a surface image to a CARTO™ 3 Map. Thus, they must be placed in the same position on the surface image and on the map. For example, if a landmark is placed in the coronary sinus, it should be *attached* to a point acquired in the same part of the coronary sinus. When the landmark is attached to the corresponding point, a landmark pair is created.

Landmarks can be renamed or deleted. Landmark pairs can also be detached.

The landmark flags are broken when they are not attached to another landmark. Landmark pairs are two identical flags; one is applied to the volume image and one is applied to a point on a map (or a contour on the map). The Point shortcut menu is used to attach the landmark on the surface image to the point on the map.

"1.10.1.11.40.7.11.1.2." "1.10.1.1-V21.40.7.-V111.1.2.-V1"

REP4611 D-V3-1.5.1.1

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteDanger.png](data:image/png;base64...) | **WARNING**  Proper registration is dependent on the correct placement of landmark pairs in corresponding locations on the surface image and map. Failure to place landmark pairs in the correct corresponding locations will provide invalid registration results. |

### Registration Process

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/The Registration Process\_EN.xml

The registration process is dependent on map points (or contours). Once started, several steps must be performed to achieve optimal registration results.

In some cases, the first step will be visual alignment or landmark registration, followed by surface registration. Registration results can be assessed using the statistical tools included with the CARTOMERGE™ Module.

#### Manual Alignment

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Manual Alignment\_EN.xml

Manual alignment is performed by manually rotating or changing the X-Y axis of a surface image. This is done using the Rotation and XY Move options.

#### Visual Alignment

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Understanding Visual Alignment\_EN.xml

Visual alignment assumes that the rotation of a surface image and a CARTO™ 3 Map is similar. When performed on a specific landmark pair, the volume image and map are moved so that both landmarks occupy the exact same location. Once visual alignment has been performed, apply surface registration.

1.10.1.1 V2

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteDanger.png](data:image/png;base64...) | **WARNING**  Visual alignment assumes that the orientation of the surface image is identical to the coordinate system of the CARTO™ 3 System. If this assumption is not true, the visual alignment method may be susceptible to severe errors.  Use visual alignment only as an initial registration method. |

#### Landmark Registration

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Understanding Landmark Registration\_EN.xml

Landmark registration fits the surface image to a CARTO™ 3 Map based on the placement of at least three landmark pairs.

#### Surface Registration (Overall)

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Understanding Surface Registration (Overall)\_EN.xml

Surface registration fits the surface image to the map's surface based on the overall surface of the map (that is, all acquired points or contours). Thus, the more points or contours acquired, the better the surface registration.

Perform surface registration after acquiring enough points or contours map of interest. Ensure that the points are well distributed within the volume. Surface registration is disabled until visual alignment or landmark registration is applied.

#### Matching Multiple Maps and Surface Images

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/MultipleMaps\_SurfaceImages\_c3\_EN.xml

The CARTO™ 3 System can create multiple maps of the same or different chambers. In certain cases, you might want to associate several maps to the same surface image. This is done using the Match Image/Match CARTO Map options. Multiple images cannot be associated with the same map.

Maps and images must be registered before they can be associated, or matched to a surface image.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  To register multiple maps with surface images, Multi Cavity Registration must be selected. |

Once associated with each other, Surface Registration can be reapplied to all associated maps, vessels, or images displayed in the map viewers. Surface registration moves all images to the best fit (minimum distance) with their associated maps.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Always verify that the correct map has been matched to the correct surface image. Incorrect matching of maps to surface images will result in incorrect registration, and misleading information regarding patient data.  Always verify registration before matching maps to surface images. |

#### Visualizing Catheter Tip Location on Surface Images

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/VisualizingCatheterTipLocation\_SurfaceImages\_c3\_EN.xml

When surface images are displayed in the Main Map Viewer, the location of the catheter tip can be shown in relation to them. This is possible via the Projection option. When enabled, if the location of the catheter tip is 10 mm or less from the surface of any displayed image, a blue disc-shaped shadow is projected onto the surface image. Display of the projection of the catheter tip is updated as changes are made to the maps or surface images.

#### Projection of Tags on Surface Images

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Project\_Tags\_v7\_EN.xml

The projection of tags on surface images is possible for surface images that are matched to specific maps. You can project this information for all maps associated with images in the Main Map Viewer, or for only a specific image.

1.40.7 V1

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteCaution.png](data:image/png;base64...) | *CAUTION*  The projection of tags is dependent on the quality of registration between the map and CT or MRI image. Verify the quality of registration before projecting tags onto surface images. |

Projections of tags on surface images in the Additional Map Viewer is based entirely on how data is displayed in the Main Map Viewer.

If only a surface image is displayed in the Additional Map Viewer, but the maps and image are displayed in the Main Map Viewer, the projection of tags is shown on the image in the Additional Map Viewer.

### Verifying Registration

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/VerifyingRegistration\_c3\_EN.xml

Verification of effective registration is essential, to be able to refer to the surface image during a CARTO™ 3 Study. The quality of registration can be verified by maneuvering the catheter on the chamber walls or vessel structures and checking for consistency between the real-time position of the catheter icon and the currently used registered anatomical structures. In addition, examining the intracardiac signals can help determine the location of the chamber wall relative to the catheter tip.

Statistical data and calculations regarding the closeness of fit are viewed in the Landmark and Surface tabs of the Match Statistics panel. A visual representation of the fit can also be displayed via the Registration Match View option. Careful review of the information provided via these options will help determine how closely the surface image and CARTO™ 3 Map match after registration.

1.40.x V1

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteCaution.png](data:image/png;base64...) | *CAUTION*  Use of the Match Statistics and Registration Match View does not replace your common clinical practice for real-time verification of catheter locations throughout the procedure (inspection of IC signals, direct imaging guidance such as fluoroscopy or ultrasound, etc.). Final determination of effective registration is recommended, and should be performed on the target area of interest. |

#### Statistical Data for Verifying Registration

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/StatisticalData\_VerifyingRegistration\_c3\_EN.xml

The Match Statistics panel displays data relating to the degree of match for landmark registration, surface registration, or both. The Match Statistics are not saved with the study, and they apply only to the most recent registration that was performed. The Multi Cavity Registration option affects how Match Statistics (Surface) are displayed for surface registration. When Multi Cavity Registration is selected, the accuracy data is based on all points and contours of all matching maps displayed in the Main Map Viewer. When not selected, the accuracy data is based only on all points and contours in the active map.

The following data is displayed in the Match Statistics panel:

* **Match Statistics (Landmarks)**: The distance between each landmark of all displayed landmark pairs, as well as the average, standard deviation, minimum, and maximum distances (in mm) of all displayed landmark pairs.
* **Match Statistics (Surface)**:
  + Multi Cavity Registration **not selected**: the name of the map and surface image being compared, as well as the average, standard deviation, minimum, and maximum distance (in mm) between the surface of the volume image and of the displayed map.
  + Multi Cavity Registration **selected**: the average, standard deviation, minimum, and maximum distance (in mm) between all displayed matching maps and images.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  VSTS 36404: NTF  The Match Statistics table is not updated continuously. Close the Match Statistics table before adding landmarks, matching landmarks, or removing images after registration.  If the Match Statistics table was open while changes were made, close and reopen the table to update the statistics. |

#### Visual Verification of Registration with Match View

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/VisualVerification\_Registration\_MatchView\_c3\_EN.xml

When the Registration Match View is displayed, all points on the map are colored according to their proximity to the surface of the surface image. The exact distance of each point from the surface of the surface image can also be viewed. Coloring of points on the map is as follows:

* **Green**: 0 to 5 mm between the map and the surface image
* **Yellow**: 5 to 10 mm between the map and the surface image
* **Red**: more than 10 mm between the map and the surface image

## Image Processing Package

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/overview\_ImageProcessingPackage\_c3\_EN.xml

The Image Processing Package is used for importing, rendering, segmenting, and editing the raw data of CT or MRI studies. The result is 3D segmented surface images of the chamber or structure of interest. These segmented images are then exported to the CARTO™ 3 System for use in real time during an EP study.[[21]](#footnote-21)

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  VSTS 36351: NTF  If the Import window of the Image Processing Package is closed during the importing of a CT or MRI study, the Image Processing Package freezes.  If this occurs, reopen the Image Processing Tool and keep the Import window open until image importing is completed. |

### Segmenting CT or MRI Images with the Image Processing Package

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Segmenting CT-MR Images with the Image Processing Package\_EN.xml

The type of image data imported will determine the extent to which images must be segmented. If original (raw) data is imported, you must completely segment the image into the desired cardiac chamber or structure. When importing a derived reformatted series or 3D model (for instance, using the CardEP module on the AW workstation) from a radiology processing workstation, you can also refine segmentation of the imported image. These procedures are performed via the CARTOMERGE™ Image Processing Package.

Before beginning a segmentation session, it is important to understand just what segmentation is, the process of segmentation, and how to use the different segmentation tools.

#### Segmentation of Image Studies

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Understanding Segmentation of Image Studies\_EN.xml

Much of the terminology used in referring to the data of CT or MRI image scans comes from its long history. Originally, all scans were axial images acquired by a lengthy sequential scanning procedure. Modern technology enables quicker acquisition of this data and, for MRI, acquisition along dissimilar axes.

When a raw series or image study is imported into the Image Processing Package, all two-dimensional (2D) slices are rendered into a single three-dimensional (3D) volume. Segmentation is required for separating this volume into the *structure(s) of interest* (SOI). In most cases, the SOI is the area of most interest for real-time mapping, such as the left atrium or left ventricle.

The gray shades in the scan are representative of different densities or intensities. Imaging software helps users set the range (termed "window") of densities (from out of 4,096 possible values), to be displayed in different shades of gray, and adjust the center of that window (termed "level").

Color is applied to those areas of the 2D slices that match the desired threshold intensity; these colored areas are termed *Labels*. After the volume has been segmented into SOIs, labels are colored to match their corresponding segmented object, for easy identification in the 2D slices.

#### Overview of the Segmentation Process

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Overview of the Segmentation Process\_EN.xml

The segmentation process involves loading an image study, setting the optimal intensity value, and selecting the labeled *volume of interest* (VOI).

This VOI becomes the baseline for segmenting the rest of the image study. This is done by applying a special algorithm in the CARTOMERGE™ Image Processing Package. The algorithm takes all voxels (volume unit of the CT or MRI) within the same intensity threshold ***and*** adjacent to the selection, to provide a 3D volume based only on that data.

At this point, there is a single segmented volume. The surface volume may now be exported. However, when working with a data series, it is likely that multiple SOI segmenting will be required.

Segmentation to various structures is performed by applying *seeds* to all areas of the volume with which you want to work or remove from the final display. For best results, seeds are placed in the middle of each structure of interest. When you apply the Segment command, a second algorithm is implemented from each seed to their respective borders (that is, implementation of the algorithm ceases when the borders meet). The result is separate anatomical regions of interest, or SOIs.

This process of applying seeds and segmenting might have to be repeated until you are satisfied with the quality of the segmented 3D image study. If automatic segmentation is inadequate (due to poor study image quality, artifacts, etc.), you can manually edit the volume in 2D or 3D mode.

It is important to verify the results of your segmentation. Using the Slice Tools, scroll through the study and review the borders of each segmented object against the original 2D data slice.

In order to use the segmented volume during a CARTO™ 3 Study, the volume must be extracted to a surface image. This is accomplished by exporting it to the CARTO™ 3 System. After exporting, all volume data is removed, providing you with an anatomical shape that can be registered to the mapping space of the CARTO™ 3 System, via the CARTOMERGE™ Image Integration Module.

#### Verifying Segmentation Quality

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Verifying\_Segmentation\_Quality\_c3\_EN.xml

The responsibility for verifying segmentation quality rests with the physician. Verify quality throughout the process of segmentation. If necessary, all segmentation can be cancelled and restarted.

##### Tips for Verifying Segmentation Quality

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Tips for verifying segmentation quality\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  These tips do not replace clinical judgment. |

* Display only one SOI and use the Clipping Plane to open the slice frame. Scroll through the slices and compare the segmented SOI with the 2D slices.
* Compare the labels of the SOI with the original 2D slices by reducing the label opacity.
* Display adjacent SOIs and verify that borders are clearly defined.
* Display only one SOI and verify that there are no missing parts.

# CARTOSEG™ Segmentation Modules

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_intro\_v6\_EN.xml

* The **CARTOSEG™ CT Segmentation Module** is an advanced segmentation tool that generates segmentations of various anatomical structures from CT scans of the heart.[[22]](#footnote-22)
* The **CARTOSEG™ MR Segmentation Module** is an advanced segmentation tool that generates segmentations of various anatomical structures from MRI scans of the heart. The MRI processing capability of the module enables definition and segmentation of specific tissues based on their intensities.[[23]](#footnote-23)

With the CARTOSEG™ Segmentation Modules, you can import, render, segment, and edit CT or MRI scans for real-time use during CARTO™ 3 System studies with the CARTOMERGE™ Module. The CARTOSEG™ Segmentation Modules provide several semi-automatic segmentation options and multiple editing options.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Imported images represent the structure at the time of acquisition, and do not reflect changes since that time. |

When a raw series or image study is imported into one of the CARTOSEG™ Segmentation Modules, all two-dimensional (2D) slices are rendered into a single three-dimensional (3D) volume. Segmentation is required to separate this volume into the structure(s) of interest (SOI). In most cases, the SOI is the area of most interest for real-time mapping, such as the left atrium or left ventricle.

The gray shades in the scan represent different densities or intensities. The segmentation to SOIs is based on the selected intensities and a cardiac model.

Colors are applied to the various SOIs. These colored areas are called *labels*. The same labels apply to both the 3D and 2D views, for easy identification of the segmented SOIs.

## Importing Images to the CARTOSEG™ Segmentation Modules

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Importing\_CT\_and\_MRI\_Images\_cartoseg\_EN.xml

You must import the relevant images to the CARTO™ 3 System workstation before using the CARTOSEG™ Segmentation Modules. The import process is the same for all scan types.

When the import is completed, the CT Segmentation window appears for CT scans and the MR Segmentation & Registration window appears for MRI scans.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Network search is not available for scans that are more than 10 years old. |

## CARTOSEG™ CT Segmentation Module

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_ct\_seg\_v6\_EN.xml

This section provides an overview of the module. Refer to the *CARTOSEG™ CT Segmentation Module User Guide* for details.

The standard workflow for CT scans consists of the following stages:

* Loading scans for segmentation. Up to 5 CT scans can be loaded.
* Previewing the loaded scans.
* Applying semi-automatic and/or manual segmentation.
* Reviewing and editing the segmented images.
* Approve and save the resulting images to be used during a CARTO™ 3 Study.

### Loading Scans for Segmentation

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_ct\_loadingScans\_v6\_EN.xml

When the import of CT scans to the local workstation is completed, the CT Segmentation window appears.

![cartoSeg_manual_CT.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 40: Layout of CT Segmentation Window in Data Loading Stage

The patient details appear on the right side of the title bar.

* To select a different patient, click the Patient Browser button in the title bar.

![cartoseg_patientList_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...)

* To select a different scan, open the Select Scan list.

The list displays the loaded scans and the currently selected scan.

![cartoseg_scanList.png](data:image/png;base64...)

You can switch to the data loading stage and select a different scan from the list at any stage in the segmentation workflow.

### Previewing Loaded Scans

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_ct\_previewingScans\_v6\_EN.xml

The layout of the CT Segmentation window consists of a large 3D view on the left and two multiplanar reconstruction (MPR) views on the right: Axial (upper) and Sagittal (lower).

You can control the image in each pane as follows:

* To scroll through the slices in a 2D view, position the pointer in the pane and rotate the scroll wheel.
* To rotate a 2D or 3D image, press the scroll wheel and drag over the pane.
* To enlarge the 2D image, double-click in the 2D view. The image is placed in the main view pane.
* To zoom the 3D or 2D image, use the zoom bar in each pane.

The visualization toolbar on the left offers additional viewing options such as brightness and contrast, clipping plane view, and transfer function view.

Refer to the *CARTOSEG™ CT Segmentation Module User Guide* for details.

### Applying Segmentation

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_ct\_ApplyingSeg\_v6\_EN.xml

To continue to the segmentation stage, click the Segmentation button.

The module performs semi-automatic segmentation of the heart chambers based on the typical tissue features of the various anatomical structures. This segmentation process produces the four heart chambers and the aorta. Segmentation of the coronary sinus, coronary arteries, and esophagus can be done using the additional semi-automatic segmentation tools.

Alternatively, you can use manual segmentation to segment the anatomical structure. To perform manual segmentation, you must specify the range of intensities that define the particular structure.

You can apply various editing options to the segmented images.

#### Semi-Automatic Segmentation

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_ct\_semiAuto\_v6\_EN.xml

Semi-automatic segmentation creates segmented images of the following objects:

* Left Atrium (LA)
* Left Ventricle Myocardium (LV Myo)
* Left Ventricle Blood Pool (LV BP)
* Left Ventricle Papillary Muscles and Trabeculations (LV PMT)
* Right Atrium (RA)
* Right Ventricle (RV)
* Aorta

Each of these objects is assigned a different color for easy identification.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  If the selected data was segmented before, you can open the stored segmented images instead of repeating the segmentation process. |

The segmented images are displayed in 3D and 2D views. The segmented object list appears on the left side of the screen.

#### Additional Semi-Automatic Segmentation Options

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_ct\_semiAuto\_addl\_v6\_EN.xml

The module can perform segmentation of the coronary sinus, of the coronary artery tree, and of the esophagus. For coronary sinus and esophagus segmentation, you must first indicate its anatomical location on the scan or the object to be segmented.

To indicate the anatomical location of the structure, click in the 2D views along the structure path. Each click produces a small red marker circle. At least two markers are required to enable the segmentation.

#### Manual CT Segmentation

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_ct\_manSeg\_v6\_EN.xml

With the Manual Segmentation tool, you can segment a new object based on a selected range of intensities.

1. Click the Manual Segmentation button.
2. Click the area of interest in one of the 2D views. An initial global segmentation is created based on the intensities of the selected area.

When the process is completed, the segmented result is displayed in the 3D view with the clipping plane, and the intensity threshold bar appears. The new segmented object appears in the Segmented Object List.

1. Refine the range of intensities by setting lower and upper thresholds on the intensity bar.
2. Click the area of interest again to create the segmentation.

Adjacent structures in the selected intensity range are added as well.

If the resulting segmented object includes multiple anatomic structures of the heart, you can use the Divide/ Resegment tool to re-segment it into distinct objects.

Refer to the *CARTOSEG™ CT Segmentation Module User Guide* for details.

In addition, you can use the Manual Segmentation tool to add new objects as markers to be used during a CARTOMERGE™ Study:

1. Click the Manual Segmentation button.
2. Hold the **SHIFT** key and click the desired location.

A new object is created as marker at the clicked location and appears in both 2D and 3D views.

Repeat these steps to add more markers.

### Segmented Object List

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_ct\_segmentedObj\_v6\_EN.xml

The segmented object list consists of all the objects produced by semi-automatic segmentation or manual segmentation.

Each list entry has three elements: a check box to show or hide the object, the object's name, and the object's color. The names and colors of the default objects are pre-assigned, but you can change them.

### Editing Segmented Objects

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_ct\_editSeg\_v6\_EN.xml

This Segmentation Module provides a variety of editing tools for improving and refining segmentation results. Some tools operate only in the 2D views. Other tools operate in both 2D and 3D views.

Refer to the *CARTOSEG™ CT Segmentation Module User Guide* for details.

### Approve and Save Segmentation Results

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_ct\_approveSeg\_v6\_EN.xml

When the segmentation stage is completed and you have finished editing the segmented image, you need to approve and save the result for export to the CARTO™ 3 System. The export process converts the segmentation to a format that can be used during a CARTOMERGE™ Study.

## CARTOSEG™ MR Segmentation Module

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_seg\_v6\_EN.xml

This section provides an overview of the module. Refer to the *CARTOSEG™ MR Segmentation Module User Guide* for details.

The MR segmentation and registration workflow provides semi-automatic and manual tools for segmenting MRI series of the left ventricle, scar tissue, and other cardiac structures.

This module requires two distinct sets of MRI series:

* Anatomy: Used for semi-automatically segmenting the left ventricle short-axis 2D cine acquisitions. When slices in the long-axis orientations are available, they can be used to improve the LV segmentation result.
* Tissue Characterization (Delayed Enhanced MRI): Used for segmenting the LV 2D acquisitions into the different myocardial, scar, and border zone tissues. The TC short-axis slice orientation should match the anatomical short-axis slice orientation. Use of additional slices in the long-axis orientation is optional.

When a selected series is processed, LV segmentation is created. The segmentation can be edited, followed by registration of Anatomy and Tissue Characterization series. Semi-automatic and manual tools can be used to segment the LV myocardial, scar, and border zone tissues and other cardiac structures.

The standard workflow for an MRI series consists of the following stages:

* Loading the selected series for segmentation. Automatic LV segmentation starts when the series is loaded.
* Previewing the loaded series.
* If required, performing short-axis alignment for breathing motion correction.
* Performing Registration between Anatomy and Tissue Characterization series.
* If required, performing long-axis alignment.
* Copying the LV segmentation from the Anatomy series to the Tissue Characterization series, and editing the result if required.
* Performing segmentation of LV myocardial scar and border zone tissue using semi-automatic and manual tools.
* Optional: Performing manual segmentation of any additional structures.
* Reviewing the segmented images with the visualization tools.
* Approve and save the resulting images to be used during a CARTO™ 3 Study.

### Loading MRI Series for Segmentation

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_loadingScans\_v6\_EN.xml

When the process of importing MRI series is completed, the MR Segmentation & Registration window appears, in the Data Loading layout.

#### Data Loading Layout

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_loadingScans\_layout\_v6\_EN.xml

![cartoseg_mri_dataloading.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 41: Data Loading Layout

The default view of the MR Data Loading layout consists of 4 panes: Anatomy - SA (Short Axis), Anatomy - LA (Long Axis), Tissue Characterization - SA, and Tissue Characterization - LA. The four panes are empty when no series is loaded.

You can open a 6-pane layout by clicking ![changeLayout_icon_inline.png](data:image/png;base64...) on the visualization toolbar. Use the extra 2 panes to preview additional series. The series loaded into these panes do not affect the segmentation result.

The patient details appear on the right side of the title bar.

To select a different patient, click the Patient Browser button (![cartoseg_patientList_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...)) in the title bar.

All imported scan series are listed in the Select Series list on the right of the Data Loading button.

To perform segmentation of MRI series, select a short-axis Anatomy series and short-axis TC series. Long-axis Anatomy and long-axis TC series are optional but they improve LV segmentation accuracy when loaded.

LV segmentation starts as soon as the Anatomy short-axis cine series is loaded, and an alert appears at the right end of the main toolbar. When the initial segmentation is completed, the LV segmentation contours appear in the panes, and the alert disappears. While LV segmentation is in progress, the segmentation stage is disabled.

At any stage in the segmentation workflow, you can go back to the data loading stage and select a different series from the list. If you choose to load a different series after segmentation has already started, a warning appears that if you continue the current segmentation results will be lost.

![cartoseg_mr_dataLoading_db.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 42: Series Replacement Warning Message

|  |  |
