| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  When you load a series that has already been segmented and exported, you are asked whether to reload the existing segmentation or to perform a new one. When the new segmentation is exported, it overwrites the stored one. |

### Previewing the Loaded Data

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_previewingScans\_v6\_ifu\_EN.xml

In the Data Loading window you can preview the loaded series. Use the mouse and the various visualization tools to adjust the view.

Using the mouse:

* To scroll through the slices in a 2D pane, rotate the mouse wheel in the pane.
* To rotate a 3D view, press the mouse wheel and drag the image.
* To maximize a pane, double-click in the pane.
* To zoom the 3D or 2D image, use the zoom bar in each pane.

### Slice Alignment—Breathing Motion Correction

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_SliceAlignment\_v6\_EN.xml

During the acquisition of MRI scans, slice misalignment may occur due to patient breath holding. You can use slice translation to correct such misalignments.

* To hide or show the segmentation contours, click ![cartoseg_hideShow_contours_inline.png](data:image/png;base64...) on the main toolbar.
* To undo all the alignment operations, click ![cartoseg_undoAlignment_icon_inline.png](data:image/png;base64...) on the main toolbar.

#### Short Axis Slice Alignment

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_shortAlign\_v6\_EN.xml

To start short-axis alignment, click S. Axis Align on the main toolbar. The default layout consists of three panes. The main pane on the left displays two consecutive slices in a blended manner with LV contours overlay. A solid line marks the displayed slice and a broken line marks the slice below. The two panes on the right present two additional views for orientation: coronal view in the upper pane, and sagittal view in the lower pane. You can perform slice alignment on either Anatomy or TC series.

![cartoseg_shortAxis_alignment.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 43: Slice Contours in S. Axis Align Layout

#### Long Axis Slice Alignment

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_longAlign\_v6\_EN.xml

Long-axis slice alignment assesses LV segmentation on the selected long-axis series. It is performed for the purpose of orientation and does not affect the LV segmentation.

To start long-axis alignment, click L. Axis Align on the main toolbar. The default layout consists of three panes. The main pane on the left displays the short-axis slices of the selected series. The lines across the image represent the loaded long-axis slices.

The two panes on the right present two additional views:

* Upper pane: long-axis slice with LV segmentation contours
* Lower pane: long-axis slice with LV segmentation contours and the short-axis slice location

The green lines in the lower right pane mark the short-axis slices and the orange line marks the slice that is displayed in the left pane.

### Registration

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_registration\_v6\_ifu\_EN.xml

Registration is done between the Anatomy series and the Tissue Characterization series. Click the Registration button on the main toolbar to display the initial automatic registration.

The Registration layout consists of 4 panes, with the registered results on the right:

* Upper left: Anatomy series
* Lower left: Tissue Characterization series.
* Upper right: short-axis registered series
* Lower right: registered sagittal view.

The registered series are displayed in a blended manner. To review the result of the initial automatic registration, scroll through the slices. To adjust the blending factor of the two registered series, hold down CTRL while rotating the mouse wheel.

In Registration layout, two registration tools appear on the main toolbar: Translation and Landmark Registration. Use these tools to refine the registration result.

### Segmentation

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_segmentation\_v6\_EN.xml

The CARTOSEG™ MR Segmentation Module provides an initial semi-automatic segmentation of the left ventricle using the short-axis anatomy cine series, which creates the epicardium and endocardium contours. The Segmented Object List displays the epicardium in yellow and the endocardium in green by default.

Segmentation contours are displayed during the workflow stages of slice alignment, registration, and segmentation.

In the segmentation stage, you can do the following:

* Review and edit the LV segmentation result
* Project the LV segmentation onto the Tissue Characterization (TC) series and edit the result
* Perform segmentation of LV myocardial tissue, scar and border zone, using semi-automatic and manual tools
* Perform manual segmentation of selected structures

To start segmentation, click the Segmentation button on the main toolbar. The main toolbar features the segmentation tools and editing tools.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![cartoseg_segmentationTools.png](data:image/png;base64...) | ![cartoseg_editingTools.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| Segmentation Tools | Editing Tools |

#### LV Myocardial Tissue Segmentation: Scar and Border Zones

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_LV\_tissue\_segmentation\_v6\_EN.xml

In the segmentation layout, the LV segmentation is presented on the anatomy series (see ["Initial LV Segmentation Contours Presented on Anatomy Series"](#target9000046)). Review the segmentation result and edit the segmentation with the editing tools if required.

The LV myocardial tissue segmentation is performed using the Tissue Characterization series. Use the Transfer LV Segmentation button to transfer the LV segmentation contours onto the Tissue Characterization series.

If the results of previous scar tissue segmentation are available, you are asked if you want to overwrite them. If you click No, the previous available segmentation is loaded. Click Yes to start a new segmentation session.

The standard segmentation workflow consists of the following steps:

* Reviewing and, if required, editing the initial LV segmentation result
* Transferring the LV segmentation contours onto the Tissue Characterization series using the Transfer LV Segmentation button
* Reviewing and, if required, editing the Tissue Characterization LV segmentation result
* Sampling the healthy myocardium or high signal intensity area between the epicardium and endocardium contours
* Adjusting the sliders of the intensity histogram
* Editing the myocardial tissue segmentation result if required
* Exporting the segmentation result to the CARTO™ 3 System

![cartoseg_lvSeg_contours_anat.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 44: Initial LV Segmentation Contours Presented on Anatomy Series

![cartoseg_lvSeg_contours_TC.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 45: LV Segmentation and Zones (Scar and Border Zone) Presented on Tissue Characterization Series

In the figure above, Zone 1 (red) represents the core scar tissue and Zone 2 (orange) represents the border tissue between the core scar and the myocardium according to the Intensity Zone Segmentation settings of the histogram (see ["LV Segmentation and Zones (Scar and Border Zone) Presented on Tissue Characterization Series"](#target9000047)).

#### Intensity Zone Segmentation

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_intensity\_segmentation\_v6\_EN.xml

**Intensity Zone Segmentation** identifies scar and border zones in the LV myocardium, between the epicardium and endocardium contours.

The segmentation is based on user input, sampling either healthy myocardium or high signal intensity (scar) tissue, and applies the Standard Deviation (SD) algorithm and the High Signal Intensity algorithm. The segmentation result is displayed in histogram format.

##### Intensity Zone Segmentation Based on Healthy Myocardium Sampling

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_intensity\_segmentation\_healthy\_v6\_EN.xml

1. Click the Intensity Zone Segmentation button.
2. Click in an area of healthy myocardium, between epicardium and endocardium contours.

The calculated histogram according to the sampled area, present in SD scale the two segmented objects: Zone 1 (scar, default color red) and Zone 2 (border zone, default color orange).

Two sliders divide the histogram into three sections: the red section represents the scar core (Zone 1), the orange section represents the scar border zone (Zone 2), and the uncolored section represents unscarred, healthy myocardium.

The default scale for the horizontal axis of the histogram is Standard Deviation (SD), but two additional scales are available: Intensity (IN) and intensity percentage (%).To switch scales, click the arrow button to the right of the scale indicator.

1. To edit the histogram result, you can move the sliders to increase or reduce the range of intensities in each section. Each change is immediately visible in all the panes.

##### Intensity Zone Segmentation Based on High Signal Intensity (SI) Sampling

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_intensity\_segmentation\_highIntens\_v6\_EN.xml

You can perform myocardial tissue segmentation based on high signal intensity (SI) to identify scar and border zones in the LV myocardium.

Click in an area of high SI between epicardium and endocardium contours.

* The calculated histogram according to the sampled area present the 35% and 50% points of the high SI on the intensity scale histogram by two blue lines.
* Adjust the sliders to meet the marked 35% and 50% points and review the updated zone segmentation results.
* To edit the histogram result, move the sliders to increase or reduce the range of intensities in each section.

#### Manual MRI Segmentation

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_manualSegmentation\_v6\_EN.xml

With the Manual Segmentation tool, you can segment a new object based on a selected range of intensities.

1. Click the Manual Segmentation button.
2. Click the area of interest in one of the 2D views. An initial global segmentation is created based on the intensities of the selected area.

When the process is completed, the segmented result is displayed in the 3D view with the clipping plane, and the intensity threshold bar appears. The new segmented object appears in the Segmented Object List.

1. Refine the range of intensities by setting lower and upper thresholds on the intensity bar.
2. Click the area of interest again to create the segmentation.

Adjacent structures in the selected intensity range are added as well.

Refer to the *CARTOSEG™ MR Segmentation Module User Guide* for details.

In addition, you can use the Manual Segmentation tool to add new objects as markers to be used during a CARTOMERGE™ Plus Study:

1. Click the Manual Segmentation button.
2. Hold the **SHIFT** key and click the desired location.

A new object is created as marker at the clicked location and appears in both 2D and 3D views.

Repeat these steps to add more markers.

| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Selected intensities may represent different tissues or areas. Apply clinical knowledge of the anatomy to distinguish between them. |

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_approveSeg\_v6\_EN.xml

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_approveSeg\_3D\_v6\_EN.xml

## Advanced MR Segmentation for Expert Users

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_seg\_advanced\_IFU\_v6\_EN.xml

The CARTOSEG™ MR Segmentation Module enables segmentation of MRI scans using any short-axis stack series, typically MRI delayed enhancement series or similar. You can manually segment the heart chamber epicardium and endocardium as well as myocardial scar and fibrosis.

To perform segmentation using a short-axis stack series, follow these steps:

1. Load the selected short-axis MRI series into both the anatomy and tissue characterization panes.

Loading the long-axis series is optional.

1. Correct slice alignment if required.
2. Review registration result and apply translation or landmarks to edit if required.
3. Perform segmentation:
   * Add epicardium and endocardium contours to the anatomy series.
   * Edit the contours using the slice or ball editing tool. Use copy contours to the slice above/below to minimize the required edits.
   * Transfer created contours to the tissue characterization series.
   * Sample the healthy myocardium or the high signal intensity area between the epicardium and endocardium contours to segment the tissues of interest in the myocardium.
   * Adjust the intensity histogram sliders as required.
   * Edit the segmentation result with the dedicated editing tools.
   * Export the segmentation result to the CARTO™ 3 System.

### Loading MR series for Segmentation (Advanced)

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_AdvMri\_load\_v6\_EN.xml

When the process of importing MRI scan is completed, the MR Segmentation & Registration window appears, in the Data Loading layout.

All imported MRI scan series are listed in the Select Series list on the right of the Data Loading button.

1. Click the SA - Anatomy pane to select it for loading.

The selected pane is highlighted.

1. Open the Select Series list and select the series for loading.

The selected series is highlighted in orange.

1. Click Load.

**Note:** You may be alerted that the selected series is not compatible with semi-automatic LV segmentation. Click OK to disregard the alert.

The selected series is loaded into the selected pane.

1. Repeat the above steps to load the same series into the SA - Tissue Characterization pane.
2. If required, repeat the same steps to load a Long Axis series into the LA - Anatomy or Tissue Characterization pane.

### Previewing the Loaded Data (Advanced)

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_advMri\_previewingScans\_v6\_EN.xml

In the Data Loading window you can preview the loaded series. Use the mouse and the various visualization tools to adjust the view.

See also:

* ["Previewing the Loaded Data"](#target9000048)
* *CARTOSEG™ MR Segmentation Module User Guide*

### Slice Alignment—Breathing Motion Correction (Advanced)

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_SliceAlignment\_adv\_v6\_EN.xml

During the acquisition of MRI scans, slice misalignment may occur due to patient breath holding.

Follow the standard slice alignment workflow when working with a short-axis stack series.

See also:

* ["Slice Alignment—Breathing Motion Correction"](#target9000049)

### Registration (Advanced)

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_registration\_adv\_v6\_EN.xml

Registration is done between the Anatomy series and the Tissue Characterization series. Click the Registration button on the main toolbar to display the initial automatic registration.

The registered series are displayed in a blended manner. To review the result of the initial automatic registration, scroll through the slices. To adjust the blending factor of the two registered series, hold down CTRL while rotating the mouse wheel.

In Registration layout, two registration tools appear on the main toolbar: Translation and Landmark Registration. Use these tools to refine the registration result.

See also:

* ["Registration"](#target9000050)

### Segmentation (Advanced Mode)

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_segmentation\_adv\_v6\_EN.xml

With the CARTOSEG™ MR Segmentation Module, advanced users can add and edit segmentation contours, and perform segmentation of the epicardium and endocardium for a selected chamber. The Segmented Object List displays the epicardium in yellow and the endocardium in green by default.

In the segmentation stage, you can do the following:

* Add generic contours for segmentation
* Edit contours (both epicardium and endocardium) to fit the selected chamber.
* Perform segmentation of myocardial tissue, using semi-automatic and manual tools
* Perform manual segmentation of selected structures

To start segmentation, click the Segmentation button on the main toolbar. The main toolbar features the segmentation tools and editing tools.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![cartoseg_segmentationTools.png](data:image/png;base64...) | ![cartoseg_editingTools.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| Segmentation Tools | Editing Tools |

#### Adding Contours to Slice

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_addContours\_adv\_v6\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Click ![cartoseg_addContours_icon_inline.png](data:image/png;base64...) to activate the add contours tool. |
| 2. | Scroll to the slice of interest and click to add the contours.  Epicardium and Endocardium contours of fixed default sizes appear around the clicked point. |

#### Editing Contours

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_editingContours\_adv\_v6\_EN.xml

You can use the slice and ball editing tools to adjust the added default-size contours.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | From the Segmented Object list, select the epicardium or endocardium object.  The color of the selected object changes to gray. |
| 2. | Click the slice or ball buttons to activate the editing tool.  ![slice_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...)![ballEdit_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...)  In the 2D view, the pointer changes to a yellow or orange circle. |
| 3. | To expand the contour, drag from within the contour out. To reduce the contour, drag from outside the contour. |
| 4. | To control the size of the editing tool circle, hold down the **SHIFT** key while rotating the scroll wheel. |

You can also use the contour copying tools to copy the contours of a slice to the adjacent slice above or below. Contours copied to the adjacent slice usually require only minor editing.

After completing the segmentation of the chamber, proceed to intensity zone segmentation.

#### Intensity Zone Segmentation (Advanced)

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSeg\_mri\_intensity\_segmentation\_adv\_v6\_EN.xml

**Intensity Zone Segmentation** identifies scar and border zones in the LV myocardium, between the epicardium and endocardium contours.

The figure below shows the epicardium contours (yellow), endocardium contours (green), and segmented myocardium zones in 2D and 3D views.

![cartoseg_segViews_LV-LA.png](data:image/png;base64...)

When the segmentation stage is completed and you have finished editing the segmented image, you need to approve and save the result for export to the CARTO™ 3 System. The export process converts the segmentation to a format that can be used during a CARTOMERGE™ Study.

# CARTOREPLAY™ Module

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_visualization\_title\_EN.xml

## CARTOREPLAY™ Module Overview

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_visualization\_intro\_v4-3\_EN.xml

The **CARTOREPLAY™ Module** records all ECG data and electrode location data (magnetic or ACL) for all connected catheters throughout the procedure. The recorded data can be accessed at any time during the study to view earlier ECG signals and catheter positions, and to retroactively acquire additional points for EA mapping.[[24]](#footnote-24)

It is also possible to retroactively capture signals (IS for PASO™ Module or body surface patterns for CONFIDENSE™ Module). Intracardiac patterns cannot be captured retroactively.

The CARTOREPLAY™ Module includes dual monitor support, which can be used to extend the display across two monitors for more convenient viewing.

The CARTOREPLAY™ Module supports all the other CARTO™ 3 System modules.

A Biosense Webster representative is responsible for the installation and initial configuration of the module.

### Timeline Mode

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_visualization\_replayMode\_v7\_EN.xml

During a study performed with the CARTOREPLAY™ Module, two display modes are available.

* **Real-time mode**: Viewers display current activity and data
* **Timeline mode**: The Monitor pane displays ECG signals and system indications from a selected time in the study. The map viewers display catheter positions from that selected time.

Timeline mode can be used for the following workflows:

* **Extended Beat Buffer**: You just missed acquiring a point. Scroll back in the Monitor pane to acquire the point.
* **Exploratory review**: Use Timeline mode to review and explore an earlier time in the study. If necessary, acquire additional points.
* **Review of selected events**: Events such as point acquisition, ablation sessions, and pacing are displayed in the event panel. You can scroll through events of the same type.

In the context of this module, an event may be instantaneous (acquisition of EA points or ultrasound contours) or of longer duration (ablation, pacing, respiration status).

Timeline mode is not available during the following processes:

* Ablation: If you start ablation in Timeline mode the system automatically reverts to Real-time mode.
* Continuous acquisition: You must end FAM acquisition or continuous EA acquisition before you can activate Timeline mode.
* When a propagation map is displayed in Timeline mode, the option to display a propagation map is disabled.

Real-time acquisition is not available in Timeline mode. Exit the mode to acquire points or FAM data.

If you hide the Monitor Pane, the system automatically reverts to Real-time mode.

See also:

* ["Retroactive Point Acquisition"](#target9000051)

### Activating Timeline Mode

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_visualization\_activate\_v7\_EN.xml

When the CARTOREPLAY™ Module is installed, the Timeline button is added to the Monitor Pane toolbar.

![timeline_button_in_monitor_4-3.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 48: Monitor Pane Toolbar

To activate Timeline mode, click the Timeline button. The button turns orange, indicating that the mode is active.

![timeline_button_in_monitor_orange_4-3.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 49: Monitor Pane Toolbar when Timeline Mode Is Active

To return to Real-time mode, click the Timeline button again.

### Exporting CARTOREPLAY™ Module Data

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_visualization\_export\_v7\_EN.xml

You can activate Timeline mode when a study is continued from Review mode.

By default, this option is only available within the first 18 hours after starting the study, because the data recorded by the CARTOREPLAY™ Module is not saved with the study. To ensure that CARTOREPLAY™ data is exported by the Study Archiver, select the Include CARTOREPLAY™ Module data option when saving the study. This option is available only when saving to USB, because of the large file size. Exporting can take longer than with standard export.

## Timeline Mode Interface

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_visualization\_ReplayInterface\_v7\_EN.xml

In Timeline mode, acquisition in the Main Map Viewer is disabled. A separate Timeline acquisition toolbar appears in the Monitor Pane. The Timeline Toolbar includes a button for capturing induced signals/body surface patterns.

![replay_toolbar_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 50: Timeline Toolbar

The Event Panel appears in the lower part of the Monitor Pane. The system clock time is displayed in the Monitor Pane in HH:MM:SS format.

![continuous_vis_fullScreen_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 51: Timeline Mode Display

The signals in the Monitor Pane are frozen at the Reviewed Time. The Reviewed Time is marked by a vertical line called the Review Line. The Review Line is always positioned in the center of the Monitor Pane. The Review Line does not move when the Reviewed Time is changed. Instead, the time segment displayed in the Monitor Pane and Event Panel is adjusted to keep the Review Line in the center of the display.

![continuous_reviewLine.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 52: Review Line

A scroll bar for time navigation appears under the Event Panel. Use the scroll bar to change the time of the Review Line. Scroll left to position the Review Line at an earlier point of the study, or scroll right to position the Review Line at a later point.

The Event toolbar appears at the bottom of the Event Panel. Click the event buttons to hide or show events of a specific type.

![continuous_visualization_events.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 53: Event Panel

Visualization of catheters at the Reviewed Time is added to the map viewers. The Timeline Catheter visualization is the same as real-time visualization except for catheter color. The Timeline visualization of a force catheter includes the force vector.

![continuous_acqu_cathVis_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 54: Timeline and Real-time Catheter Visualizations

In Timeline mode, the Show Catheter option is disabled. If the option was active when entering Timeline mode, it is automatically restored on the return to Real-time mode.

### Selecting the Reviewed Time

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_visualization\_selectReplayTime\_v4-3\_EN.xml

There are two options for entering Timeline mode:

* Current time: The Reviewed Time is the time you enter Timeline mode
* Last viewed time: The Reviewed Time is the time last displayed in Timeline mode

These options are set in the drop-down menu of the Timeline button.

If you change the Reviewed Time setting while in Timeline mode, the change does not affect the current display. It will be applied the next time you enter Timeline mode.

When you activate Timeline mode for the first time in the study, only the Current time setting is available. For subsequent entries into Timeline mode, you can choose the setting you prefer.

### Displaying Timeline Catheters

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_visualization\_showCathVix\_v4-3\_EN.xml

You can show or hide Timeline catheter visualizations in the map viewers. The Show/hide Replay Catheters option in the map viewer right-click menu lists all catheters that were connected at any time during the study. By default, all the listed catheters are selected. Clear the selection to hide a catheter.

You can hide any catheter at the Reviewed Time except for the mapping and ablation catheters. If a Timeline catheter is hidden and you select a replay time when it was the mapping catheter or ablation catheter, the catheter is displayed. When the Reviewed Time changes so the catheter is no longer the mapping or ablation catheter, the catheter is hidden and its checkbox on the list is cleared.

Icons in the Timeline Catheters list indicate the show/hide settings.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![replay_MapCath_visStatus_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Mapping Catheter |
| ![replay_ablCath_visStatus.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Ablation Catheter |
| ![replay_hiddenCath_visStatus.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Indicates a Timeline catheter that is currently visible because it is the mapping or ablation catheter at the Reviewed Time. |

### Event Panel

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_visualization\_events\_v4-3\_EN.xml

The Event Panel displays the following event types:

| Icon | Description | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ![replay_event_resp.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Respiration |  |
| ![replay_event_EApt.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Focal EA points |  |
| ![replay_event_MEMpt.png](data:image/png;base64...) | MEM points |  |
| ![replay_event_uls.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Ultrasound contours | Available only with the CARTOSOUND™ Module |
| ![replay_event_abl.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Application of RF energy |  |
| ![replay_event_visiTag_focal.png](data:image/png;base64...) | VISITAG™ Site | Available only with the CARTO VISITAG™ Module |
| ![replay_event_pacing.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Pacing |  |
| ![replay_event_paso.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Reference IS capture | Available only when the PASO™ Module is active |

For instantaneous events (acquiring points or ultrasound contours, capturing an IS), the event icon marks an instance of the event. For ongoing events (pacing, ablation, VISITAG™ Sites) the width of the icon indicates the duration of the event.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  The icons of points, contours, RF sessions, and VISITAG™ Sites that are not on the active map are displayed in gray. |

By default, the Event Panel shows all the events that took place during the displayed time segment. To hide or show a type of event, click the event button on the event toolbar at the bottom of the event panel. When an event type is hidden, its toolbar button is displayed in gray.

If you double-click an event in any viewer (Point List, Main Map Viewer, etc.), the event is selected in the Event Panel. The selected event is highlighted and positioned at the Review Line. Only one event can be selected at any time. When a VISITAG™ Site or ablation event is selected, its start time is at the Review Line. When a pacing event is selected, its end time is at the Review Line.

When an event with several instances is selected, arrows are displayed to the left and right of the Review Line. Click an arrow to jump to the previous or next instance of the event. You can also jump by pressing the keyboard left and right arrows. For the first and last instances of the event, the corresponding arrow is disabled.

If you double-click an event in the event panel, it is selected in the appropriate viewers. For example, double-clicking a point in the Event Panel automatically selects it in the map viewers and on the Point List. Double-clicking a VISITAG™ Site event in the Event Panel automatically selects the VISITAG™ Site in the Main Map Viewer and displays its data.

If you change the VISITAG™ Preferences, the system recalculates the VISITAG™ Sites and updates the Event Panel.

![replay_eventPanel_full_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 55: Event Panel with Selected Point and Various Events

### Using Calipers

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_visualization\_calipers\_v4-3\_EN.xml

* To add a time caliper when in Timeline mode, press ALT and drag horizontally in the Monitor pane.
* To add a voltage caliper when in Timeline mode, press CTRL + ALT and click a signal.

You can add multiple instances of each caliper.

Moving to a different segment of the study (by scrolling or by event jumping) may push time calipers out of view but they stay in place. Moving back to the same segment of the study brings the time calipers back into view.

Calipers are deleted when you exit Timeline mode.

### Retroactive Point Acquisition

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_visualization\_acquisition\_v7\_EN.xml

In Timeline mode, you can acquire EA points at the Reviewed Time, either manually or using continuous acquisition.

When Timeline mode is active, the acquisition panel in the Main Map Viewer is disabled. The Timeline acquisition toolbar appears in the Monitor pane, displaying recorded point data from the mapping catheter at the Reviewed Time. The point data includes cycle length (CL), local activation time (LAT), voltage (Bi), impedance (Imp), and force (g).

![replay_toolbar_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 56: Timeline Toolbar

The annotation of the retroactively acquired point is the closest annotation prior to the Reviewed Time. The retroactively acquired points are added to the active map and to the Point List. The acquisition time of a retroactively acquired point is the time of its reference annotation. To display the points in chronological order, sort the Point List by time.

To acquire points retroactively, click the manual acquisition button on the Timeline toolbar. Points are acquired with the mapping catheter that was active at the Reviewed Time. You cannot change the mapping catheter retroactively.

![replay_manual_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Points cannot be acquired retroactively in the following situations:

* There was an error message affecting acquisition at the Reviewed Time.
* The reference annotation in the Active Map does not match the annotation at the Reviewed Time. To enable retroactive point acquisition, select an Active Map with the same annotation.

If points cannot be acquired, the Timeline acquisition button is disabled. A tool tip on the button describes the error.

## Dual Monitor

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/dualMonitor\_Intro\_v4-3\_EN.xml

The **Dual Monitor** feature supports display of the CARTO™ 3 System Application on two monitors in the observation room, with two repeater monitors in the operating room.

You can use the dual monitor option to extend the display across two monitors for more convenient viewing.

Table 35: Dual Monitor Configuration Options

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| 2 + 2 | Two monitors in observation room, two monitors in operating room |
| 2 + 1 | Two monitors in observation room, one monitor in operating room |

The basic configuration (one monitor in each room) is still available.

When two monitors are in use, two display options are available:

* Clone Mode: The display on both monitors is the same.
* Extended Mode: One monitor is defined as the Primary monitor, and the other is the Secondary monitor. The CARTO™ 3 System display extends across both monitors.

![DualMonitor_extendedMode.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 57: Dual Monitor Extended Display Mode

In Extended Mode, some items can be displayed in either monitor, some are always displayed in both monitors, and some are only displayed in the primary monitor.

Table 36: Extended Mode Display Locations

| Monitor | Item |
| --- | --- |
| Primary | * Opening Screen buttons * Initial Setup Screens (except for Location Setup) * External utilities such as the Export Data Tool, Image Processing Package, and the Catheter Definition Tool |
| Both | REP4611 D-V3-1.20.1.2   * Error and alert messages * Warning popup messages * Location Setup screen * Stimulation routing indication |
| Either | * Fixed windows such as the Map Viewers, ECG Viewers, and Point List   At least one fixed window must be displayed in the secondary monitor.   * Floating windows such as the Preferences dialog box or Transparency dialog box |

VSTS 24628

### Dual Monitor Layouts

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/DualMonitor\_layouts\_4-3\_EN.xml

The Dual Monitor configuration includes two predefined layouts.

Table 37: Predefined Layouts for Dual Monitor Configuration

| Icon | Primary Monitor | Secondary Monitor |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ![layouts_dualMonitor_icon_1_selected.png](data:image/png;base64...) | * Monitor pane * Point List * Annotation Viewer * Selected Point Viewer | * Main Map Viewer * Additional Map Viewer |
| ![layouts_dualMonitor_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...) | * Additional Map Viewer * Main Map Viewer | * Selected Point Viewer * Annotation Viewer * Point List * Monitor pane |

If you open a Study or template that was saved in Extended Mode while the System is in Clone Mode, the Dual Maps predefined layout is used on both monitors.

If you open a Study or template that was saved in Clone Mode while the System is in Extended Mode, the secondary monitor appears empty.

### Changing the Display Mode and Primary Monitor

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/DualMonitor\_changeConfiguration\_4-3\_EN.xml

You can change the display mode or the primary monitor before you begin a Study without opening the Monitor Setup dialog box.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteCaution.png](data:image/png;base64...) | *CAUTION*  Changing the display mode or the primary monitor during a study will cause the CARTO™ 3 System Application to reload. |

# CARTOSOUND™ Image Integration Module

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSound\_title\_v7\_EN.xml

## CARTOSOUND™ Module Overview

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartoSound\_intro\_v7\_EN.xml

The **CARTOSOUND™ Module** combines real-time ultrasound image data with CARTO™ 3 point-by-point maps. Ultrasound images are acquired with a supported catheter from the SOUNDSTAR™ Ultrasound Catheter family (referred to as the SOUNDSTAR™ Catheter). These catheters include an ultrasound transducer and a Biosense Webster location sensor. The ultrasound transducer emits and receives multiple rays of ultrasound. The real-time ultrasound images are displayed in the CARTO™ 3 System, as well as on a compatible ultrasound system.[[25]](#footnote-25)

Once the streaming ultrasound is stopped, a 3-second video clip is displayed. You can then draw contours of the required anatomy on any frame of the ultrasound clip and add this information to a 3D reconstruction. The 3-second ultrasound clip is saved with these contours, and the frames containing the contours are marked for easy identification. Contour data contributes anatomical information to the map. Contours are listed in the Point List.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Information and instructions provided about the CARTOSOUND™ Module assume that users are experienced in both intracardiac echocardiography (ICE) and the CARTO™ 3 System. |

### Working with the CARTOSOUND™ Module

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/CARTOSOUND\_using\_V8p2\_EN.xml

The SOUNDSTAR™ Catheter is connected to the ULTRASOUND catheter socket on the PIU.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  The SOUNDSTAR™ Catheter is used for diagnostic purposes only and has no therapeutic capabilities. |

A SOUNDSTAR™ Catheter is displayed in the Main Map Viewer or the Additional Map Viewer with an outline representing the ultrasound fan. The catheter's tip position can be displayed in either Stable or Gated visualization mode.

* Stable -To present and acquire a stable location during the cardiac cycle, location is calculated as the average of the real-time locations detected during the previous second.
* Gated - To present and acquire a specific timing during the cardiac cycle, the location is taken at the annotation time.

Electroanatomical (EA) points can be acquired in either Stable or Gated mode. If you want to combine EA points and contours, it is recommended to draw the contours before acquiring EA points.

The catheter's 3D image can be displayed in either Real Time or Gated mode.

![cartosound_cath_mapViewer.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 58: Ultrasound Catheter Tip Position (1) and 3D Image (2)

CARTOSOUND™ Maps can be combined with FAM maps, using the Anatomical Merge feature.

It is recommended to use the CARTOSOUND™ Module with the Respiration Gating feature[[26]](#footnote-26).

VSTS 36376: NTF

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  If an error message in the Ultrasound window conflicts with a CARTO™ 3 System error message, refer to the CARTO™ 3 System error message. |

### About the SOUNDSTAR™ Ultrasound Catheter Family

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/About the SoundStar 3D Catheter\_EN.xml

When working with the CARTOSOUND™ Module, you must use a supported catheter from the SOUNDSTAR™ Ultrasound Catheter family (referred to as the SOUNDSTAR™ Catheter). These catheters incorporate a pre-calibrated Biosense Webster location sensor, which enables the same location detection of a SOUNDSTAR™ Catheter as for a focal ablation mapping catheter, with no need for additional registration to the streaming ultrasound.

There are several versions of the SOUNDSTAR™ Catheter[[27]](#footnote-27), compatible with GE and Siemens ultrasound systems:

* SOUNDSTAR™ 3D 10 F Diagnostic Ultrasound Catheter
* SOUNDSTAR™ eco 10 F Diagnostic Ultrasound Catheter
* SOUNDSTAR™ eco 8 F Diagnostic Ultrasound Catheter
* SOUNDSTAR CRYSTAL™ 9 F Ultrasound Catheter

The ultrasound catheter is connected to the ULTRASOUND Catheter socket on the Patient Interface Unit (PIU). You can choose to work with only an ultrasound catheter, or with both an ultrasound catheter and a focal ablation catheter connected to the PIU.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  SOUNDSTAR™ Catheters are used for diagnostic purposes only and have no therapeutic capabilities. |

### Compatible Ultrasound Systems

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/CSound\_compatible\_sys\_v8\_EN.xml

The CARTOSOUND™ Module displays ultrasound images acquired from a compatible ultrasound system. Each ultrasound system software version is compatible with the current version of CARTO™ 3 System and may not be compatible with previous versions.

After installing the CARTOSOUND™ Module, the following ultrasound systems can be connected to the current version of the CARTO™ 3 System:[[28]](#footnote-28)

* Siemens ACUSON SC2000™ PRIME Ultrasound System
* Siemens ACUSON P500™ Ultrasound System
* Siemens ACUSON Origin™ Ultrasound System
* GE HealthCare Vivid™ *iq* and Vivid™ S70/S60/S70N/S60N ultrasound systems (all referred to as "Vivid™")

## Connections between the CARTO™ 3 System and the Ultrasound System

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartosound\_uls\_connections\_v7-5\_EN.xml

A Biosense Webster representative is responsible for the installation and initial configuration of the module.

For compatibility reasons, any ultrasound system that is connected to a CARTO™ 3 System must be set up by a Biosense Webster Field Representative. Each ultrasound system at the site is assigned a unique identifying name, to be selected in the Study Setup screen at the beginning of an ultrasound study.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteDanger.png](data:image/png;base64...) | **WARNING**  1.15.2.1 V3  Each ultrasound system at the site is set up with its own specific operating parameters at installation (even if the systems are of the same model) and labeled accordingly. Failure to select the correct ultrasound system in the Study Setup screen could result in erroneous display of contours in the reconstruction. |

### CARTO™ 3 System Connection to Ultrasound Systems

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/CSound\_SystemSetup\_c3\_v8\_EN.xml

V7.4:REP13691 D1.2, V8:REP14517 D-38

The CARTO™ 3 System Workstation is connected to the ultrasound system via an Ethernet cable for video over LAN and data communication. The CARTO™ 3 System Workstation and the ultrasound system exchange data using socket communication in TCP/IP data streaming mode. The ultrasound system acts as a server and CARTO™ 3 System connects to the ultrasound system as a client through a dedicated peer-to-peer network connection. The IP address and port number of the ultrasound server are pre-configured on the CARTO™ 3 System.

REP14517 D-35

The CARTO™ 3 System optimally preserves the image quality from the ultrasound system with a target frame rate of 30 frames per second.

V7.4:REP13691 D1.4, V8:REP14517 D-24, D-26, D-28, D-29, D-32, D-34

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  If images from the Ultrasound System are not displayed on the CARTO™ 3 System, check the connectivity between the Ultrasound System and CARTO™ 3 System. |

For the Vivid S70N ultrasound system, the CartoSound workflow must be turned ON (on the touch panel, select More > Utility > CartoSound). To set the dataflow, refer to the Preset and System Setup chapter of the *Vivid User Guide*.

The CARTO™ 3 System Workstation can also be connected to the ultrasound system via a video cable for transmission of video signals and via an Ethernet cable for data communication. For GE Vivid™ systems and Siemens ACUSON P500™ systems, a video splitter is provided. The video splitter must be used to ensure safe use of the system.

Connections for video signal between Siemens ACUSON P500™ systems and the CARTO™ 3 System must be made only through the Isolated Video Splitter supplied by either Siemens or Biosense Webster.  Use of any other splitter might cause degraded quality of the ultrasound images displayed by the CARTO™ 3 System. |

### Connecting an Ultrasound Catheter to the PIU

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/CatheterConnections\_toPIU\_c3\_EN.xml

Before performing an ultrasound study, verify the necessary catheter connections:

* The ultrasound catheter must be connected as follows:
  1. The 33-pin end connects to the ULTRASOUND socket of the PIU via an extension cable.
  2. The SwiftLink™ catheter connector end connects to the ultrasound system.

![soundstar_eco_10f_callouts.png](data:image/png;base64...)

* Optionally, a therapeutic catheter connects to the MAP socket of the PIU via an extension cable.

### Ultrasound System Setup

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Setting Up the Ultrasound System\_EN.xml

The ultrasound system(s) at your site must be set up for compatibility with the CARTO™ 3 System when installing the CARTOSOUND™ Module. This must be done for each ultrasound system at your site that will be used with the CARTO™ 3 System. Once set up, a label will be applied to the ultrasound system indicating its unique name on the CARTO™ 3 System (for connectivity purposes).

#### Incompatible Modes of Operation

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/incompatibleModes\_v8p2\_EN.xml

Numerous modes of operation are available on the ultrasound system. The CARTO™ 3 System supports the following modes:

* Full size
* 2D
* Color Doppler (supported only with video over LAN configuration)
* ROI (Region of Interest)

If any other mode is used, an error message will appear in the CARTO™ 3 System.

REP14517 D-37

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteDanger.png](data:image/png;base64...) | **WARNING**  Mismatch between the ultrasound video mode setting (PAL/NTSC/VGA) and the expected mode on the CARTO™ 3 System will result in an error. |

#### Considerations for ACUSON Ultrasound Systems

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Considerations\_acuson\_v8\_EN.xml

**ACUSON P500™ and ACUSON SC2000™ Ultrasound Systems**:[[29]](#footnote-29) Only the 2D mode (B-mode) is supported.

Use of any other mode will cause the CARTO™ 3 System to issue an error.

#### Considerations for Vivid™ Ultrasound Systems

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Considerations\_vivid\_v8\_EN.xml

When using the Vivid™ *iq* ultrasound system with the CARTO™ 3 System, the ultrasound dataflow must be set to CartoSound interface in the Vivid™ system.

For Vivid™ S70/S70N/S60/S60N ultrasound systems, the CartoSound workflow must be turned ON (on the touch panel, select More > Utility > CartoSound). To set the dataflow, refer to the Preset and System Setup chapter of the Vivid User Guide.

Only the 2D mode (B-mode) is supported. Use of any other mode will cause the CARTO™ 3 System to issue an error.

BR97338

For the Vivid™ *iq* and Vivid™ S70/S70N/S60/S60N ultrasound systems, only the single window display mode is supported in the CARTO™ 3 System.

## Concepts and Software Options

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Concepts and Software Options\_EN.xml

The CARTOSOUND™ Module provides ultrasound compatibility along with software features for enhancing an electrophysiology study.

Before performing an ultrasound study with the CARTOSOUND™ Module, it is important to understand certain concepts relating to the function of ultrasound systems with the CARTO™ 3 System, and how data from an ultrasound frame is added to a reconstruction.

### Contours in CARTO™ 3 System Maps

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Understanding Maps that Include Ultrasound Contours\_EN.xml

Ultrasound images are relayed from the ultrasound system to the CARTO™ 3 System, where they are displayed in real time. The Ultrasound window is used to pause real-time imaging and display a 3-second video clip. A marker in the Ultrasound window indicates the frames of the clip that are gated to the end-expiration phase and to the maximal LA volume (end of the T wave ±2 frames).

The System supports the following volume of contour data, with frames and stored clips counted as contours:

* 100 unique clips acquired during a single study
* 50 contours saved in a single clip
* 200 saved contours in a single map
* 400 saved contours in a single study

You can then draw a contour outlining a structure of interest and assign the contour to a specific map. The contour is made up of a series of points that approximately delineate the desired anatomical shape when added to a map. Contours contribute only location information to a map. No electrical data is acquired from a contour. Each contour added to a map is listed in the Point List (indicated by an ultrasound icon).

There are two types of contours:

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Normal | A normal contour contributes directly to the anatomical shape of a map. Normal contours are included in surface registration. |
| Floating | Floating contours are displayed in space relative to the map, and do not contribute to the map itself. When using CARTOMERGE™ Module, floating contours are not calculated in surface registration. |

As contours are added to a map, the reconstruction is created in the same manner as with standard points acquired with a mapping catheter. The map is updated in the Main Map Viewer (and Additional Map Viewer, if open).

You can mark contours with Contour Tags or OS Tags to label anatomical structures. Contour Tags add a color and label to an anatomical structure such as the LIPV. OS Tags use colored dots at the beginning of the line to mark the ostium, valves, artery, or appendage.

Electrical data is acquired only via a Biosense Webster mapping catheter. If point data is acquired with a mapping catheter, the electroanatomical information is added to the reconstruction. Color is applied to maps in the standard manner. Map shape is recalculated as points and contours are added to the reconstruction.

1.40.4. V1; REP4611 D-V1-1.36.1

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteDanger.png](data:image/png;base64...) | **WARNING**  If a mismatch is suspected between ultrasound contours and standard mapping points, or inaccurate synchronization between ultrasound images and their related signals, use fluoroscopy to verify catheter tip position, and perform one of the following procedures:   * Create a new map based on standard mapping points only. * Use a new SOUNDSTAR™ Catheter. * Contact the Biosense Webster Field Support Department.   Failure to follow the above instructions might result in incorrect placement of the catheter tip, and harm to the patient. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  When using ARA, you can assign contours to any map that uses ARA (single or multi-channel) from the same socket. The reference annotation of the assigned contour and the displayed channel in the Ultrasound window are derived from the source map. |

### Ultrasound Window

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/UltrasoundViewer\_v8\_EN.xml

The ultrasound fan is displayed in the Ultrasound window. The Ultrasound window provides functions for freezing a frame, drawing a contour, assigning the contour to a CARTO™ 3 System Map, and returning to a saved video clip.

#### Ultrasound Window Toolbars

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/CARTOSOUND\_v8\_phase-3\_Toolbar\_EN.xml

The Contour Toolbar provides options for creating a clip, and creating, editing, and assigning contours.

The Ultrasound Toolbar provides options for display settings.

The toolbar has three states: collapsed, favorites, and expanded. The default view is favorites.

* Collapsed: One icon is visible.
* Favorites: Three icons are visible. The default icons are Maps Intersection, Maps Tag Intersection, and Show Tip Overlay.
* Expanded: All icons are visible.

You can customize the toolbar by dragging and dropping icons. This will change which icons are visible in each state.

You can drag and drop the toolbar anywhere within the Ultrasound window.

#### Ultrasound Window Display Options

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ultrasoundViewer\_tools\_v8\_EN.xml

Click ![tools_general.png](data:image/png;base64...) in the Ultrasound window to open the following map display options:

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Show fan outline | Hide/show the boundaries of the ultrasound fan and orientation (red square) of the transducer, in correlation to the display in the Ultrasound window. It is recommended that this option always be enabled.  See also ["Orientation and Scale of Ultrasound Images"](#target9000053) |
| Resume imaging on assign | When enabled, automatically returns to display of streaming ultrasound after a contour is assigned to a map. |
| Show contours while playing clip | When enabled, unassigned contours are displayed as the clip is played. |
| Display image settings | Enables adjustment of the contrast, brightness, gamma, and transparency in the image in the Ultrasound window only. |

#### Orientation and Scale of Ultrasound Images

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/cartosound\_orientation\_scale\_v7-5\_EN.xml

Display of ultrasound images on the CARTO™ 3 System includes information provided by the ultrasound system. For the purposes of the CARTO™ 3 System extracting data for a map, only the area that reflects the ultrasound data itself is needed.

The icon (representing the orientation of the streaming ultrasound), and the scale of the ultrasound data (represented by small dots along the side of the fan) are displayed differently depending on the ultrasound system being used.

The Show fan outline option shows the correlation between the actual scale of the ultrasound image on the ultrasound system and the CARTO™ 3 System. The scale on the CARTO™ 3 System is indicated by a red outline on the boundaries of the ultrasound images.

The ultrasound transducer icon indicates the orientation of the ultrasound images on the ultrasound system. When the Show fan outline option is enabled, the red square indicates how the ultrasound orientation is detected on the CARTO™ 3 System. The red square and the ultrasound transducer icon should always be aligned relative to the ultrasound image. To activate the option, click ![tools_general.png](data:image/png;base64...), and select Show fan outline.

V3: REP4611 1.15.6, V8: REP14517 D-37

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteCaution.png](data:image/png;base64...) | *CAUTION*  It is recommended that the Show fan outline option always be enabled. |

#### Tagging Contours

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/CSound\_Tagging\_Contours\_EN.xml

You can mark contours with Contour Tags or OS Tags to label anatomical structures. Contour Tags add a color and label to an anatomical structure such as the LIPV. OS Tags use colored dots at the beginning of the line to mark the ostium, valves, artery, or appendage.

Tags can be applied to contours in the same way that tags are applied to points. When a contour tag is applied, the contour outline is tagged with the color for that tag and receives a unique label.

When applying contour tags, OS options are available. The OS contour tags are used to create OS anatomical tags.

When working with the CARTOSOUND™ Module, the Contour Tag dialog box includes several default contour tags, and options for creating new contour tags.

You can tag an acquired contour or change the tag for a selected contour in an active *or inactive* map using the Tag drop-down menu.

### Setting Frame Gating

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/CSoundFAM\_Setting\_Gating\_Default\_EN.xml

When you acquire a clip, an Input Frame is gated to the end-expiration phase and to the maximal LA volume (end of the T wave ±2 frames), according to the default Frame Gating settings.

You can change the default settings if required, as follows:

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | After acquiring a clip, in the Ultrasound viewer, click ![uls8_FAM_frame_settings.png](data:image/png;base64...) to display the Frame Gating settings.  ![CSoundFAM_Frame_Gating_Settings_v8.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 2. | From the list, select the gating method:   * % shift of CL * mSec shift of CL |
| 3. | Set a value for a percentage or specified milliseconds relative to the Cycle Length. |
| 4. | If you want the input frame to be calculated only after the respiration phase is final (with a delay of up to 3 seconds), select the Verified Respiration check box.  If you want the input frame to be calculated as soon as the clip is acquired, leave the Verified Respiration check box cleared. |
| 5. | Click OK to activate the new settings or click Cancel to return to the previous settings. |

# CARTOUNIVU™ Module

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/fluoroBG\_title\_v7\_EN.xml

## CARTOUNIVU™ Module Overview

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/fluoroBG\_overview\_c3\_v7\_EN.xml

With the **CARTOUNIVU™ Module**, fluoroscopic captures (images and cine clips) can be imported to and displayed in the CARTO™ 3 System.[[30]](#footnote-30)

These images are not for diagnostic purposes.

When fluoroscopic captures are integrated into the CARTO™ 3 System map viewers (Main Map Viewer and Additional Map Viewer), CARTO™ 3 Maps, MRI or CT images, and catheter visualizations are superimposed on the fluoroscopic capture.

In order to work with the CARTOUNIVU™ Module, the following is required:

* Dedicated installation and initial calibration

The CARTOUNIVU™ Module installation is unique to a specific Location Pad, Registration Plate, fluoroscopy system, and EP lab.

* Registration between the fluoroscopy system and the CARTO™ 3 System for each procedure.

The CARTOUNIVU™ Module extends the working range of the fluoroscopy system (SID, table height, etc.) during a CARTO™ 3 Procedure. After registration, you can work with the detector positioned closer to the patient and with the patient table lowered closer to the collimator.

REP4611 1.6.2.2, v3 tab; A-V2-1.4.3.1

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteDanger.png](data:image/png;base64...) | **WARNING**  Before applying energy for ablation, visually verify correctness of the tip position of the mapping catheter in relation to the visualized map. Use your common clinical practice for real-time verification of catheter locations throughout the procedure (inspection of IC signals, direct imaging guidance such as fluoroscopy or ultrasound, etc.). Failure to do so might result in incorrect placement of the catheter. |

### Supported Fluoroscopy Systems

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/fluoroBG\_supportedSystems\_v6p5\_EN.xml

The following systems are supported:

* GE Innova System, Discovery System and Allia System
* Philips Allura, Azurion, and UNIQ Systems
* Siemens Artis Systems
* Canon Medical Infinix and Alphenix Systems

The compatibility of specific systems depends on several components, such as software version, table type, and detector type. Compatibility of a specific machine will be determined according to an inspection form.

Consult your Biosense Webster representative for details of compatible systems.

### Installation

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/fluoroBG\_hwAndInstallation\_EN.xml

Biosense Webster service personnel are responsible for activating the CARTOUNIVU™ Module on the Workstation, connecting the fluoroscopy system with the CARTO™ 3 System, and calibrating the CARTOUNIVU™ Module. The installation includes attaching a Registration Plate to the Location Pad.

After installation, the fluoroscopy system will be listed in the Study Setup screen.

Addendum to V6, SRA002588

**Considerations for labs equipped with MediGuide™ Systems:**

MediGuide™ Systems must be turned off during CARTO™ 3 procedures. Turning the MediGuide™ System on may affect the conditions under which the CARTO™ 3 System was installed and calibrated.

## CARTOUNIVU™ Module Study

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/fluoroBG\_study\_v7\_EN.xml

### Registration

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/fluoroBG\_registration\_c3\_v3\_EN.xml

Registration is the process of aligning the coordinates of the fluoroscopy system with the CARTO™ 3 System.

If a fluoroscopy system was selected in the Study Setup screen, you will automatically be prompted to perform registration at the beginning of the initialization process.

REP4611 1.6.1.15, v3 tab; REP4611 A-V3-1.6.1.2, D-V3-1.6.6.1

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteDanger.png](data:image/png;base64...) | **WARNING**  Ensure that the fluoroscopic image is captured in AP orientation, and that neither the patient table nor the Location Pad moves from the acquisition of the registration image until the registration process is complete. |

#### Perform Registration

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/fbg\_Register\_EN.xml

Before you begin, ensure the following:

* The fluoroscopy system is switched on.
* The fluoroscopy system is connected to the CARTO™ 3 System.
* The Registration Plate is attached to the Location Pad.
* All mandatory fields in the fluoroscopy system are filled in.

Ensure that Head First - Supine is selected (nose up, legs down).

* The fluoroscopy system is in AP orientation, with no tilt or rotation in the patient table, and the C‑Arm is in the head position.

REP4611 1.6.1.14, V3 tab

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteDanger.png](data:image/png;base64...) | **WARNING**  Ensure that the disc is visible. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  VSTS 36267: NTF  If a back patch becomes out of range during a CARTOUNIVU™ Study (due to movement of the patient or a patch), a message appears with options to continue with the current registration or to repeat registration.  If this occurs, do not choose the Continue option. Instead, reposition the back patches and then perform registration again. (If you do not reposition the back patches first, the Registration window does not appear.) |

### Receiving Fluoroscopic Captures

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/fluoroBG\_receivingCaptures\_c3\_v4\_EN.xml

The fluoroscopic captures are acquired in the fluoroscopy system and sent to the CARTO™ 3 System. The captures will be displayed in CARTO™ 3 Map Viewers at their acquisition orientation. The captures are stored in the CARTO™ 3 System in orientation regions of 5º. Up to 10 images can be saved for each region. After that, the 11th image overwrites the first, and so on.

REP4611 1.6.2.2, v3 tab; REP4611 1.6.4.1, v3 tab

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Fluoroscopic captures are not gated according to heartbeat or respiration. If possible, manually gate the fluoroscopic captures to the end-expiration and end-diastole phases. |

Images can be imported to the CARTO™ 3 System automatically or manually.

REP4611 1.6.1.5, V3 tab

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  When importing captures manually, import immediately after capturing the image or cine clip in fluoroscopy system. |

#### Viewing Captures in Review Mode

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/fluoroBG\_reviewMode\_c3\_v3\_EN.xml

Up to 10 fluoroscopic captures are saved with the study for each orientation region.

If a cine capture is marked as a Favorite, the entire capture is saved. If a cine capture is not marked as a Favorite, the last frame and the last displayed frame of the cine are saved. (In the event of an unplanned exit, the entire cine capture is saved.)

These captures can be viewed in Review Mode.

#### Rescaling Fluoroscopic Captures

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/fluoroBG\_repositioning\_v4-2\_EN.xml

Fluoroscopic captures are presented in a perspective view, where closer objects appear smaller. In the CARTO™ 3 System, objects always appear the same size, regardless of distance. Because of this difference in presentation, captures are scaled when they are received by the CARTO™ 3 System.

When a new capture is received, its position in the CARTO™ 3 System map is determined according to a region of interest defined by the position of the catheter connected to the MAP socket. If the MAP catheter is not connected, the System searches for an alternative catheter that is connected to the 20 POLE A, 20 POLE B, ULTRASOUND, or REF/DECA socket (in that order). If no catheter is connected when the capture is received, the region of interest will be defined by the fluoroscopy system's isocenter level.

To avoid compromising the alignment between the fluoroscopic capture and the CARTO™ 3 System map, it is recommended to position a catheter that is connected to one of the previously mentioned PIU sockets in the chamber of interest when receiving a new capture.

If captures were received when there was no catheter in the relevant chamber or if the region of interest changed, you can rescale the previously acquired captures according to current catheter position. (This applies only to captures that are currently displayed in the map viewer.) To rescale the currently displayed capture, place the catheter in the appropriate chamber and then click ![fbg_reposition_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...) on the CARTOUNIVU™ Toolbar.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteCaution.png](data:image/png;base64...) | *CAUTION*  This rescaling is not reversible. Ensure that the catheter is in the region of interest before you rescale the captures. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  For manually adjusted captures, the rescaling is only visible after manual adjustment is disabled.  For a capture that is part of a rotational scan, the rescaling affects all images included in the same rotational scan. |

#### Unsupported Modes

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/fluoroBG\_unsupported\_c3\_v4\_EN.xml

REP4611 1.6.1.6, v3 tab; REP4611 1.6.1.7, v3 tab

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Do not capture images in the fluoroscopy system when the C‑Arm or table is in an unsupported mode. |

Table 40: Unsupported Fluoroscopy System Modes with CARTOUNIVU™ Module

| Mode | Notes |
| --- | --- |
| Patient table tilt or rotation | No tilt in patient table (roll, pitch, or yaw) |
| Detector rotation | No rotation of the fluoroscope detector before and during the procedure  The detector's base orientation (portrait or landscape) is determined during module installation. |
| Fluoroscope arm position | Must be in head position |

If the fluoroscopy system enters an unsupported mode, troubleshooting information appears in the Geometry Information window. Follow the instructions until the issue is resolved. The text and the image are updated in real time to reflect the current status.

![fbg_geoInfo_troubleshooting_pin.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 59: Geometry Information Window Displaying Troubleshooting Information

When the issue is resolved, the Geometry Information window is updated. Close the window and continue working.

### CARTOUNIVU™ Toolbar

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/fbg\_toolbar\_ref\_c3\_v4\_EN.xml

The CARTOUNIVU™ Toolbar provides status indication, alerts and information, and buttons for using the module. A separate toolbar appears in each map viewer. The availability of buttons depends on the current orientation and action.

Some features are only available with specific fluoroscopy systems.

The CARTOUNIVU™ Toolbar buttons are used for displaying and managing fluoroscopic captures.

The extended cine player provides additional playback options. It also displays current respiration gating status, annotations, and synchronization status.[[31]](#footnote-31) To open the extended player, rest the cursor over the play or pause icon for 2 seconds.

See also:

* ["Rescaling Fluoroscopic Captures"](#target9000054)

# CARTO™ CFAE Module

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/CFAE\_intro\_ifu\_EN.xml

1.31.3. V1; REP4611 A-V1-1.31.1

The **CARTO™ CFAE Module** enables detection of Complex Fractionated Atrial Electrograms (CFAE) on IC electrograms, based on user-defined parameters. The system measures the amplitude and duration of CFAEs. In addition, it calculates the interval between consecutive CFAEs. The system generates color-coded maps displaying areas of CFAEs according to the degree of signal fractionation. The system also generates color-coded maps displaying areas of CFAEs according to Shortest Complex Interval values.[[32]](#footnote-32)

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteDanger.png](data:image/png;base64...) | **WARNING**  The clinical significance of utilizing the CFAE Module to help identify areas with complex fractionated atrial electrograms for catheter ablation of atrial arrhythmias, such as atrial fibrillation, has not been established by clinical investigations. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  You must acquire EA points before activating CFAE functionality.  If CFAE is activated and then points are acquired, no threshold lines or peaks appear in the Selected Point Viewer. If this issue occurs, switch the map to LAT and then back to CFAE. The lines and peaks will appear. |

## Displaying a New CFAE Map

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Displaying\_CFAE\_Map\_c3\_ifu\_EN.xml

CFAE maps are displayed according to values for specific CFAE parameters. Since CFAE phenomena are considered to be relatively focal, map coloring is per point. Color interpolation is restricted; the maps are visually similar to Remaps, with gray color applied to areas for which no electrical data is available.

A CFAE map is colored according to the shortest complex interval, which is the shortest interval between identified repeated complexes, for each acquired point.

The color scale bar indicates the maximum and minimum durations [ms] detected (purple to red, respectively).

CFAE maps can be displayed in the Main Map Viewer or the Additional Map Viewer.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE   * When displaying a CFAE map, points with ABL tags are defined as Location Only.   VSTS 35979: NTF   * CFAE tags are not projected on images (CARTOMERGE™ Module). |

## Selected Point Viewer

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/annotation\_SelectedPointViewer\_C3\_EN.xml

The Selected Point Viewer displays the shortest interval of the selected point and all the enabled display parameters.
