## Continuous Mapping

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_acquisition\_intro\_v6-3\_EN.xml

The **Continuous Mapping Feature** enables continuous acquisition of EA points which meet a set of pre-defined filters.

You must set the filters and their thresholds before mapping. After you activate Continuous Mapping, the CARTO™ 3 System continuously acquires points each time all the pre-defined filters are met on each reference annotation. Acquisition continues until you stop it. Points can be manually acquired during Continuous Mapping.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Points acquired by continuous acquisition are not synchronized with recording system, even if the systems are in one of the synchronized modes. |

### Continuous Mapping Filters

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_acquisition\_preferences\_v6p5\_EN.xml

When electroanatomical points are acquired, they are evaluated according to a set of pre-defined filters. Points are accepted only if they pass all the selected filters.

Filters are set in the Acquisition Filters tab of the Preferences dialog box. Each map has its own settings. The name of the current map is displayed in the Acquisition Filters tab of the Preferences dialog box.

Table 41: Map Related Filters for Continuous Mapping

| Filter | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Cycle Length | Activates the selected cycle length filter (Stability or Range).   * Range: A point can be acquired only if the cycle length is within the defined range. * Stability: A point can be acquired if the difference (%) between the current cycle length and the median cycle length of all relevant points in the current map is less than the selected threshold.   Floating points, location-only points, and points in the Recycle Bin are not included in the calculation. |
| Pattern Matching | The Pattern Matching option is not available if there are no captured patterns.  See ["Continuous Mapping with Pattern Matching"](#target9000055). |
| Position Stability | A point can be acquired if the location stability between consecutive beats is less than or equal to the threshold. |
| LAT Stability | A point can be acquired if the LAT difference between consecutive beats is less than or equal to the threshold.  Consecutive points with no LAT value are considered to be stable. |
| Force | REP4611 D-V3-1.3.1.15  A point can be acquired according to the force reading. Select either Above Minimum Threshold (equal to or greater than the minimum force threshold) or Within Thresholds (between the minimum and maximum thresholds).  Available only when the mapping catheter is a force catheter.[[34]](#footnote-34) |
| Do Not Acquire While Pacing | If selected, points are not acquired during stimulation.  Applies only to catheters connected to the PIU. |
| Density | REP4611 A-V6-1.10.1  A point can be acquired only if it is at least this far from all existing points.  The first point acquired in a region defines the center of a sphere. No other points can be acquired within that sphere, unless the first point has no LAT value. In this case, the first point with a LAT value is also acquired. |

You can change the Continuous Mapping settings during acquisition. Changes take effect after you click Apply or OK.

The System Filters apply for both Continuous Mapping and manual acquisition.

Table 42: System Filters

| Filter | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Tissue Proximity | A point can be acquired if it is in close proximity to the tissue.  Available only for mapping catheters which support Tissue Proximity Indication (TPI) |
| Respiration Gated | A point can be acquired only if it is within the respiratory gating threshold. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  You can change the filters before initialization. After clicking the New Study button, configure the required filter settings from the Preferences dialog box. The active map's Acquisition Filters settings are saved to the template (except Respiration Gated, Pattern Matching filter state and selected pattern). |

### Activating Continuous Mapping

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_acquisition\_activating\_v7\_EN.xml

The Continuous Mapping mode is selected from the acquisition panel.

Table 43: Acquisition Panel with Continuous Mapping

| Appearance | Description |
| --- | --- |
| ![acquire_panel_ea_CM.png](data:image/png;base64...)  ![acquire_panel_ea_CM_FAM.PNG](data:image/png;base64...) | Click ![acquire_button_continuous.png](data:image/png;base64...) to start Continuous Mapping.  The position of the button depends on the map type (Point-by-Point or FAM). |
| ![acquire_panel_ea_FAM_CM_active_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...) | The Continuous Mapping button is orange and the image is animated while acquisition is in progress.  Left-click the Continuous Mapping button to end acquisition.  Right-click the Continuous Mapping button to open the Filters Status toolbar.  If the system stops Continuous Mapping, the Continuous Mapping button alternates twice between orange and blue, then stays blue. |
| ![acquire_panel_ea_FAM_CM_linked_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Click ![acquire_panel_link_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...) to start both Continuous Mapping and FAM acquisition.  This button is disabled if either FAM or Continuous Mapping is disabled. |
| ![acquire_panel_CM_disabled_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...) | If Continuous Mapping is not available, the button is gray. |
| ![acquire_panel_filter.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Click the filter icon to open the Acquisition Filters tab of the Preferences dialog box. |

Points acquired in this mode are the same as manually-acquired EA points. Points are acquired to the Active Map. If you change the Active Map, acquisition stops.

During Continuous Mapping, the last acquired point or point group is not automatically selected in the Point List, the Selected Point Viewer, or the map. The Beat Buffer is not available for automatically acquired points.

If the Point List is sorted by anything other than index number, the sorting is not updated until Continuous Mapping stops. An icon appears by the column to indicate that sorting is not up to date. The list is sorted automatically when Continuous Mapping stops.

Continuous Mapping is not available in the following situations:

* Application of energy for ablation is detected
* Respiration gating training is required (if filter is active)
* The number of points in the map or the study is near the limit
* CFAE is active (CARTO™ CFAE Module)
* Replay mode is active (CARTOREPLAY™ Module)

### Continuous Mapping Status

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_acquisition\_status\_v7\_EN.xml

REP4611 v6 tab, continuous mapping, ways to find why points aren't acquired

The Continuous Mapping Filters Status toolbar gives a visual indication of the active filters. There are several ways to open the toolbar:

* Right-click the Main Map Viewer background and select Toolbars > Filters Status.
* Press CTRL + ALT + F.
* Right-click the Continuous Mapping button in the Acquisition panel.
* Click the filter icon in the Preferences dialog box.

A tab at the top of the toolbar displays the map name and the pattern number (if the pattern matching filter is active).

![filter_status_V7p3.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 60: Filters Status Toolbar

The Continuous Mapping filters are evaluated in the following order: cycle length (stability or range), stimulation, pattern matching, tissue proximity, force, position stability, LAT stability, and density. If a filter is not active, the row is hidden.

* **Green:** The channel met the filter threshold
* **Red:** The channel did not meet the filter threshold
* **Gray:** The channel was not evaluated (did not meet a previous filter)

The filter status is calculated with each annotation.

![filter_status_V7p3_failed.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 61: Filters Status Toolbar: No Point Acquired

To collapse the Filters Status toolbar, click the filter icon at the top of the toolbar. Only the tab with the map name is visible. The lower border of the tab indicates the status: it is green if the point passes all the filters, and red if it does not.

![filter_statusesV7p3_collapsed.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 62: Filters Status Toolbar: Collapsed

If Continuous Mapping is active but points were not acquired recently, click the filter icon to view the current acquisition filters in the Filters Status toolbar. Adjust the filters as needed. (Click the pattern indication to open the Pattern Matching Viewer.) Changes are applied immediately.

![Filter_status_toolbar_in_edit_mode_v8.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 63: Filters Status Toolbar in Edit Mode

For MEM catheters with a single spline, such as LASSO™ Catheters, indication is presented for each mapping channel.

Figure 64: Filters Status Toolbar

For multi-spline catheters, the indication is presented for each spline. The display can be expanded to view mapping channels.

Figure 65: Filters Status Toolbar per Spline (Left) and per Electrode Pair

The PTRN row displays the current correlation value.

![continuous_acqu_filters_status_pattern_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 66: Filters Status Toolbar: Pattern Matching Correlation

| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  When the Respiration Gated filter is used, the Density filter is not applied continuously. At the end-expiration phase, the filter is applied to points that passed all the other filters within the last respiratory gate. This means that the density indication is not synchronized with the other filters.  The Respiration Gated filter status is only displayed when the Filters Status toolbar is in edit mode. |

### Continuous Mapping with Pattern Matching

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_patternMatch\_intro\_7p3\_EN.xml

The Pattern Matching filter is an optional Continuous Mapping filter. It allows acquiring points that match a user-defined pattern from body surface (BS) ECG signals or from unipolar intracardiac (IC) electrograms.

A pattern is a collection of signals captured in a single beat. The *pattern of interest (POI)* is the section of the pattern representing the desired activation. The system automatically suggests a POI based on the pattern type and reference channel.

When a pattern is captured, a reference annotation is calculated according to the reference channel and criterion of the Active Map. When a pattern is selected as an Acquisition Filter, the reference annotation of each real-time beat is aligned with the reference annotation of the Pattern, and the real-time signals enclosed by the POI is used for calculating the weighted correlation value. It is recommended to use the same reference channel and criterion on both the pattern and the map.

* BS Pattern: Weighted correlation is calculated based on the 12-lead ECG.
* IC Pattern: Weighted correlation is calculated based on the selected unipolar channels on the reference catheter electrode. (The default is 10 channels.)

The typical Pattern Matching workflow consists of the following activities:

* Capturing and defining a pattern (see ["Capturing Patterns"](#target9000056) and ["Defining and Editing Patterns in the Pattern Bank"](#target9000057))
* Setting a pattern filter for Continuous Mapping (see ["Setting the Pattern Matching Filter"](#target9000058))
* Optional: Comparing real-time signal with the captured pattern (see ["Viewing the Active Pattern"](#target9000059))
* Optional: Start a new map with a name based on the captured pattern (see ["Creating New Map from Pattern Capture"](#target9000060))

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  If the PASO™ Module is installed, the capture button is also available in the PASO™ Viewer toolbar.  If the CARTOREPLAY™ Module is installed, the capture button is also available in the Timeline toolbar. Only body surface signals can be acquired in Timeline mode. |

#### Capturing Patterns

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_patternMatch\_capture\_v7p5\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Use the menu on the Pattern Capture button to select the pattern type: body surface (BS Pattern) or intracardiac (IC Pattern).  The default pattern type is set according to the selected reference channel. The IC Pattern option is only available when the reference annotation is intracardiac.  ![PM_capture_options_v75.png](data:image/png;base64...)  The Pattern Capture button shows the current status. ![PM_capture_IC_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 2. | Click the Pattern Capture button.  62.5 seconds of recorded data are captured. The Pattern Bank window opens, with the automatically suggested POI.  ![pattern_bank_window_v8.png](data:image/png;base64...)  The system assigns each captured pattern a name that consists of the pattern type (IC PTRN or BS PTRN) and a number. |

The System automatically determines the POI according to the pattern type.

**Body Surface Patterns (BS PTRN):**

* For body surface reference channels, the POI is set from earliest onset to latest offset of the 12 ECG leads surrounding the reference annotation.
* For IC reference channels:
  + Automatic Reference Criterion (ARA): POI is set around ventricular activation closest to the latest annotation
  + Other reference criterion: POI is set around the onset and offset of the activation surrounding the selected IC reference annotation

**Intracardiac Patterns (IC PTRN):** The POI is automatically set from earliest onset to latest offset of the selected unipolar channels, surrounding the selected beat's reference annotation.

* Automatic (ARA) Reference Criterion: The POI is suggested around latest reference annotation with a minimum of 100 ms between atrial and ventricular activation.
* Other IC Reference Criterion: POI is suggested around the latest reference annotation, regardless of the chamber of activation.
* Pacing reference: POI is suggested around the latest reference annotation (i.e., pacing spike).

The system can store up to 40 captured patterns per study, including body surface patterns, intracardiac patterns, and Induced Signals (PASO™ Module). To capture additional patterns, delete existing patterns. (This limit does not include patterns that are copied in the CONFIDENSE™ Module.)

#### Defining and Editing Patterns in the Pattern Bank

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_patternMatch\_editPattern\_v7p3\_EN.xml

The Pattern Bank window is used to view all patterns in the study. The viewer opens by default when you capture a signal. To open it later, click Map > Pattern Bank, or right-click ![PM_capture_bs_icon.png](data:image/png;base64...).

#### Setting the Pattern Matching Filter

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_patternMatch\_set\_patternFilters\_v7p3\_EN.xml

REP4611 A-V6-1.10.2

To use the Pattern Matching filter during Continuous Mapping, you must first set the filter for the map. This can be done in one of following ways:

* In the Acquisition Filters tab of the Preferences dialog box.
* In the Filters Status Toolbar in edit mode (see ["Continuous Mapping Status"](#target9000061)).
* Create a new map from the Pattern Bank after capturing a pattern. The pattern and additional filters are set according to the selected arrhythmia type (see ["Creating New Map from Pattern Capture"](#target9000062)).

If the filter is active during Continuous Mapping, points are acquired only if their pattern matches the selected pattern *and* if they meet all other applicable filters.

![continuous_acqu_filters_pattern_v7p3.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 67: Acquisition Filters: Pattern Matching

The Pattern Matching option is not available if there are no captured patterns.

After the pattern filter is assigned, click the View icon (![continuous_acqu_filters_view_v6.png](data:image/png;base64...)) to display and edit it in the Pattern Matching Viewer (see ["Viewing the Active Pattern"](#target9000063)).

REP9626 1.11.1, v7 ICPM

During Continuous Mapping, use the Filters Status toolbar for a visual indication of the active filters.

#### Viewing the Active Pattern

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_patternMatch\_viewPattern\_v7p3\_EN.xml

The Pattern Matching Viewer is for viewing and editing the pattern associated with the map. The pattern can be displayed with the real-time overlay (**Play** mode) or without the overlay (**Stop** mode). The pattern signal is displayed in gray. The real-time signal is displayed in the color you selected for the ECG signal in the Catheter Setup screen.

There are two ways to open the Pattern Matching Viewer:

* Click the PTRN row in the Filters Status toolbar.

The Pattern Matching Viewer appears in **Play** mode.

Click ![ptrn_switch_square.png](data:image/png;base64...) in the upper right corner to switch to **Stop** mode.

* Click the View icon (![continuous_acqu_filters_view_v6.png](data:image/png;base64...)) in Acquisition Filters tab of the Preferences dialog box.

The Pattern Matching Viewer appears in **Stop** mode.

Click ![ptrn_switch_arrow.png](data:image/png;base64...) in the upper right corner to switch to **Play** mode.

![pattern_matching_viewer_play_and_stop_v8.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 68: Pattern Matching Viewer in Play Mode (Left) and Stop Mode (Right)

For BS pattern, if a point is acquired when the Pattern Matching filter and Continuous Mapping are active, the pattern is locked for that specific map. In the Pattern Matching Viewer, this status is indicated by an icon next to the pattern name.

![pattern_matching_viewer_locked_v8.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 69: Pattern Matching Viewer with Locked Pattern

When the pattern is locked:

* You cannot select a different pattern for that map. The Pattern Matching drop-down list in the Acquisition Filters tab is disabled.
* You cannot change the pattern's beat selection (that is, reference annotation).
* You cannot delete the pattern from the Pattern Bank.

The POI and the threshold can be adjusted at any stage.

If all map points are permanently deleted from the recycle bin, the pattern becomes unlocked.

When using IC pattern matching the pattern is not locked, even if points are filtered by it. You can select a different pattern for a map, but you cannot delete the pattern from the Pattern Bank or change the pattern's beat selection.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  You can also open the Acquisition Filters tab of the Preferences dialog box, and then click the View icon (![continuous_acqu_filters_view_v6.png](data:image/png;base64...)). |

##### Working with Intracardiac Patterns

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/parallel-LAT\_ICPM\_v7p3\_EN.xml

When IC pattern matching is used, the electrode position in the heart must be stable. Therefore, the CS catheter is recommended.

![pattern_matching_viewer_IC_play_mode.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 70: IC Pattern in Pattern Matching Viewer, Play Mode

REP9626 1.11.3, v7 ICPM

The unipolar leads used in the correlation calculation can be changed. Clear or select the channel in the Pattern Matching Viewer. After a channel is removed from the correlation calculation, it is displayed in a darker color.

To hide the removed channels in the Pattern Matching Viewer, click the channel selection button and clear the channels. Only channels which are not part of the correlation can be removed from view. The same channels are displayed for this pattern in all the pattern viewers.

![pattern_matching_viewer_IC_show_signals_v8.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 71: IC Pattern Display in Pattern Matching Viewer

The Pattern Matching Viewer supports viewing correlation value per channel. Channels can be added or removed during mapping as required.

* Red value – Correlation is below the selected IC pattern threshold.
* Green value – Correlation is above the selected IC pattern threshold.

When using the IC pattern matching filter consider deselecting the unstable channels.

If the IC pattern correlation values drops during mapping, consider whether activation has changed or if the IC reference catheter moved.

![RedGreen_Correlation.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 72: Red (correlation below selected value) and Green value (correlation above selected value)

The display can also be set to unipolar or bipolar, from the drop-down arrow on the channel selection button.

* The weighted correlation calculation and displayed value always derive from the unipolar channels, even if the Bipolar view is selected.
* Adding or removing channels from the correlation calculation can only be done in the Unipolar view.

![patternMatchingViewer_IC_menu.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 73: Selecting Channel Display in the Pattern Matching Viewer

##### Viewing Pattern Matching for Point

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_patternMatch\_patternForPoint\_v6p5\_EN.xml

The Pattern Matching for Point window displays the signal overlay of the selected point.

* For points that were captured during Continuous Mapping (automatically or manually) when the Pattern Matching filter was active, the POI and weighted correlation value are also displayed.
* For points that were acquired when Continuous Mapping was not active or when the Pattern Matching filter was not selected, only the ECG overlay is displayed.

To open the Pattern Matching for Point window, right-click a point in the Point List or in a map viewer, and select Show Pattern Matching. No changes can be made in this window.

![patternMatching_forPoint_7p3.png](data:image/png;base64...)

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_patternMatch\_capture\_newMap\_v7p3\_EN.xml

You can create a new map from the Pattern Bank after capturing a pattern.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Capture a pattern according to the Pattern Capture button settings (BS Pattern or IC Pattern).  The Pattern Bank window opens.  ![pattern_bank_window_v8.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 2. | Adjust the beat and the POI as necessary. |
| 3. | Select an arrhythmia type from the Type list.  For body surface captures, the list includes both atrial and ventricular arrhythmias.  ![patternBank_afterCapture_IC_list.png](data:image/png;base64...)  The New Map button is enabled.  ![pattern_bank_new_map_button_enabled.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 4. | Click the New Map button.  A new map appears, using the settings of the original map. The map name includes the selected arrhythmia type and cycle length information for the selected POI. The suggested WOI, based on the arrhythmia type and captured POI, can be adjusted in the Annotation Viewer.  The Filters Status toolbar opens in Edit Mode, with filters set according to the selected arrhythmia. For all arrhythmias:   * Pattern Matching filter (PTRN) is on. * Force filter (FRC) is off. * Density filter (DEN) is 1 mm.   For all other filter defaults, see ["Default Filter Settings"](#target9000064).  ![pattern_bank_filter_TPI_NA_v8.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 5. | Adjust the filters, if needed.  Optional: To change the unipolar IC leads used for correlation, see ["Working with Intracardiac Patterns"](#target9000065).  REP4611 V6 Risk analysis; user error   * The PTRN check box is selected by default. Clear this to stop using the pattern matching filter.   If this filter is active during Continuous Mapping, points are acquired only if their morphology matches the selected pattern *and* if they meet any other applicable filter.   * Set the weighted correlation threshold by dragging the slider. The value is set according to the arrhythmia type. * You can select a different pattern from the drop-down list. The tab on the Filters Status toolbar is updated to display the new pattern. |

You can now start Continuous Mapping according to the selected filters.

Table 44: Default Filter Settings

| Arrhythmia  (Default Map Name) | CL (Range) | PTRN Threshold (BS) | PTRN Threshold (IC) | Position Stability | LAT Stability | No Pace |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Ventricular Sinus Rhythm  (V SR) | Off | 0.9 | N/A | 4 | 4 | On |
| Premature Ventricular Contraction  (PVC) | Off | 0.97 | N/A | Off | Off | On |
| Ventricular Tachycardia  (VT) | Off | 0.9 | N/A | 4 | 4 | On |
| Ventricular Pacing  (V Pace) | Off | 0.9 | N/A | 4 | 4 | Off |
| Atrial Sinus Rhythm  (A SR) | CL ± 20 | 0.9 | 0.75 | 4 | 4 | On |
| Atrial Flutter  (AFL) | CL ± 10 | 0.9 | 0.75 | 4 | 4 | On |
| Atrial Tachycardia  (AT) | CL ± 20 | 0.9 | 0.75 | 4 | 4 | On |
| Atrial Pacing  (A Pace) | Off | 0.9 | 0.75 | 4 | 4 | Off |

#### Interaction with the PASO™ Module

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_patternMatch\_paso\_7p3\_EN.xml

In general, the Pattern Matching filter in the CONFIDENSE™ Module functions independently from the PASO™ Module. However, if both modules are installed, captured body surface signals (IS/BS PTRN) appear in both the Pattern Bank and the IS List.

* The pattern in the Pattern Bank and the signal in the IS List will have the same number, but different prefixes. For example, the third capture will become BS PTRN 3 and IS 3.
* Numbering is global and cannot be edited.
* The global number increases by 1 each time a signal/pattern is captured.
* The global number increases by 1 any time a signal/pattern is duplicated/copied in either the pattern bank or the IS List. However, a copied IS signal does not appear in the pattern bank and a copied pattern does not appear in the IS List.
* The global number does not change if a signal is deleted from either list.
* Once the signal and pattern appear in their respective banks, they are independent. For example, edits to BS PTRN 3 do not affect IS 3.
* Captures during the setup phase (before moving to the Mapping phase) appear only in the IS List. For example, if there are 3 captures during setup and then one when mapping starts, the IS List has 4 signals, but the pattern list has only 1 signal: BS PTRN 4.

The pattern of interest may be different in the two modules. The default POI in Pattern Matching is set to the last annotation.

REP4611 v6 tab, pattern matching

The two modules use different methods to calculate correlation. Therefore, the same data can yield different results in each module.

### Manual Point Acquisition during Continuous Mapping

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/continuous\_acquisition\_manual\_v6-3\_EN.xml

You can acquire points manually during continuous acquisition.

* The current settings of the Tissue Proximity filter and Respiration Gated filter apply to manual acquisition.
* Manually acquired points are selected in the map, the Point List, and the Selected Point Viewer.

In the Point List, these points are marked as manually acquired.

* The Beat Buffer is available.

## Tissue Proximity Technology

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/TissueProximity\_technology\_overview\_EN.xml

With **Tissue Proximity Technology**, the CARTO™ 3 System uses impedance measurements to determine an electrode's proximity to cardiac tissue. If the electrode is in the proximity of the tissue, the electrode is considered to be touching.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  In order to train the TPI, make sure to move the catheter sufficiently within the entire chamber, until there is no brown frame (band) around the electrode. (See ["Tissue Proximity Indication"](#target9000066),) |

The proximity status can be used for the following features:

* **Tissue Proximity Indication** in the map viewers, Point List, and Selected Point Viewer
* **MEM point acquisition based on Tissue Proximity Filter**

REP4611 v4 tab, 1.1.1, 1.1.2

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteDanger.png](data:image/png;base64...) | **WARNING**  Use your common clinical practice for real-time verification of catheter locations throughout the procedure (inspection of IC signals, direct imaging guidance such as fluoroscopy or ultrasound, etc.). Failure to do so might result in incorrect placement of the catheter. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Tissue Proximity Indication is not available during application of RF energy. |

For more information on TPI, refer to the *CONFIDENSE™ Module User Guide*.

### Supported Catheters

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/TissueProximity\_filteredMEM\_catheters\_V7\_EN.xml

The Tissue Proximity Technology is available for the following Biosense Webster catheters:[[35]](#footnote-35)

* PENTARAY™ NAV Catheters
* LASSO™ NAV Catheter family
* Supported HD Catheters

Tissue Proximity Technology is not available for focal therapeutic catheters.

CONFIDENSE FRS 2001

TPI is supported with the following sockets:

* 20 POLE A
* 20 POLE B
* DECA
* QUAD B

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  If a compatible ACL Tx card is not detected, use 20 POLE A. |

### Determining Proximity Status

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/TissueProximity\_technology\_determineStatus\_v6\_EN.xml

1.1.5 v4

After the catheter visualization appears, it takes the System a few seconds to collect the data needed to determine proximity status. Move the TPI supported catheter during this training period, while making sure that the electrodes do not touch the tissue. Training might take a few seconds.

Training is done when no electrodes show unknown status indication.

Proximity status is determined for each electrode and for each mapping channel.

Choose one of the following options for determining when the mapping channel is considered to be in close proximity:

* The *distal* electrode of the electrode pair is in close proximity.
* *Both* electrodes of the electrode pair are in close proximity.
* *Either* electrode of the electrode pair is in close proximity.

This option is set in the Catheter Setup screen. It applies to all functionality that requires a bipolar mapping channel.

### Tissue Proximity Indication

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/TissueProximity\_indication\_overview\_4-3\_EN.xml

**Tissue Proximity Indication** provides graphical indication of the proximity status.

The proximity status is always unknown in the following situations:

* During TPI training (including following manual reset)
* When electrodes on a TPI-supported catheter are in proximity with each other or with an electrode on a focal ablation catheter
* During application of RF energy

V7 REP9626 1.8.13

* During pacing: The pacing electrodes are always affected. Sometimes electrodes near the pacing electrodes are affected.

#### Resetting Tissue Proximity Indication

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/TissueProximity\_reset\_EN.xml

The following actions trigger an automatic TPI reset:

* A catheter visualization reset
* A body coordinate system (BCS) movement
* An Auto-ID catheter is disconnected and reconnected to a different socket, or a catheter is disconnected and a different catheter is connected to the socket (Reconnecting a catheter to the same socket does not trigger the TPI reset.)

### Tissue Proximity Filter for MEM Point Acquisition

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/TissueProximity\_filteredMeM\_overview\_6p5\_EN.xml

The Tissue Proximity filter applies to both Continuous Mapping and manual MEM point acquisition. The filter is set in the Acquisition Filters tab of the Preferences dialog box. The filter is available whenever a supported catheter is used for mapping.

If the filter is active during manual point acquisition, the System checks the proximity status of each point in a newly-acquired point group. Points acquired when the electrode(s) were in close proximity are automatically marked as accepted (checked) in the Selected Point Viewer.

You can override the automatic selection. Overriding the automatic selection does not affect the point's proximity status.

![tissueProximity_filteredMEM_spv_6p5.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 76: Point Group Acquired with Tissue Proximity Filter

If the Tissue Proximity filter is active, the filter is applied to each beat in the Beat Buffer. The filter is reapplied every time a beat is re-selected.

The Tissue Proximity filter setting of each map is saved with the Study.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  The Internal Points Filter and the Tissue Proximity filter can be used simultaneously. |

See also:

* ["Continuous Mapping Filters"](#target9000067)

## Wavefront Mapping Annotation

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/signalAnotation\_wavefrontMappingIntro\_v8\_EN.xml

The **Wavefront Mapping Annotation** uses an algorithm to evaluate unipolar signals for the greatest negative deflection (‑dV/dt). To use this algorithm, select Wavefront as the Mapping Criterion in the Map Setup screen.

For each paired bipolar signal, both distal and proximal electrodes are evaluated. The mapping annotation is set on the unipolar signal with the greatest negative deflection. The point's location is on the electrode from which the mapping annotation was identified.

If a Wavefront Mapping Annotation is not identified, the system evaluates the point candidate to determine whether to add to the map as a no-LAT point. The point candidate is then added as a no-LAT point if the following conditions are met:

* There was no Wavefront candidate in the previous beat
* The electrode was in close proximity for both current and previous beats (given that the Tissue Proximity filter is active)
* The point candidate passes the position stability and bipolar voltage thresholds

No-LAT points are marked in gray and do not affect the LAT coloring of the map.

The position stability and bipolar voltage thresholds depend on the selected chamber.

* Position stability:
  + Equal to or less than 4 mm when the Chamber of Interest selected in the Map Setup screen is either Left Atrium or Right Atrium
  + Equal to or less than 8 mm when the Chamber of Interest selected in the Map Setup screen is either Left Ventricle or Right Ventricle
* Bipolar voltage:
  + Equal to or less than 0.1 mV when the Chamber of Interest selected in the Map Setup screen is either Left Atrium or Right Atrium
  + Equal to or less than 0.5 mV when the Chamber of Interest selected in the Map Setup screen is either Left Ventricle or Right Ventricle

If the point candidate does not meet all these criteria, it is not added to the map.

REP4611 v4 tab, 1.18.5

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteDanger.png](data:image/png;base64...) | **WARNING**  When Wavefront Mapping Annotation is used, ensure that the intracardiac unipolar signals are clear and without noise. Noise on the signals might cause incorrect calculation of annotation and inaccuracies in the map. |

### Wavefront Annotation Settings

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/signalAnotation\_wavefrontMapping\_setting\_v6p5\_EN.xml

Wavefront Mapping Annotation is selected in the Map Setup screen.

You can set the Bipolar Noise Level on the Wavefront tab of the Preferences dialog box. The setting defines the minimum bipolar voltage taken into consideration for calculating annotations. Voltage values lower than the setting are considered noise and are disregarded. By default, the Bipolar Noise Level is set to 0.03 mV.

![WF_Preferences_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 77: Wavefront Preferences

### Working with Wavefront Annotation

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/signalAnotation\_wavefrontMapping\_working\_8\_EN.xml

If you double-click a bipolar signal of a mapping channel in the Selected Point Viewer, its two corresponding unipolar signals are displayed (Wavefront view). The unipolar signal used for the annotation is marked with a green M. When you double-click the signal again, Wavefront view is closed and the Selected Point Viewer returns to the previous signal display.

![WavefrontSignals.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 78: Bipolar Signal and Corresponding Unipolar Signals

REP4611 A-V4-1.18.4

The point's annotation can be edited manually in the Selected Point Viewer while in Wavefront View by right-clicking the unipolar or bipolar signal and selecting Set LAT.

Points that were acquired using a different mapping criterion can be copied and moved to maps with Wavefront annotation. The annotation of these points is recalculated for Wavefront annotation.

In the Beat Buffer, the recommended beat is indicated. The recommended beat is determined based on the following criteria:

* Multi-electrode mapping catheter: Greatest number of annotated channels and greatest average negative deflection
* Focal catheter: Greatest average negative deflection

![WF_BeatBuffer.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 79: Beat Buffer Showing Recommended Beat (–6) for Wavefront Annotation

## Map Consistency Display

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/consistency\_7p5\_EN.xml

The **Map Consistency Display** is a point filter that identifies points whose Local Activation Time (LAT) values are not consistent with the LAT values of neighboring points. When this filter is applied, the CARTO™ 3 System marks these outliers.

The filter can be run during point acquisition or applied after the EA map is acquired. Only the Active Map is evaluated.

The Map Consistency algorithm is applied in two steps:

* Identifying outliers
* Evaluating these outliers to determine whether they are part of an activation pathway

The identified outliers do not contribute to LAT map coloring, and they are not projected to the map.

![consistency_tags_v7p5.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 80: LAT Outlier Point: Before Applying Algorithm (Left) and After Applying Algorithm

In the Selected Point Viewer, the mapping annotation is marked in gray.

![consistency_point_filtering_SPV.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 81: LAT Outlier in the Selected Point Viewer

Only points that contribute to the LAT map are included in the calculation. The following points are excluded:

* Scar points
* Ablation points
* Floating points
* Location-only points
* Points with no LAT value (Wavefront Mapping Annotation was not identified)

Points with manually-edited LAT values are excluded from the calculation.

In addition, a point must have a minimal number of neighboring points to be included in the calculation. You have the option of marking points that were not included in the calculation due to low point density.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteDanger.png](data:image/png;base64...) | **WARNING**  Before applying energy for ablation, visually verify correctness of the tip position of the mapping catheter in relation to the visualized map.  Use your common clinical practice for real-time verification of catheter locations throughout the procedure (inspection of IC signals, direct imaging guidance such as fluoroscopy or ultrasound, etc.). Failure to do so might result in incorrect placement of the catheter. |

### Map Consistency Settings

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/consistency\_settings\_7\_EN.xml

Figure 82: Map Consistency Settings in the Point Filtering Dialog Box

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Show LAT Outlier Points | When selected, additional Consistency Filter settings are available. After calculation, outliers are displayed.  Clear the selection to hide the markers. |
| Narrow Results | When selected, the calculation uses more stringent parameters. |
| Automatic | Click to start automatic calculation during point acquisition. Click again to stop the calculation.  This is the default calculation option. The other options are available through the button's drop-down menu.  During automatic calculation, the text on the button is orange. |

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/consistency\_review\_7\_EN.xml

You can review the LAT outlier points after the calculation.

To select all outliers, click the Select All button in the Point Filtering dialog box, or select the Select by LAT Outlier option in the Point List right-click menu.

![pointList_select_by_LAT.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 83: Selecting LAT Outliers in the Point List

If you determine that a point is not an outlier, click the point's annotation line in the Selected Point Viewer or edit the mapping annotation. ## Ripple Maps

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/RippleMap\_EN.xml

A **Ripple Map** is a dynamic display of bipolar electrogram data in an electroanatomical map. At each point, the corresponding bipolar electrogram is dynamically displayed as a bar perpendicular to the map surface. The Ripple Window of Interest (WOI) defines the interval of electrogram data to be played in a loop. Display of the bar changes in relation to the timeline of the Ripple WOI. The length of the bar at any given time corresponds to the point's voltage for that time. [[36]](#footnote-36)

After several points have been acquired, the change in the size of each bar produces a ripple-like movement over the map surface.

The Ripple WOI is initially set to the LAT WOI of the Main Map Viewer, but it can be reset manually. The System does not reset the Ripple WOI automatically during the study. The Ripple WOI applies to all displayed maps.

The series of images below demonstrates the dynamic relation between the point's electrogram data and the Ripple bar.

Table 47: Relation between Electrogram and Ripple Bar

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | ![Ripple1.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...) |
| 2 | ![Ripple2.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...) |
| 3 | ![Ripple4.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...) |
| 4 | ![Ripple6.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...) |

Ripple bars are not displayed for floating points or points with ablation, scar, or location-only tags.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteWarning.png](data:image/png;base64...) | **WARNING**  REP4611 D-V4-1.17.1  Before applying energy for ablation, visually verify correctness of the tip position of the mapping catheter in relation to the visualized map.  Use your common clinical practice for real-time verification of catheter locations throughout the procedure (inspection of IC signals, direct imaging guidance such as fluoroscopy or ultrasound, etc.). Failure to do so might result in incorrect placement of the catheter. |

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/RippleMap\_MarkPoints\_v6\_EN.xml

* In the map: a fixed, colored bar on the point
* In the Ripple Viewer: the bipolar channel of the point in the same color as the bar

The Ripple Viewer is a dedicated viewer for the bipolar mapping channels of marked points together with the BS ECG lead II of the point that was acquired first. Mapping channels from different acquisitions and different maps can be displayed. The reference annotation of each marked point is aligned along the red, dotted reference line in the signal viewer. When a marker is selected on the map or the corresponding electrogram is selected in the Ripple Viewer, the point is also selected to enable easy review of the electrograms in the Selected Point Viewer.

The BS ECG lead II cannot be removed from the Ripple Viewer. Other leads cannot be displayed.

# EP Recording System Interface

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/EP RECORDING SYSTEMS INTERFACE\_EN.xml

Interfacing with EP Recording systems includes both hardware and data connectivity. This chapter describes only data connectivity.

The CARTO™ 3 System provides an interface that supports data connectivity with the EP recording system. This interface can be used for a variety of features, based on the CARTO™ 3 System configuration and the functionality that was implemented on the recording system.

Following are the main supported features:

* Send events, such as point acquisition or the end of stimulation, from the CARTO™ 3 System to the recording system
* Send images to the recording system (for example, Map images)
* Send video captures to the recording system (CardioLab® only)
* Import the patient details from the recording system
* Synchronize the view between the two systems
* Synchronize the stimulation routing between the two systems

See also:

* ["EP Recording System Connectivity"](#target9000068)

## Communication States

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/COMMUNICATION STATES\_EN.xml

When using the EP Recording System Interface, there are four possible states of communication between the two systems. Two states refer to physical connection. Two states are set in the software.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Disconnected | There is no communication between the two systems. ![EP_icon_disconnected.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| Standby | Bilateral communication has been established between the two systems. Only patient data (demographics on the recording system) can be imported from the recording system. ![EP_icon_standby.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| Passive Interfaced | When the two systems are in standby, the Passive Interfaced state is activated in the recording system. In this state, both systems are connected and share the same study information. The CARTO™ 3 System sends events to the recording system. The recording system stores these events and snapshots of maps created on the CARTO™ 3 System. ![EP_icon_passiveInterfaced.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| Active Interfaced | The Active Interfaced communication state is activated in the CARTO™ 3 System. In Active Interfaced state, selecting a point on the CARTO™ 3 System selects the corresponding event on the recording system, and vice versa. This is in addition to the functionality of the Passive Interfaced state. ![EP_icon_activeInterfaced.png](data:image/png;base64...) |

In the software applications of both systems, icons are used to indicate the communication states.

## Considerations When Using an EP Recording System with the CARTO™ 3 System

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/EP\_RecordingSystem\_considerations\_c3\_EN.xml

**Patient Data:**

The EP Recording System and the CARTO™ 3 System may support different character sets for entering patient data. When setting up a study:

VSTS: 35936

* Enter the patient data on the EP Recording System, and import the data in the CARTO™ 3 System (from the Study Setup screen).
* Do not leave the Gender field blank.

If it is blank, Gender will be randomly set in the CARTO™ 3 System.

* Only Latin letters, numbers, dashes, and underscores are supported by the CARTO™ 3 System.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  If other characters are used in the EP recording system, the Patient Data will remain blank after importing. If this occurs, edit the data in the EP recording system and import it again. |

**Map Names:**

Use names with less than 30 characters.

**Number of Points:**

Some recording systems limit interfaced points in a Study. If you acquire more than the limit, the additional events may not appear in the recording system.

## End Stimulation Data

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Stimulation\_log\_c3\_EN.xml

When the CARTO™ 3 System detects a stimulation sequence having at least four stimulation pulses on the stimulation train, the CARTO™ 3 System monitors the data and calculates the stimulation parameters. At the end of the stimulation sequence, the following data can be sent to the EP recording system:

* Stimulation electrode pairs
* Pacing Cycle Length (PCL)
* Last five extra-stimulus intervals

The CARTO™ 3 System uses a configurable time interval (Post-stimulation Detection Interval Criterion) to determine the end of stimulation.[[37]](#footnote-37) If a stimulation train is present and no stimulation pulse is detected during the interval, stimulation is considered to be over.

You can disable recording system notification for specific stimulation input (recording system, PIU, or both).

# LAT Velocity Vectors

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/LVV\_v8\_EN.xml

The LAT Velocity Vectors (LVV) feature provides a representation of the activation wave propagation over a substrate map done in sinus rhythm or during pacing. The vectors indicate the LAT movement on the 3D map using the anatomy and point information of an existing LAT map. The vectors appear thicker and shorter for areas of relatively slower conduction (below the user defined threshold), and thinner and longer for areas of relatively normal or fast conduction (above the user-defined threshold).

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  The LAT Velocity Vectors feature was validated on ventricular maps created during sinus rhythm or pacing, and not during tachycardia. Use of the algorithm was validated only in these conditions. |

The LAT velocity vectors are calculated while considering LAT values of the near surroundings of the vector location, and therefore you do not need a full map of the chamber for the vectors to be calculated and displayed. However, the area where the vectors are displayed must be dense enough to allow continuous interpolation of activation between the points. If the map is not dense enough, vectors are not displayed.

The algorithm processes the data as follows:

* For each voxel (3D pixel) on the reconstruction, the system analyzes the LAT values and distances of the projected points that are in the vicinity of the voxel.
* Where enough data exists, the System employs a statistical algorithm to determine the conduction's direction and speed at this region by means of a velocity vector.
* It takes into account LAT values suggestive of wave discontinuation (velocity below 0.1 mm/ms).
* Each vector is assigned with
  + A **direction** — represented by an arrow
  + A **velocity value** — measured in mm/ms, used with the velocity threshold setting
  + A **LAT value** — used for LAT velocity vector clustering and propagation display

LVV is not supported for COHERENT maps.

![LVV_LATMapWithLVV.PNG](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 87: LAT Map with LAT Velocity Vectors

## Viewing LAT Velocity Vectors

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/LVV\_Viewing\_LVV\_EN.xml

To activate the feature and adjust the display settings, right-click the map and select LAT Vector Setup.

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Show Conduction Velocity Vectors | When selected, velocity vectors are displayed.  There may be a slight delay while the vectors are calculated. |
| Static | When selected, the velocity vectors are shown as static. |
| Dynamic | When selected, the velocity vectors are animated. |
| Intensify Slow Vectors | When selected, velocity vectors with speed above the user-defined threshold are dimmed. |
| Vector Clustering | LVV vector clustering is designed to visually emphasize the vectors' main direction and areas with potential discontinuity.  The default value is 4.0.  When vector clustering is changed, the system re-analyzes each vector's LAT values and the distances between the evaluated vector and other vectors in its vicinity. The evaluated vector's location and angle are adjusted towards the center of the analyzed area.  If the vector's distance movement exceeds 4 mm, or orientation change exceeds 45 degrees, the vector will not be displayed on the map for that clustering level.  See ["Sample Map with Lower (Left) and Higher (Right) Vector Clustering"](#target9000069) |
| LAT Velocity Threshold | User-defined threshold for velocity value. Ranges between 0.05 to 1 mm/ms. The default value is 0.20 mm/ms.  Vectors below the selected threshold are thicker and shorter than the ones above the threshold. |
| Do not show this window when re-calculation is required | When enabled, the LAT Vector Setup window will not pop up when changes are made to the map that causes the Velocity Vectors to disappear. |

LAT Velocity Vectors are calculated separately for each map. They can be displayed in either map viewer.

REP14517 A-4

You can display LAT Velocity Vectors for multiple maps in the same map viewer. Vectors in the active map are presented with normal display settings. Vectors in the additional maps are presented in a different color.

![LVV_sample_map_with_intensify_slow_vectors.PNG](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 88: Sample Map with Intensify Slow Vectors Off (Left) and Active (Right)

![LVV_sample_map_with_CompressionLevels.PNG](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 89: Sample Map with Lower (Left) and Higher (Right) Vector Clustering

## Recalculating Velocity Vectors

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/LVV\_RecalculatingVelocityVectors\_EN.xml

REP14517 D-4

If the map changes after the LAT Velocity Vectors have been calculated, either from a change to the FAM reconstruction or after adding, removing, or editing contributing points, the velocity vectors are no longer valid and are removed from the map. The LAT Vector Setup window appears so you can recalculate the LAT Velocity Vectors and display them again.

To recalculate the LAT Velocity Vectors, select the Show Conduction Velocity Vectors checkbox.

If you don't want the window to appear when recalculation is necessary, select the Do not show this window when re-calculation is required checkbox.

## Propagation Maps with LAT Velocity Vectors

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/LVV\_PropagationMapsWithLVV\_EN.xml

Each of the Velocity Vectors is associated with a LAT value (this is only used by the algorithm) that represents the time that the wave passes in the vicinity of the vector . To further investigate the activation sequence, the LVV can be played as a propagation wave on top of displayed vectors.

During playback, the propagation wave is white over the vectors, which are displayed in gray.

![LVV_SamplePropagationMap.PNG](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 90: Images of LAT Velocity Vectors in a Sample Propagation Map

## Different Types of Velocity Vectors

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/LVV\_InteractionCoherentMapping\_EN.xml

The LVV feature provides a local solution for calculating velocities, whereas COHERENT maps provide a global fit solution.

The two types of vectors cannot be active in the same map viewer simultaneously. However, they can be displayed side by side, with one in the Main Map Viewer and the other in the Additional Map Viewer.

If you change the vector type for a map, the newly selected vector type is displayed, and the former is removed. The vector type is indicated under the color scale bar.

![LVV_LAT_ColorScale.PNG](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 91: LAT Color Scale Bar with COHERENT Vectors (Left) and LAT Vectors (right)

**Display behavior with COHERENT map:**

* LVV cannot be displayed over a COHERENT map.
* If a COHERENT map is displayed, LVV are removed, and the LVV option is disabled in the map viewer menu.
* After returning from a COHERENT map, the LVV will automatically re-appear (unless COHERENT vectors are displayed).

# HD Catheter Support

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/HD\_catheters\_intro\_8p3\_EN.xml

## Overview

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/HD\_catheters\_overview\_8p3\_EN.xml

The CARTO™ 3 system supports connection of diagnostic catheters with more than 20 mapping electrodes to theCARTO™ 3 System. These catheters are referred to as HD catheters.[[38]](#footnote-38) These catheters connect to the CARTO™ 3 PIU via the Signal Processing Unit (SPU).

HD Catheters can be used for standard functionality, including mapping (both FAM and EA) and pacing. Bipolar channels between splines are not supported.

The HD Catheters can be used with most modules, with the following exceptions:

* Pairing visualization of HD catheters with the CARTO VIZIGO™ Sheath is not supported.
* CARTOFINDER™ Module is not supported with OPTRELL™ Catheters (part of the CARTO PRIME™ Module).
* Multipolar Mapping is not supported with the OCTARAY™ Catheter (part of the CARTO ELEVATE™ Module[[39]](#footnote-39)).

REP13141 D-2, D-3

REP11406 D-62, D-63, A-96, D-97, D-98

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  While mapping and before applying energy for ablation, check the catheter tip position in relation to the map. Use your common clinical practice for real-time verification of catheter locations throughout the procedure (inspection of IC signals, direct imaging guidance such as fluoroscopy or ultrasound, etc.). |

* Refer to the *Instructions for Use* (IFU) of the catheter before using this catheter with the CARTO™ 3 System.
* Refer to the *CARTO™ Signal Processing Unit (SPU) Hardware Instructions for Use* for details on connecting the SPU to the PIU.

### OCTARAY™ Catheters

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/HD\_catheters\_OCTARAY\_8p3\_EN.xml

OCTARAY™ Catheters are multi-spline diagnostic catheters, available and supported in the following configurations (both D and F curve):

Table 48: OCTARAY™ Catheter Definitions

| Spline Length | # of Splines/Electrodes | Electrode Spacing |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2 cm | 8/51 | 3-3-3-3-3 mm |
| 2 cm | 8/51 | 2-5-2-5-2 mm |
| 1.5 cm | 8/51 | 2-2-2-2-2 mm |

The CARTO™ System supports 48 unipolar ECG channels and 40 adjacent bipolar channels along the splines.

### OPTRELL™ 48 Mapping Catheter

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/OPTRELL\_8p0\_Catheter\_Specifications\_EN.xml

The OPTRELL™ 48 Mapping Catheter is a triple-loop diagnostic catheter, with 2.4 × 2.4 spacing (in spline and across spline). It is available and supported in both D-F and F-J curves.

The CARTO™ System supports 48 unipolar ECG channels and 42 adjacent bipolar channels along the splines.

### OPTRELL™ 36 Mapping Catheters

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/OPTRELL\_36\_Catheter\_Specifications\_7p5\_EN.xml

The OPTRELL™ 36 Mapping Catheter is a triple-loop diagnostic catheter, with 2.4 × 2.4 spacing (in spline and across spline). It is available and supported in both D-F and F-J curves.

The CARTO™ System supports 36 unipolar ECG channels and 30 adjacent bipolar channels.

## CARTO™ Signal Processing Unit (SPU)

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/HD\_catheters\_SPU\_intro\_EN.xml

The SPU is used for connecting HD catheters to the PIU.

![SPU_holder_veiw.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 92: SPU in Holder

For details regarding connection and care of the SPU, refer to the *CARTO™ Signal Processing Unit (SPU) Hardware Instructions for Use*.

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/HD\_Catheters\_connecting\_to\_SPU\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Before connecting the catheter to the SPU, ensure that the SPU is connected and ready for use. The LED is green and the green connection icon appears on the System status bar. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Connect the catheter to the SPU extension cable connector.![OCTARAY_SPU_Extension_Cable.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 2. | Connect the SPU extension cable to the SPU. ![OCTARAY_SPU_and_Extension_Cable.png](data:image/png;base64...) |

### Status Indication Panel

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/SPU\_status\_indication\_v7p5\_EN.xml

When you create a new study with the SPU connected to the PIU, the SPU Status icon appears on the System Status Panel.

![SPU_Status_Panel_with_Fully_Connected_SPU_Highlighted_EN.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 93: Sample System Status Panel with Fully Connected SPU

The SPU automatically performs several start-up and validation functions, including electrical and external device communication status, cable connectivity, noise filtering, and temperature monitoring. If issues are detected, the SPU Status icon and/or the System messages provide indication.

Table 49: SPU Status Indication

| Status | Icon | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Connected | ![SPU_Icon_Indicator_Con_Con1_EN.png](data:image/png;base64...) | The SPU is fully connected and ready for use. This indicates the following:   * The branched cable is connected to the Power Supply. * The 20 POLE interface cables are connected to the SPU, and to the 20 POLE A and 20 POLE B sockets of the PIU. * A functioning Ethernet cable is connected to the SPU and the PIU. * The SPU and the PIU are synchronized. |
| Disconnected or Error | ![SPU_Icon_Indicator_Dis_Dis1_EN.png](data:image/png;base64...) | There is an error or a lack of communication between the SPU and the Workstation. |
| REP9626: V7 SPU 1.6.4, 1.8.2, 1.10.1  No Sync  or  Self test | ![SPU_Icon_Indicator_Dis_Con1_EN.png](data:image/png;base64...) | The SPU and the PIU are connected via the Ethernet cable, but they are not synchronized or when the SPU is first started, during the self-test. |
| Error 401  Map points cannot be acquired  This situation may be related to a buildup of electrostatic discharge. | ![SPU_Icon_Indicator_Dis_Con1_EN.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Ensure that the relative humidity in the operating room is between 25% and 75%. The System will return to a synchronized state and the System Sync icon turns green. |
| Error 401  Map points cannot be acquired  In rare situations:   * Voltage interruptions may occur that cut the power to the SPU * Out of range voltage may be supplied that results in the SPU automatically turning itself off   As a result, the SPU LED indicators turn off. | ![SPU_Icon_Indicator_Dis_Dis1_EN.png](data:image/png;base64...) | No action required.  Once the nominal voltage is restored, the SPU essential performance is restored and is safe to use. |

### SPU Troubleshooting

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/SPU\_troubleshooting\_IFU-cug\_7p5\_EN.xml

If an electrode is defective or in the sheath, there is no indication on the non-ACL electrodes. To determine if there is an issue with an electrode:

* Display all the unipolar signals in an ECG viewer.
* Inspect the signal quality of each electrode.
  + If the electrode is defective, the signal shown is random noise.
  + If the electrode is functional, but still in the sheath, the signal is flat.

## CARTO™ 3 System Interface with HD Catheters

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/HD\_catheters\_interface\_EN.xml

### Catheter Setup Screen

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/HD\_catheters\_CatheterSetup\_EN.xml

When an HD catheter is connected to the SPU and/or defined in the template, both 20A and 20B sockets are displayed under the catheter image in the Catheter Setup screen. The label for the socket is automatically set to SPU.

You can change the text of the label for the socket, as well as other visualization settings for the HD catheter. For example, in the Color Per Spline section, set the spline color for the HD catheter to a single color for all splines, or choose to display a different color for each spline.

To change the orientation of the catheter image, center-click the image and rotate.

![C3_v7p5_optrell_Catheter_Setup_screen.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 95: Catheter Setup Screen with OPTRELL™ 48 Mapping Catheter Connected

If the HD catheter is connected to the SPU and/or defined in the template, both 20A and 20B sockets are displayed under the catheter image.

If the 20 POLE cables only partially connect the SPU to the PIU, the following is displayed on the Catheter Setup screen:

* The connected socket is blue
* The SPU image is shadowed
* No HD catheter image is displayed
* Socket label SPU is shadowed

### HD Catheter Visualization

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/HD\_catheters\_visualization\_EN.xml

For optimal visualization of HD catheters:

* Ensure that the chest patches are positioned properly. They must be placed an adequate distance apart, and they must encircle the heart without overlapping. Use the Location Setup screen to assist in placing the patches in the outer part of the highlighted area.
* If possible, build the matrix with the catheter that will be used for mapping. Using a different catheter may affect visualization.

### Out of Sheath Indication for HD Catheters

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/SPU\_HD\_catheter\_out-of-sheath\_EN.xml

In situations where the catheter is partially out of the sheath but visualization is not possible, the tip of the shaft visualization is highlighted. This can occur when the catheter is first exiting the sheath, or in situations where conditions prevent visualization (for example, inadequate matrix). The indication disappears when the splines are visualized.

![in_sheath_7p5.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 96: HD Catheter with Out of Sheath Indication

This indication is on by default. It can be turned off and on in the Catheter Setup screen using the Show Out of Sheath Indication checkbox.

The catheter is available for mapping and building the matrix when the S1 electrode is out of the sheath.

### TRUEref™ Electrode Functionality

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/hd\_trueRef\_functionality\_EN.xml

REP9626: V7 1.8.8

Supported HD catheters have a special TRUEref™ electrode that can be used as a unipolar reference. This option is off by default when an HD catheter is connected. Unipolar channels of other catheters are not affected and continue to function according to the WCT or IC System setting.

To switch to using TRUEref™ as the unipolar reference, click the Enable TRUEref™ unipolar reference check box in the Filters Configuration dialog box.

![Trueref_Electrode_c7p3.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 97: Filters Configuration Dialog Box

You can change the unipolar reference for the HD Catheter in the same map, with no limitations.

If you start a CARTOFINDER™ recording session when TRUEref™ unipolar reference is active, the algorithm input will be based on the TRUEref™ unipolar signals.

If the TRUEref™ electrode is nonfunctional:

* If the HD Catheter is the mapping catheter, an error message is displayed, and no data can be acquired.
* If the HD Catheter is not the mapping catheter, an alert is displayed. The System will be able to acquire data with other catheters.

### SPU Smoothing Filter

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/SPU\_P3\_Smoothing\_Filter\_EN.xml

To select a Low Pass Filter that is optimal for ECG signals, select the Enable SPU smoothing filter from the Filter Configuration dialog box.

![Smoothing_filter_c7p3.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 98: Filter Configuration Dialog Box

This setting is saved with the Study and can also be saved to a template.

### TPI Functionality

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/HD\_catheters\_TPI\_EN.xml

Tissue Proximity Indication (TPI) is active by default.

Table 50: TPI Functionality with Different HD Catheters

| Catheter | Functionality |
| --- | --- |
| OCTARAY™ Catheters | REP9626: V7 1.8.10  When the TPI filter is active:   * TPI functionality, including status indication and filtered point acquisition, is not available for the following electrodes:   B5, B6, C5, C6, E5, E6, F5, F6, G5, G6, H5, and H6   * If Distal Electrode or Either Electrode is selected, the CARTO™ 3 System does not acquire points for the following bipolar channels:   B5-6, C5-6, E5-6, F5-6, G5-6, and H5-6   * If Both Electrodes is selected, the CARTO™ 3 System does not acquire points for the following bipolar channels:   B4-B5, B5-6, C4-C5, C5-6, E4-E5, E5-6, F4-F5, F5-6, G4-G5, G5-6, H4-H5, and H5-6 |
| OPTRELL™ 48 Catheters | REP9626: V7 1.8.10 (From OCTARAY™)  TPI impedance calculation is not available for the following electrodes, as they are not directly connected to the PIU:   * A3, B3, C3, D3, E3, F3 * A6, B6, C6, D6, E6, F6   A *Virtual TPI* calculation is performed for these unsupported electrodes based on the TPI status of neighboring electrodes:  When both neighboring electrodes along the spline of any of the unsupported electrodes are in close proximity (positive TPI status), the unsupported electrode is considered positive as well. For example, if both electrodes A2 and A4 are positive, A3 is also considered to be positive.  Virtual TPI is automatically available when TPI is active and does not require any additional user configuration. Virtual TPI affects all TPI functionality, including status indication and filtered point acquisition. |
| OPTRELL™ 36 Catheters | TPI is available for all 36 electrodes. |

### Local Conduction Vectors (LCV)

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/OPTRELL\_LCV\_7p5\_EN.xml

OPTRELL™ Catheters only: Local Conduction Vectors (LCV) tool displays real-time information on conduction direction, propagation velocity, and low voltage areas for the OPTRELL™ Catheter 3D image.

The LCV feature is only available when mapping criterion is set to WaveFront or Late Annotation, and an LCV-supported catheter is set and configured as the mapping catheter.

To access the LCV settings, right-click the map and select Catheter Vector Setup. The Catheter Conduction Vector Setup window appears.

![Catheter_Conduction_Vector_Setup_1.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 99: Catheter Conduction Vector Setup

Table 51: Catheter Conduction Setup

| Setting | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Catheter Conduction Velocity Vectors | Visualization of local conduction vectors is enabled by default. Clear the checkbox to hide the vectors. This can be set for each map viewer.  The vectors appear thicker and shorter for areas of relative slow conduction, and thinner and longer for areas of relative fast/normal conduction. |
| Color | LAT (default): reflects the range of colors for the local conduction under the catheter surface from earliest to latest.  Solid: removes color, making arrows appear gray. |
| Size | needs FRS ref  Vector size scale values range from 1 to 11. Default is 5. |
| Bipolar scar threshold (mV) | A dark gray disk appears on top of the electrode when the catheter is in the proximity of a low voltage area and only while the electrode has positive tissue touch indication with TPI.  ![gray%20discs.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)  needs FRS ref  The default threshold is set to 0.15 mV.   |  |  | | --- | --- | | ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  This is not the same as the Bipolar scar threshold in the Scar Settings preferences. | |

There are 3 distinct velocity types: **slow conduction** area, **fast/normal conduction** area, **no conduction** (scar area):

![C3_V7p5_vectors_and_scar_indication.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 100: Vector Velocity Indication

When visualization of local conduction vectors is enabled, you can display vectors for the catheter shadow for a point. Right-click the map viewer background and select Show Catheter > LCV. The vectors appear on top of the catheter shadow, and the vectors appear less saturated.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  When choosing Show Catheter and editing a point, velocity vectors will be recalculated retrospectively based on the new parameters. |

Velocity vectors on the catheter shadow will change when point values are changed by:

* Reference annotation
* WOI borders
* VWOI borders
* Mapping annotation
* Late Boundary

You can display LCV on both map viewers, and on every map coloring type. They are also displayed on the following:

* Propagation/ Ripple display
* Glass mode
* Anatomical structure
* Merged images

## Pacing with HD Catheters

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/SPU\_ifu-cug\_pacing\_v7p5\_EN.xml

**Through the CARTO™ 3 System:**

Bipolar pacing on SPU electrodes is supported, except in the following situations:

* When the SPU is powered off.
* During PIU power-up initialization.
* When the PIU is powered off.
* When there is no communication with the Workstation.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE   * Do not disconnect the SPU during pacing. * If you disconnect the SPU and connect a different catheter to 20 POLE A, you must select the 20 POLE A channel for pacing, even if pacing was previously routed to SPU channels. The reverse also applies. |

**Through the Recording System:**

The SPU enables pacing through the Recording System. Pacing through the Recording System is also available:

* When the SPU is off.
* During PIU power-up initialization.

Each supported catheter has specific electrodes that are not available.

For additional information, refer to the relevant Catheter Support Module's Instructions for Use.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteCaution.png](data:image/png;base64...) | *CAUTION*  REP9626: V7 SPU 1.4.7, 1.4.8   * Do not pace through or connect channel 20 POLE A electrodes 17-20 to the Recording System.   REP9626: V7 SPU 1.4.9   * Do not connect channel 20 POLE A electrode 19 to the Recording System because you can lose the synchronization or sensing between the signals.   REP9626: V7 SPU 1.4.10   * Selecting channel 20 POLE A electrodes 19-20 (SPU Sync Signal) as the sensing channel in the pace stimulator, might result in non-synchronized pacing. |
