# Pace Mapping Software (PASO™) Module

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/paso\_title\_IFU\_v6p5\_EN.xml

## PASO™ Module Overview

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/paso\_workingWith\_c3\_v3\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  The performance of the PASO™ Module to help pace mapping for catheter ablation of arrhythmias in the Right Ventricle has not been demonstrated. |

**The Pace Mapping Software (PASO™) Module** (PASO™ Module) can be used for correlating 12-lead ECG captured during the patient's induced or spontaneous arrhythmia to other induced signals (IS) or to the signal captured while pace mapping. While induced arrhythmia signals are not associated with any point or map, the pace-mapped signal (PM) is always associated with an electroanatomical point. The System calculates a correlation index that helps to compare between pace mapped points and between different induced signals. You can use the automatically-assigned best match or choose your own.[[40]](#footnote-40)

REP4611 A-V1-1.32.2

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteDanger.png](data:image/png;base64...) | **WARNING**  Be sure to carefully examine the signal set before selecting it for use in the PASO™ Module because the selected set will be used as a template for data calculation and display until the study is completed. If an incorrect set is selected, the basis for the calculations (and therefore the results) will not be optimal for the given data. |

### Installation and Hardware Configuration

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Installation\_PaSo\_c3\_v4\_EN.xml

A Biosense Webster representative is responsible for the installation and initial configuration of the module.

This module requires the same hardware and cabling as the standard CARTO™ 3 System.

## PASO™ Module Concepts

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/paso\_concepts\_c3\_v3\_EN.xml

### Induced Signals

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/paso\_induced\_signals\_c3\_v4\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  A focal ablation catheter does not have to be connected in order to acquire induced signals (IS). |

Induced or spontaneous signals can be captured at any point after completion of the Study Setup screen. During the Setup phase, the PASO™ Viewer opens in the Setup Help pane. You can hide the PASO™ Viewer to review the Setup Help.

For each captured IS, 62.5 seconds for all body surface leads are recorded:

* 2.5 seconds after acquisition are used for correlation calculation
* 60 seconds before acquisition serve as an ECG buffer

The following IS information is saved with each study:

* Name
* Reference IS status
* Median cycle length (CL) as calculated on the Lead II signal
* Time the signal was recorded
* 62.5 seconds of recorded signals for all body surface leads

Each IS recording is available for all the maps in the current study.

### Morphology of Interest

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/paso\_MOI\_c3\_v4\_EN.xml

After recording an IS, the PASO™ Module automatically sets the morphology of interest (MOI) at a fixed width of ± 150 ms. For the first IS, the MOI is set around the first peak of the last 2.5 seconds of the Lead II channel. If the first peak on the leading channel is less than 150 ms from the beginning of the data recording, the second peak is used.

For each additional IS, the system sets the MOI where the best correlation within the last 2.5 seconds, calculated with existing IS references, was indicated.

The MOI is used when calculating correlation with other signals (both IS and PM). If the MOI does not fully enclose the signal of interest of the arrhythmia, you can edit the MOI. You can scroll back up to 60 seconds so as to select the desired complex.

![paso_MOI_c3_v3.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 102: Morphology of Interest

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Ensure that the MOI does not include irrelevant data that might interfere with the correlation calculation of that signal. |

### Reference IS

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/paso\_referenceIS\_c3\_v6\_EN.xml

If an IS has a unique morphology it is designated a Reference IS. Only Reference ISs are used for comparison with PM signals.

The first IS you record is marked as a Reference IS by default. Each time you record a new IS, it is compared to every Reference IS in the study, according to the correlation parameters.

* If the new IS differs significantly from every Reference IS (low correlation value), it is designated as a Reference IS.
* If the new IS corresponds to a Reference IS (high correlation value), the IS remains in the list of available signals, but it is not used in IS or PM correlation calculations.

You can change the Reference IS designation manually.

If an IS contains more than one MOI, you can create a copy of the IS. Each version can be designated as a Reference IS, with a different MOI.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  When the Reference IS designation is changed, subsequent IS MOIs are not restored to their automatically calculated values and remain the same as before.  You can recalculate the values from the PASO™ Preferences. However, this recalculation removes all user editing. |

### PM Signals

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/paso\_pm\_signals\_c3\_v4-1\_EN.xml

When a PM is captured, 10 seconds are recorded for all body surface leads:

* 2.5 seconds after acquisition
* 7.5 seconds before acquisition

To view the full 10-second PM history, set the Correlation field to None.

An active interval of 2.5 seconds is used for calculating correlation. By default, the active interval is the last 2.5 seconds of the PM; however, you can move the active interval to any part of the captured signal.

REP4611 A-V1-1.32.2

Pace mapped (PM) signals are always associated with a selected point in a CARTO™ 3 Map. If no point is selected, the PM will be associated with the last acquired point. If a point group is selected (Group Selection mode), the PM will be associated with the Active Point.

REP4611 A-V1-1.32.2

Each point can have only one associated PM.

### Comparing IS and PM Signals

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/paso\_IS-PM\_signals\_compare\_c3\_v4\_EN.xml

REP4611 A-V1-1.32.5

Each PM is automatically compared to each reference IS, to find the signal set with the highest correlation. A PM signal is considered Matched only if the level of correlation is equal to or greater than the threshold values entered in the PASO™ Preferences.

The correlation can be viewed in the PASO™ Viewer, the Point List, and the Correlation Map.

During the procedure, if a new reference IS with a closer correlation to mapped PM signals is acquired, the system automatically updates the information for the relevant points.

For any PM, the matched Reference IS having the highest correlation value is considered the best IS and is associated with that PM.

You can manually change the Reference IS that is associated with the PM (Current IS) to another Matched Reference IS. Subsequent matched ISs will not override the user designation.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE   * Noisy BS signals might result in misleading automatic association. In this case, check the associated signals for accuracy. * When correlation between IS and PM is calculated, the System takes into account everything that is within the MOI. If the pacing spike is within the MOI, correlation value is reduced.   To ensure accurate correlation calculation, adjust the MOI so that it does not include the pacing spike. |

You can export the correlation values to a text file.

See also:

* [" PASO™ Module User Interface"](#target9000070)

### PASO™ Module Preferences

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/paso\_preferences\_c3\_v6p5\_EN.xml

The PASO™ Preferences control the IS and PM correlation threshold parameters.

PM correlation can be displayed according to all Reference ISs or according to one Reference IS. This filter affects the display in the PASO™ Viewer, Main Map Viewer, Correlation Map and Point List.

You can configure the size of PASO™ Tags and change the default PM and IS name prefix.

PM points can be automatically tagged as location only at the time of PM capture.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  Changing the PM Correlation Threshold during a study updates the current list of matched signals but does not override manual selection of Current IS, as long as the current IS correlation is above the new thresholds. It does not change ISs MOI or override IS reference designations. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteCaution.png](data:image/png;base64...) | *CAUTION*  Changing the IS Correlation Threshold during a study causes a recalculation of all the data. This removes any editing, including MOI. The IS that was recorded first is set as Reference IS, and all other References ISs are set again. Current IS designation is overridden.  If you reset the IS Correlation Threshold during a study, review each IS before continuing. |

## PASO™ Module User Interface

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/paso\_tools\_c3\_v4\_EN.xml

The PASO™ Module interface has the following tools for viewing and comparing IS and PM signals:

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| PASO™ Viewer | Displays numerical correlation values in the PM List or IS List (Acquisition tab) and the correlation table (Correlation tab).  Signals are displayed in the PASO™ Signal Viewer. |
| PASO™ Tags and Labels | Indicate whether at least one IS matches this PM (orange) or if it has no matched IS (green). A label with the name of the Current IS can be displayed as well as the matched IS. |
| Correlation Map | Provides a visual tool for viewing points according to the correlation values of the PM signals associated to the points. All PM points are included in the correlation map (matched and unmatched). |
| Point List | Marks points that are associated with a PM signal, even if there is no matched IS.   * The PASO™ column is available with any map. Points associated with a matched PM display the Current IS and its IS-PM correlation value. Points associated with an unmatched PM display N/A. * The Correlation column is available with the PASO™ Correlation Map. All points that are associated with a PM display the best IS-PM correlation (matched and unmatched). |

## General Workflow

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/paso\_generalWorkflow\_c3\_v4\_EN.xml

The PASO™ Module workflow is divided into the following general phases:

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Preferences | Set the correlation threshold parameters. It is recommended to set the preferences before capturing signals. Preferences can be saved in a user-defined template. |
| IS Capture | BS-ECG signals representing the patient's arrhythmia are recorded. If necessary, the arrhythmia can be induced.  Induced signals can be recorded in the Setup phase of the Study, as soon as the Study Setup screen is completed and all the BS-ECG patches have been attached to the patient and connected.  Each unique morphology has a designated Reference IS. |
| Pace Mapping | A pace mapped signal (PM) is associated with a selected point in a CARTO™ 3 Map. PMs can be recorded during the Mapping phase, when there is at least one IS and one electroanatomical point.  The recorded PM is automatically compared to each Reference IS, to find the signal set with the highest correlation. |
| Comparison | PASO™ Tools such as the PASO™ Viewer and the **Correlation Map** display correlation numerically and visually. |

You can record an IS after recording a PM signal, and you can switch between the Acquisition tab and the Comparison tab at any time after completing the Setup phase.

# CARTO™ 3 System with CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Module

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/smarttouch\_title\_EN.xml

## CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Module Overview

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Smarttouch\_Overview\_EN.xml

**The CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Module** enables the CARTO™ 3 System to display the magnitude and direction of the force applied to intracardiac tissues when used with a compatible force catheter. The CARTO™ 3 System displays the force data in real time. It also records the data for review.[[41]](#footnote-41)

When the CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Module is installed, the CARTO™ 3 System can display force readings when a compatible force catheter is connected.

REP4611 A-V2-11.1.1.2

For accurate force measurement, all four electrodes must be outside the sheath. If the electrodes are covered, the Dashboard displays **SH**.

Before force readings can be displayed, it is necessary to zero the force reading.[[42]](#footnote-42) Perform this zeroing when the following conditions are met:

* The catheter is within chamber of interest
* No force is applied on the catheter tip
* No other catheter is in close proximity

In some instances, it may also be necessary to zero the force reading during the procedure.

After zeroing, the user can set a force range with upper and lower force thresholds. These thresholds are used in the following ways:

* Display of force magnitude includes indication of whether the force is below, within, or above the thresholds.
* The percentage of force which is above the lower threshold during a user-defined time interval can be displayed.
* During FAM data acquisition, it is possible to block acquisition of volume if the force is above the upper threshold.

An optional maximum force threshold can be set. If the force reading is over this threshold, the Main Map Viewer background will flash.

1.1.5 V3

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  If force readings become unavailable, the indication of force above the maximum threshold behaves as follows:   * If force was above the maximum threshold before entering the error state, the background continues to blink, even if the force is reduced. * If force was below the maximum threshold before entering the error state, the background will not blink, even if force is increased. |

The CARTO™ 3 System measures the force magnitude and direction (from tip axial line). The force measurements can be displayed in several ways:

* Vector indication on the Catheter icon
* Force Maps
* Real-time line graph displaying force over time
* Dashboard, displaying angle of contact and force magnitude (in grams)
* Force magnitude (in grams) in the Frc column of the Point List

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE   * If force readings might be inaccurate or another catheter is in proximity: an alert message appears, force readings on the Dashboard are displayed in gray, and the force graph is colored white. * If force readings cannot be received or another catheter is in close proximity: an alert message appears and force data is not displayed.   In either case, resolve the issue according to the directions in the alert message to enable force measurement. You can also continue the study without force data. |

### Compatible Catheters

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/smartTouch\_catheter\_about\_EN.xml

Catheters with CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Catheter Technology (referred to as force catheters) contain an embedded force sensor, which collects data about the force magnitude and the angle of the force between the catheter tip and the intracardiac tissue. As with other mapping catheters, the force catheter contains a magnetic sensor in its tip that continuously collects location data as the catheter is moved inside the heart.

Consult your Biosense Webster representative regarding the availability of compatible force catheters in your country. For more information about using these catheters, refer to the *Instructions for Use* of the catheter.

### CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Module Hardware

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Hardware\_EN.xml

The CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Module uses the standard CARTO™ 3 System hardware.

The module is designed for use with a compatible force catheter and its accessories.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  If Alert 84 appears frequently, it may be necessary to use a thicker mattress or to add spacers to the Location Pad Holder. Contact Biosense Webster Service and Support. |

### Installation

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/SWInstallation\_EN.xml

Biosense Webster service personnel are responsible for installing the CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Module software on the workstation. After installation, users start the application in the same way as the standard CARTO™ 3 System Software. The CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Module options become available when the module is installed, although force readings are only available when a compatible force catheter is connected to the MAP socket of the PIU.

## Working with the CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Module

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/SW\_smarttouch\_v6\_EN.xml

When the CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Module is active, several settings must be configured. These settings are defined in the Force tab of the Preferences dialog box. These preferences are also used to zero the force reading and to customize the display options.

After setting the preferences, you can create EA maps and FAM maps. The force data is also available in Review mode.

### Force Preferences

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/CFPreferences\_6p5\_EN.xml

The Force tab of Preferences dialog box is used for the following:

* Defining the calculation method and time intervals
* Setting the force thresholds
* Customizing the display
* Zeroing the force reading

### Display Options in the CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Module

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/DisplayOptions\_smatrtouch\_v6\_EN.xml

The CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Module includes several options for displaying force data.

Table 52: Overview of the Display Options

| Display Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Force Dashboard | The Force Dashboard displays the current force (in grams), the current angle, and the percentage of force which is above the lower threshold during a user-defined time interval.  If the force readings might be inaccurate due to high force, the Dashboard displays **HI**.  Show/Hide the Dashboard in the Force preferences. Alternatively, right-click the Main Map Viewer and select Toolbars. Then select or clear Force. |
| Graphs Viewer | The Graphs Viewer displays the force magnitude over time. The current force value is displayed above the graph.  During ablation, the Graphs Viewer also displays power, temperature, and impedance graphs. |
| Vector | The Vector, indicating the current force direction, is colored according to the defined force thresholds: gray (below the lower threshold), green (within the thresholds) and red (above the upper threshold).  Show/Hide the Vector in the Force preferences. |
| Force Map | When the Force map coloring type is selected, CARTO™ 3 Maps are colored according to force data measured at the point's annotation.  Show/Hide Force map coloring from the Map right-click menu. |
| Frc column in the Point List | Force magnitude (in grams) for acquired points is listed in the Frc column of the Point List. |

Force readings are also available in the Selected Point Viewer and the Readings Dashboard.

### Force Catheter Visualization

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/smartTouch\_catheter\_visualization\_EN.xml

A compatible force catheter has four tip electrodes and two shaft electrodes. You can choose to view the full catheter (tip and shaft) or just the tip.

This option can be set in the Catheter Setup screen at the beginning of the study. You can also change the setting during the study by right-clicking the catheter visualization.

### EA Maps with Force Data

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Dxmapping\_ea\_cf\_EN.xml

A compatible force catheter can be used with the CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Module to create electroanatomical CARTO™ 3 Maps. In addition to the standard map coloring types (LAT, Voltage, etc.), a Force Map can be displayed. The force magnitude for each point can be displayed in the Point List.

If the force calculation method or the calculation interval is changed, the force values are updated in the Point List and the Main Map Viewer.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  This also applies to FAM Maps with EA points. |

### Excluding High Force in FAM Reconstructions

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/DXmapping\_FAM\_force\_EN.xml

FAM volume can be excluded from the FAM reconstruction if it is acquired while the force is above the higher Force Threshold value.

To use this filter, select Exclude When Force Is Above Higher Threshold in the Acquisition Filters tab of the Preferences dialog box.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  If Respiration Gating is enabled, FAM acquisition can also be filtered according to the Respiration Gating Threshold. In this case, FAM data will only be acquired when the data is below the upper force threshold **and** within the Respiration Gating Threshold. |

## Performing a Study with Force Data

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/performing\_CF\_Study\_EN.xml

When performing a study with the CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ module, be aware of the following options and differences.

**Preparations:**

1.3.1.15; REP4611 D-V3-1.3.1.15

* Refer to the catheter's *Instructions for Use* for details.
* Set the force preferences before beginning the study.

**Mapping:**

* Use display options such as the Dashboard, Vector, and force line graph to ensure that optimal force is applied before acquiring points.
* Use the Point List or Force Map to identify points above or below the thresholds, and delete them if desired.
* Standard FAM Acquisition is the same as in a CARTO™ 3 Study.
* During FAM acquisition with Force Filter, no location data (FAM volume) is acquired if the force is above the upper threshold.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  If force readings become unavailable, the indication of force above the maximum threshold behaves as follows:   * If force was above the maximum threshold before entering the error state, the background continues to blink, even if the force is reduced. * If force was below the maximum threshold before entering the error state, the background will not blink, even if force is increased. |

**Ablation:**

* Use the Graphs Viewer to view force, power, impedance, and temperature graphs in real time.
* When a point is acquired during ablation, that point in time is marked on the force graph.

**Reviewing ablation sessions:**

* The force line graph for an ablation session can be reviewed.
* Points acquired during ablation are linked to the Graphs Viewer.

For details, refer to the *CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Module User Guide*.

# CARTO™ 3 System with QDOT MICRO™ Module

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/QDOT\_title\_EN.xml

## Overview

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_intro\_8p1\_EN.xml

The **QDOT MICRO™ Module** provides real-time display of the QDOT MICRO™ Catheter tip surface temperature and microelectrode signals in the CARTO™ 3 System.  [[43]](#footnote-43)

QDOT MICRO™ Module features are available only with the QDOT MICRO™ Catheter. The QDOT MICRO™ Catheter has a force sensor,[[44]](#footnote-44) 6 thermocouples, and 3 microelectrodes.

When the QDOT MICRO™ Catheter is used with a compatible RF generator, support is available for both QMODE™ and QMODE+™ ablation modes. (See ["QMODE+™ Functionality in the CARTO™ 3 System"](#target9000071).)

Before using this module:

* Read the *Instructions for Use* of the QDOT MICRO™ Catheter.
* Read the *Instructions for Use* of the TX *eco* cable.
* Read the *Instructions for Use* of the supported RF generator.

In this document, the high power, short duration mode is referred to as QMODE+™. The name in the generator may be different.

### Supported RF Generators

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_gen\_v7\_EN.xml

Consult your Biosense Webster representative regarding the availability of compatible devices in your country.

If the connected generator is not compatible with the QDOT MICRO™ Catheter, an error message appears, and the CARTO™ 3 System will not display ablation indication including the following:

* Mapping functionality is disabled
* Temperature distribution on the tip of the catheter
* Temperature indication in the Catheter View
* Ablation point tagging
* VISITAG™ Site and Grid Cell creation
* Readings Dashboard ablation mode

#### Generator Connection Status

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/QMODEplus\_GeneratorModes\_v8\_EN.xml

When the CARTO™ 3 System is connected to a supported RF generator, an icon indicates the generator's connectivity status.

![generator_connection_status.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 103: System Status Panel when connected to a supported RF Generator

While connected to an nGEN™ RF Generator with QMODE+™ functionality, an additional icon indicates the active mode of operation.

![qmode+_system_indication_c3v7p2.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 104: System Status Panel when Connected to an RF Generator with QMODE+™ Functionality

Table 53: System Status Icons

| Icon | Description |
| --- | --- |
| ![QMODE+.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...) | The nGEN™ RF Generator is currently set to QMODE+™ ablation mode.  For details, see ["QMODE+™ Functionality in the CARTO™ 3 System"](#target9000072). |
| ![QMODE.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...) | The nGEN™ RF Generator is currently set to QMODE™ ablation mode. |

If no communication with a generator is detected, a System message appears.

|  |  |

| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  The generator does not recognize that the CARTO™ 3 System is connected until the CARTO™ 3 Study is initialized. |

## TX eco Cable

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_dongle\_V7\_EN.xml

The TX *eco* Cable is used with a TX *eco* EXT Cable (extension cable) to connect a QDOT MICRO™ Catheter to the Patient Interface Unit (PIU) of the CARTO™ 3 System.

![qdot_dongle.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 105: TX eco Cable

The TX *eco* Cable transfers data from the QDOT MICRO™ Catheter to the CARTO™ 3 System and the RF generator. The information transferred is listed below.

* Force readings
* Location
* ECG signals, such as IC signals, from the 3 microelectrodes in the tip of the catheter
* Temperature measurements from the 6 thermocouples in the tip of the catheter. (Only the highest temperature is transferred to the RF generator.)

The TX *eco* Cable has two sockets: one for the TX *eco* EXT Cable, and one for catheters that connect to the MAP socket.

![qdot_dongle_socketView_annotated.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 106: Socket for TX eco EXT Cable

### TX eco Cable Warnings

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_TXWarnings\_v8\_EN.xml

* Before using the TX *eco* Cable, read the *Instructions for Use* for the QDOT MICRO™ Catheter.
* The TX *eco* Cable must be at least 1 m (39.4 inches) away from the location pad of the CARTO™ 3 System during the procedure.

REP4611 v6 tab, dongle, general, warmup

* The TX *eco* Cable must be connected to the PIU for a 5-minute warm-up period before use to ensure accurate temperature readings. After the warm-up period, the temperature readings stabilize. If the cable is used before the end of the warm-up period, a temperature drift might occur.

### Connecting the Cable

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_dongle\_connections\_V7\_EN.xml

![qdot_dongle_piu_connection.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 107: TX eco Cable Connected to the PIU

|  |  |  |  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1. | Connect the TX *eco* Cable to the CARTO™ 3 System PIU:   |  |  | | --- | --- | | 1.1 | Connect the red connector to the MAP socket on the PIU and wait until the green LED on the cable stops blinking. | | 1.2 | Connect the yellow connector to the QUAD B socket on the PIU.  You can also connect to the QUAD A or DECA socket. However, QUAD B is recommended for better signal quality on the microelectrode channels.  After the cable is connected to the PIU, allow approximately 10 seconds for the cable to initialize and for the CARTO™ 3 System to recognize it. | |
| 2. | Connect the catheter to one end of the TX *eco* EXT Cable (extension cable). |
| 3. | Connect the other end of the TX *eco* EXT Cable to the socket at the end of the TX *eco* Cable. |

### Disconnecting the Cable

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_DisconnectingTX\_v7\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | Disconnect the red connector from the CARTO™ 3 System PIU. |
| 2. | Disconnect the yellow connector from the PIU. |
| 3. | After disconnecting the cable, wait 10 seconds before reconnecting the cable to the PIU. |

You can switch between a QDOT MICRO™ Catheter and a standard focal ablation catheter without disconnecting the TX *eco* Cable.

REP4611 D-V6 - 1.2.2, D-V6 - 1.2.3, REP9626\_34:1.8 V7 QMODE

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE   * If you are using a QDOT MICRO™ Catheter, both connectors must be connected to the PIU for normal functionality. Do not leave one cable connected and the other disconnected. * If you are using a standard catheter and want to use the secondary socket (for example, QUAD B), disconnect both connectors and then reconnect the red connector before using the QUAD B socket. |

### LED Status

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_dongle\_instructions\_BIT\_v7\_EN.xml

REP4611 D-V6 - 1.1.2, D-V6 - 1.1.3, D-V6 - 1.1.5, D-V6 - 1.1.13, D-V6 - 1.1.14, D-V6 - 1.1.15, D-V6 - 1.1.16, D-V6 - 1.1.17, D-V6 - 1.1.18, D-V6 - 1.1.21, D-V6- 1.3.1, D-V6- 1.3.2

After the cable is properly connected to the CARTO™ 3 System PIU and to a QDOT MICRO™ Catheter, the cable performs a Built-In Test (BIT). The status of the cable is indicated by an LED.

![qdot_dongle_annotated.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 108: LED

Table 54: LED Status

| LED | Cable Status | Troubleshooting |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Green: slow blink | BIT in progress | N/A |
| Green: solid | Ready for use | N/A |
| Red: fast blink | Error, BIT failed | The temperatures are not sent to the CARTO™ 3 System or RF generator.   1. Disconnect the catheter from the TX *eco* Cable and the TX *eco* Cable from the PIU. 2. Reconnect the TX *eco* Cable to the PIU and the catheter to the TX *eco* Cable.   If the problem persists, replace the TX *eco* Cable. |

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_mapping\_EN.xml

When a QDOT MICRO™ Catheter is used, the Micro Bipolar option is added to the map right-click menu. Select this option to view a voltage map based on the bipolar voltages of the micro-electrodes.

![map_menu_microBi.PNG](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 111: Map Types Available with QDOT MICRO™ Catheter

The highest of the three micro-electrodes bipolar peak-to-peak voltages is the one selected for each point in this type of map. In the Selected Point Viewer, the label µBi marks the channel that provided the value.

Figure 112: µBi Label in the Selected Point Viewer

Micro-electrodes cannot be used for electro-anatomical (EA) point acquisition.

### Displaying QDOT MICRO™ Catheter Signals

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_multielectrode\_EN.xml

The QDOT MICRO™ Catheter has 3 microelectrodes in the catheter tip. You can display IC signals from these microelectrodes in the ECG viewers by selecting the corresponding channels.

* The standard channels are labeled MAP M1 through MAP M4.
* Bipolar microelectrode channels are labeled MAP u1-u2, MAP u2-u3, and MAP u3-u1.
* Unipolar microelectrode channels are labeled MAP u1, MAP u2, and MAP u3.

![ECG_Viewers_micro.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 113: Signal Display in ECG Viewers

### Special Microelectrode Considerations

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_microelectrodes\_v6p5\_EN.xml

The locations of the microelectrodes relative to the catheter deflection colors are displayed as follows:

* µ1 is located between the red and green arcs
* µ2 is located in front of the blue arc
* µ3 is located between the red and yellow arcs

![qdot_microelectrode_locations.PNG](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 114: Location of Microelectrodes on Catheter Tip

The microelectrodes are visible on the 3D visualization of the catheter.

Recorded and Mapping Channels:

* Each microelectrode uses resources that equal the resources used by 3 electrode channels.
* The 3 bipolar microelectrode channels are selected by default in the Recorded and Mapping Channels dialog box.
* Adding or removing a microelectrode channel increases or decreases the total number of open channels by 3.

REP9626: V7 QMODE 1.9

REP4611 D-V6-1.17.1, D-V6-1.17.1

Filters:

When a QDOT MICRO™ Catheter is connected, QDOT options are added to the Filter Configuration window.

![qdot_filter_config.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 115: Filter Configuration Window with QDOT Options

QDOT options include two ECG filter settings - one for regular MAP electrodes (M1-M4: bipolar and unipolar) and one for the micro-electrodes (µElectrodes µ1-µ3: bipolar and unipolar). This allows you to set different filters on each type of electrode.

Default filters for all connectors:

* Unipolar:
  + High Pass: 2 hz
  + Low Pass: 240 hz
* Bipolar:
  + High Pass: 16 hz
  + Low Pass: 500 hz

Choosing higher High Pass filters such as 30 hz, 70 hz, 100 hz might be taken in consideration if needed.

You can save these setting to a template. Using the template will result in the following behavior:

* MAP settings will be applied to all catheters connected to the MAP socket, not just the QDOT MICRO™ Catheter.
* The micro-electrode filters will be applied and visible once the QDOT MICRO™ Catheter is connected to the CARTO™ 3 System and the Filter Configuration window is displayed.

Pacing:

REP9242\_1.2: 4.1.3 Pacing Limitation

* Pacing through the microelectrodes is limited to 6 mA.

# CARTO VISITAG™ Module

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/visiTag\_module\_title\_6p5\_EN.xml

## CARTO VISITAG™ Module Overview

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/visiTag\_featureOverview\_v8p3\_EN.xml

The CARTO VISITAG™ Module includes the following features:

* CARTO VISITAG™ Module
* The Stability+ Algorithm
* QMODE+™ Functionality in the CARTO™ 3 System

The CARTO VISITAG™ Module provides the ability to continuously store, track, and quantify ablation catheter positions along with the electrophysiological parameters acquired during RF applications, according to user preferences.[[45]](#footnote-45) Visual Indication can be presented as either VISITAG™ Sites or Grid Cells. Both types of visual indication can be projected onto a map reconstruction. These visual indications do not interfere with the standard manner of acquiring points or applying ABL tags during application of RF energy.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteWarning.png](data:image/png;base64...) | **WARNING**  The CARTO VISITAG™ Module provides access to data collected during the application of RF energy. The data provided by the CARTO VISITAG™ module does not indicate the effectiveness of RF energy application.  REP4611 D-V4-1.22.2, D-V4-1.22.5  During an RF session, monitor data such as catheter location, Graphs Viewer, and Readings Dashboard.  The Cumulative Data Popup window provides access to data collected during the application of RF energy when the VISITAG™ Site or Grid Cell met all the criteria set in the Preferences dialog. |

### VISITAG™ Sites

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/VisiTag\_tagOverview\_V8\_EN.xml

Ablation catheter positions that meet the user-defined thresholds for filters such as Location Stability and Force Over Time data[[46]](#footnote-46) are grouped into areas according to their spatial stability.

A VISITAG™ Site (also called VISITAG™ Location) represents a single ablation site, accumulating consecutive catheter positions recorded during application of RF energy when the ablation catheter has met all filter parameters. Catheter movement to a new location (or a location that was visited in a previous session) creates a new VISITAG™ Site. These sites appear as an element similar to ABL Tags.

After the creation of a VISITAG™ Site, if the condition of any of the filter criteria is no longer met, the site breaks and the system starts tracking the conditions for creation of a new VISITAG™ Site.

There are two VISITAG™ stability algorithms available to adjust for movement due to respiration.

* With the standard Gated (ACCURESP™) algorithm, catheter positions are adjusted and brought to the end expiration phase location during the creation of the VISITAG™ Site tag. The final location of the VISITAG™ Site tag will be the average of all catheter locations collected and brought to end expiration.
* The Stability+ algorithm provides a continuous analysis of catheter movement. This can be used instead of the Gated (ACCURESP™) algorithm, which depends on ACCURESP™ Respiration Gating Training. For more information about Stability+ see ["Stability+"](#target9000073).

QMODE+™ Functionality provides a unique type of VISITAG™ Site, which indicates very high power, short duration ablation sessions. These sessions create a distinct QMODE+™ Site which indicates the ablation location. QMODE+™ Sites appear differently depending on which stability algorithm is used. For more information regarding QMODE+™ Sites functionality with each of the algorithms, see ["Catheter Stability Status "](#target9000074).

### Grid Cells

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/VisiTag\_gridCells\_v8\_EN.xml

A Grid Cell represents an accumulation of positions recorded during all the ablation sessions that took place within a 1 mm cube. The Grid Cells appear as small spheres. If Minimum Time is not defined in the current preset, Grid Cells appear after 1 second. Otherwise, they appear when they have met the defined stability time (see ["CARTO VISITAG™ Module Preferences"](#target9000075)). Grid cells do not include data from QMODE+™ ablation sessions.

### CARTO VISITAG™ Module Installation

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/visiTag\_installation\_v4-3\_EN.xml

For certain focal ablation RF generators, a communication link option is available to enable display of RF parameters in the CARTO™ 3 System. For these generators, a Global Port is required. If you do not have this option, contact the Biosense Webster Service and Support Department.

Consult your Biosense Webster representative regarding the availability of compatible devices in your country.

## CARTO VISITAG™ Module Preferences

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/VISITAG\_preferences\_v8p3\_EN.xml

Several user-defined preferences can be applied when the CARTO VISITAG™ Module is active. These settings are accessed through the VISITAG tab of the Preferences dialog box (Tools> Preferences).

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  During a Study: Settings that require force data are only available if a supported force catheter is used and a compatible module is installed.  In Review Mode: Settings that require force data are available if a compatible module is installed. |

The image and table below describe VISITAG Preferences when using the Stability+ algorithm. When Gated is selected, the Preferences dialog box is slightly different.

![preferences_visitag_stability_no_index.jpg](data:image/jpeg;base64...)

Figure 119: VISITAG Preferences when the Stability+ Algorithm is Selected

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| VISITAG™ Stability Algorithm selected | REP14517 A-7, A-8  This statement indicates which VISITAG Stability Algorithm is currently selected.  The default setting is Stability+. To use the Gated stability algorithm instead, click Tools > VISITAG™ Stability Algorithm selected > Gated (ACCURESP™). |
| Presets | Presets provide a way to save multiple filter configurations with meaningful names. Presets are defined in the Preferences dialog box, and selected in the VISITAG Toolbar.  The preset cannot be changed during application of RF energy. |
| Respiration Adjustment | To increase accuracy, catheter positions can be adjusted to minimize the respiration effect. Click to use respiration adjustment.  When the Stability+ Algorithm is active, this option is selected by default.  With the standard Gated stability algorithm, catheter positions are adjusted and brought to the end expiration phase location during the creation of the VISITAG™ Site tag. The final location of the VISITAG™ Site tag will be the average of all catheter locations collected and brought to end expiration. This option is disabled if Respiration Gating Training has not been performed.  VISITAG™ Sites that were created before Respiration Gating Training do not have the required data for adjusting their positions to end expiration. Therefore, these sites will not be displayed once this option is selected. To see these VISITAG™ Sites uncheck Respiration Adjustment.   |  |  | | --- | --- | | ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  For scenarios of long apnea, hyper ventilation, and jet ventilation, the performance of the Stability+ Algorithm provides optimal performance with no additional adjustment for respiration required. Uncheck the Respiration Adjustment preference option. | |
| Location Stability | User-defined thresholds for distance change and time define an area in which a catheter is considered to be stable. This filter is available at any time during the study.  The catheter is stable if consecutive positions remain within the threshold for distance from the location mean (Maximum Distance Change), in either direction, for longer than the user-defined time threshold (Minimum Time). Only if these 2 conditions are met will a VISITAG™ Site tag be created. If these conditions are not maintained, the VISITAG™ Site will break.  If the maximum distance change filter is set when using the Gated stability algorithm, and then the stability algorithm is changed to Stability+, the Maximum Distance Change filter is automatically set by the system to 3mm. If the stability algorithm is then changed back to Gated, the Maximum Distance Change is changed back by the system to the original user defined preference. |
| Force Over Time | A VISITAG™ Site is created only if a user-defined minimum force (Minimum Force) was applied at the site over a user-defined minimum percentage of time. If this condition is not met, no VISITAG™ Site will be created. If this condition is not maintained, the VISITAG™ Site will break.  This filter can be used in combination with the Location Stability filter. |
| Show Site Break Indication | This option is only available if you are using Stability+.  If this option is enabled, when a site is broken a white halo appears, indicating the site was broken during ablation. The halo displays for 3 seconds during ablation or when ablation has ended.  A-6  The Stability+ algorithm has a higher instability detection rate than the Gated algorithm, resulting in earlier and more frequent site breakages. |

### Using Global or Multiple Presets

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/visiTag\_presets\_apply\_4.3\_EN.xml

A single preset can be applied globally to all RF sessions, or you can use different presets for each RF application session throughout the study.

To select the way presets are applied, right-click the Presets button on the VISITAG™ Toolbar.

Table 58: Description of VISITAG™ Preset Options

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Global Preset | * The current preset is used for the entire study. * If you change the preset, all previous sessions are updated accordingly. * If the current preset is edited, the changes are applied to all VISITAG™ data. |
| Multiple Presets | * The current preset is used for the next RF session. * If you change the preset, it will be applied to future sessions. Previous sessions are not affected. * If a preset is edited, the changes are applied to all ablation sessions that use the preset. * When selected, the Preset button on the VISITAG™ Toolbar has an orange border. |

When Grid Cell Coloring is set to Total Time, Grid Cells can be filtered according to a user-defined minimum time threshold. Grid Cells with accumulated time below the threshold are not displayed.

To set the time threshold, click and move the Color Scale Bar slider to the desired threshold value.

### VISITAG™ Indication in the Graphs Viewer

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/visiTag\_graphsViewer\_7p3\_EN.xml

When a VISITAG™ Site is selected in a map viewer, an indication appears on the Graphs Viewer representing the start and end of the respective stable site.

### RF Sessions List

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/visiTag\_sessions\_6p5\_EN.xml

The RF Sessions List of the Graphs Viewer displays the preset that was applied during each session.

* If the Global Preset option is active, the preset cannot be modified from the RF Sessions List.
* If the Multiple Presets option is active, you can manually modify the preset for a specific session.

To select the VISITAG™ sites that are associated with a session, right-click the session and click Select VISITAG™ Locations

### Displaying Cumulative Data

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/visiTag\_cummulativeData\_7p3\_EN.xml

Additional data that is acquired during ablation, such as impedance and temperature, can be displayed for each Grid Cell or VISITAG™ Site. The data is displayed in the Cumulative Data Popup window when you double-click a Grid Cell or VISITAG™ Site to select it in one of the map viewers.[[48]](#footnote-48)

If you click a different VISITAG™ Site, the data is updated. The window remains open until you click the x in the upper right corner.

The Cumulative Data Popup window provides access to data collected during the application of RF energy when the VISITAG™ Site or Grid Cell met all the criteria set in the Preferences dialog.

Table 61: Cumulative Data Description

| Value | Description |
| --- | --- |
| ID | An identifier for the selected object |
| Time (sec) | Time stamp |
| Power (W) | Range of values  Median value (VISITAG™ Sites only) |
| Temperature (°C) | Range of values  Median value (VISITAG™ Sites only) |
| Impedance (Ω) | Baseline value to lowest recorded value; impedance drop |
| Force (g) | Range of values  Average value (VISITAG™ Sites only) |
| Force Time Integral (FTI) (g\*sec) | Current value |

If the RF Generator becomes disconnected during ablation, a comment appears in the Cumulative Data popup: \* Lost communication – data might be partial. A red asterisk (\*) appears next to the values requiring input from the generator (power, temperature, impedance).

If the values are not available for another reason and for a defined period of time, N/A is displayed.

### VISITAG™ Sites and Projections

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/visiTag\_overlapping\_7p3\_EN.xml

If you click a VISITAG™ Site or Grid Cell and projections are on, both the location/cell and the projection are selected. This is indicated by a black and orange ring.

REP9626 1.22.1

Multiple VISITAG™ Sites can project to the same place on the map, creating one projection tag for more than one VISITAG™ Site.

If a VISITAG™ Site overlaps another by more than 75% and its projection does not fully overlap another projection:

* When selected, both the VISITAG™ Site and its projection have a double black and orange ring.

Clicking either the projection or the VISITAG™ Site selects both.

* You can double-click either the VISITAG™ Site or its projection to view the Cumulative Data window with data for the selected site.

![visitags_projection_75pc.png](data:image/png;base64...)

If a VISITAG™ Site overlaps another by more than 75% and its projection fully overlaps another projection:

* Only the VISITAG™ Site can be selected. It has a double black and orange ring. There is no indication on the projection.
* The projection coloring matches the stronger VISITAG™ Site.
* You cannot double-click the projection to view the Cumulative Data window.

![visitags_projection_100pc.png](data:image/png;base64...)

If two VISITAG™ Sites overlap another by less than 75% but their projections fully overlap:

* The VISITAG™ Sites can be selected, but there is no indication on the projection.
* The projection coloring matches the stronger VISITAG™ Site.
* You cannot click the projection to select it.
* You cannot double-click the projection to view the Cumulative Data window.

### Distance Measurement Tool

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/visiTag\_distance\_V7-P3\_EN.xml

The Distance Measurement Tool provides two ways to measure the distance.

* **Catheter**: Measures the distance between the real-time ablation catheter tip position and the last VISITAG™ Site that was created. You can also display the distance between the catheter tip and a selected point or VISITAG™ Site within 15 mm.
* **Standard**: Measures the distance between any two selected end points (VISITAG™ Sites, Grid Cells, points, tagged points, or locations on the map). This option is not available during FAM acquisition.

The distance measured is the direct line connecting the center of the object or the catheter tip (not the distance on the map or tissue).

You can activate the tool from the Map Menu or the VISITAG™ Toolbar.

* From the Map, select Distance Measurement - Catheter or Distance Measurement - Standard
* From the toolbar, select Catheter or Standard

In **Standard** mode, click two endpoints. The distance is displayed.

![visitag_siteToSite.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 122: Distance between Two VISITAG™ Site

In **Catheter** mode, the distance between the catheter tip and the last created VISITAG™ Site is automatically displayed. If a new site is created, it becomes the endpoint.

* To measure the distance between the catheter tip and a different VISITAG™ Site, click the site.
* To display the distance between the catheter tip and an EA point, press SHIFT while clicking the point. The point is not selected (map viewers, Point List, or Selected Point Viewer).

In both cases, the object must be within 15 mm of the catheter tip.

![visitag_siteToTip_4-3.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 123: Distance Real-Time Catheter Position and Last VISITAG™ Site

### Automatic Distance Tool

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/visiTag\_AutoDistance\_V8\_EN.xml

The Automatic Distance Tool automatically highlights the Euclidian distance between the centers of two VISITAG™ sites, where the distance is larger than the predefined thresholds. A distance of 12 millimeters or higher between one VISITAG™ site and the next means that these sites belong to separate clusters. Therefore, these distances are not highlighted.

This feature applies to all valid VISITAG™ sites of focal ablations and does *not* apply to the white and gray VISITAG™ sites and the non-focal VISITAG™ tags.

![VisiTag_AutoDistanceTool.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 124: Two VISITAG™ Sites and the Highlighted Distance Between Them

Each section represents one millimeter of Euclidian distance between the VISITAG™ site centers. To see the accurate numeric representation of the distance, click the highlighted distance between the VISITAG™ sites.

![VisiTag_AutoDistanceTool_and_number.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 125: Numeric Representation of the Distance

The distances are recalculated when one or more of the following occurs:

* The VISITAG™ data is recalculated
* VISITAG™ sites are shown or hidden
* There is a change in the map source selection display filter in the VISITAG™ toolbar
* A new VISITAG™ site is created

## Stability+

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/VisitagStabilityAlgorithm\_EN.xml

To set the VISITAG™ stability algorithm, go to Tools > VISITAG™ Stability Algorithm selected. The default is Stability+.

When Stability+ is selected, the System continuously analyzes the overall movement of the catheter, separating the movement due to respiration from the actual catheter movement. This continuous analysis reduces the time for initial indication of stability and the appearance of VISITAG™ Sites.

Claim: support for QMODE+

This algorithm supports all focal ablation types including short duration sessions (such as QMODE+ sessions).

**Considerations when using Stability+:**

* The algorithm requires a training period. This happens automatically in the background either during or after the catheter visualization process while a magnetic sensor-based catheter is in the mapping zone. If a focal ablation catheter is connected while the training is in process, an alert message is displayed until the training period is finished.
* This algorithm measures the distance from the center of the site to the catheter tip location. If the catheter moves beyond a distance of 3 mm, the site breaks. If a site breaks during ablation due to instability, and Show Site Break Indication is enabled (see ["CARTO VISITAG™ Module Preferences"](#target9000076)), a ring (halo) is displayed for up to 3 seconds around the broken site tag. This indication disappears at the end of the ablation session or when a new site appears.

![VisitagStabilityAlgorithm_halo.png](data:image/png;base64...)

* For faster tag appearance, ensure catheter stability before starting ablation.
* For optimal algorithm indication, use point by point ablation sessions.

REP14517 D-5, D-5.1

Claim: velocity

* In the Gated (ACCURESP™) stability algorithm, the maximum distance is based on the standard deviation, whereas in the new Stability+ algorithm, the maximum distance change is 3 mm. This makes Stability+ more sensitive to movement.
* If you switch between algorithms during a study, the System recalculates and presents the VISITAG™ Sites according to the current algorithm. It is not recommended to switch between algorithms during a study. To avoid VISITAG™ Site displacement and their data changes due to new calculation, switch between stability algorithms only in retrospective study.
* If the Maximum Distance Change filter is set using the Gated (ACCURESP™) stability algorithm, and then the stability algorithm is changed to Stability+, the Maximum Distance Change filter is set by the system to 3 mm.
* Stability+ cannot be used on a loaded study which was performed on CARTO™ 3 version 7.5 or earlier.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  If you switch to the Gated (ACCURESP™) site stability algorithm and Respiration Gating Training was not performed before the start of the ablation session, VISITAG™ Sites might not appear. For more information see ["CARTO VISITAG™ Module Preferences"](#target9000077). |

### Stability Indication with Stability+

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Visitag\_stabilityIndication\_comp\_v8\_EN.xml

REP14517 A-10

When Stability+ is active, additional indication appears in the Readings Dashboard if data is not available during ablation.

### White Site Tags

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/visitag\_display\_WhiteTags\_v8\_EN.xml

Claim: white site tags

If Stability+ is selected, white site tags indicate the first site in an ablation session where the catheter is stable, and before the Minimum Time threshold had been met and the VISITAG™ coloring is applied.

A white site tag might appear if one or more of the following occurs:

* The system defined Maximum Distance Change of 3 mm was met.
* No other VISITAG™ Sites were created during the current ablation session.
* The Minimum Time filter was not met.
* The Force Over Time filter was met (if active).

Once the Minimum Time filter was achieved, the tag is colored according to the current settings. If the Minimum Time threshold is not met, it remains white.

When the White Site Tags On/Off option is selected in the VISITAG Toolbar, the white tags remain after the end of the ablation session. If this option is not selected, white tags disappear up to 5 seconds after the end of the ablation session.

![VISITAG_StbPlus_WhiteTags.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 126: A Sample Map with White VISITAG™ Sites (Left) and White QMODE+™ Sites (Right)

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  If Tag Index and White Site Tags On/Off are both selected, the Tag Index becomes available after a white tag is displayed. |

### Site Tag Coloring

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/visitag\_SiteTagColoring\_EN.xml

REP14517 A-10.1

Different coloring methods can be used simultaneously for QMODE+ Sites and VISITAG™ Sites. These are set in separate drop down lists in the Site Tag Coloring On/Off option ![visiTagToolbar_blue_locationColor_enabled.png](data:image/png;base64...) in the VISITAG toolbar.

To enable or disable site tag coloring for both VISITAG™ Sites and QMODE+ Sites together, select the Site Tag Coloring On/Off icon.

To disable coloring for either VISITAG™ Sites or QMODE+ Sites, go to Site Tag Coloring On/Off, select the option for which you want to disable coloring, and then select None.

![visitag_coloring.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 127: Site Tag Coloring Options for the QMODE+ Sites (None - to Disable Coloring)

### Comparison Between the VISITAG Stability Algorithms

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Visitag\_algorithm\_compare\_v8\_EN.xml

REP14517 A-7

| **Gated (ACCURESP™)** | **Stability+** |
| --- | --- |
| Respiration Gating Training is required. | Training occurs automatically in the background after catheter visualization when a sensor based (TAS) catheter is in the mapping zone. Respiration Gating Training is not required for VISITAG™ Site creation. Training is still required for other features such as gated point acquisition and Exclude Respiration Data for FAM reconstructions. |
| The Maximum Distance Change filter is set by the user and is based on the standard deviation. | The Maximum Distance Change filter set by the System to 3 mm. It is absolute, and not based on standard deviation. This provides a higher detection rate of instability. The Maximum Distance Change filter is not displayed in the Preferences dialog box. |
| There can be a delay of more than one respiration cycle before VISITAG™ Site can be displayed. | Claim: time for tag appearance  If the catheter is stable before the ablation session begins, Stability+ takes up to 2 seconds to optimize and display CARTO VISITAG™ Module information after an ablation session begins. Otherwise, it can take up to 5 seconds for the VISITAG™ Site(s) to display. |
| No indication of the first site if the Minimum Time threshold was not met. | White tags indicate the first site in an ablation session where the catheter is stable, but before the Minimum Time threshold was met and the VISITAG™ or QMODE+ Site Coloring was applied (see ["White Site Tags"](#target9000078)). |
| Minimum Time threshold range: 3 s to 120 s | Minimum Time threshold   * For VISITAG™ Sites, range between 3s to 120 s * For QMODE+ Sites can be either 2 s, 2.5 s, 3 s, or 3.5 s |
| No indication of a break in the site. | A ring (halo) appears around a broken site until a new site appears or the ablation session ends. |
| Grid cells can be displayed even in areas where the catheter was not in the region for the minimum time (Excluded Grid On/Off ). | Excluded grid cells cannot be displayed. |
| The QMODE+ tab is active and the VISITAG™ tab doesn't apply to QMODE+ ablation sessions. | VISITAG™ Preferences apply to all focal VISITAG™ Sites, including QMODE+ Sites. The QMODE+ tab is removed from the Preferences dialog box. |
| QMODE+ Sites are colored red unless the Color according to checkbox in the QMODE+ Preferences dialog box is selected.  In this case, it is colored according to the status, detailed in the QMODE+ Preferences dialog box. For more information see ["QMODE+™ Sites with the Gated Stability Algorithm"](#target9000079) | QMODE+ Sites are colored according to QMODE+ Site coloring settings set in the VISITAG Toolbar, |

These two algorithms have different valid ranges for Maximum Distance Change threshold and Minimum Time threshold. If a preset was set without a QMODE+Minimum Time value, the value will be set to 2 seconds.

## QMODE+™ Functionality in the CARTO™ 3 System

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qmode\_interface\_v8\_EN.xml

QMODE+™ Functionality provides a unique type of VISITAG™ Site, which indicates very high power, short duration ablation sessions. These sessions create a distinct QMODE+™ Site which indicates the ablation location. QMODE+™ Sites are marked with a textured surface.

QMODE+ ablation sessions do not generate grid information.

### Catheter Stability Status

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qdot\_stability-indication\_V8\_EN.xml

When using the Gated stability algorithm. during QMODE+™ ablation sessions, the stability status indication shows catheter stability based on the Location Stability filter parameters associated with the active CARTO VISITAG™ Module preset. If the Location Stability filter is not defined, N/A is displayed.

#### QMODE+ Sites with Stability+

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/visiTag\_QmodePlus\_EN.xml

Claim: QMODE+ based on VISITAG

For QMODE+ ablation sessions with Stability+, the standard VISITAG Preferences apply. This is indicated in the VISITAG Preferences tab. The QMODE+ tab is removed. QMODE+ Sites are colored according to QMODE+ coloring settings set in the VISITAG Toolbar. For more information see ["Site Tag Coloring"](#target9000080) .

The selected VISITAG preset appears in the ablation session list.

#### QMODE+™ Sites with the Gated Stability Algorithm

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/qmode\_visitag\_functionality\_v8p1\_EN.xml

When the Gated stability algorithm is selected, a QMODE+ Session Review Indicator can be added to a QMODE+™ Site. This indicator is used to show whether the total energy applied for that site has passed the user-defined threshold. The indicator is enabled in the QMODE+ tab of the Preferences dialog box.

When using the Gated stability algorithm QMODE+™ Sites are displayed only at the end of each QMODE+™ ablation session.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteGeneric.png](data:image/png;base64...) | NOTE  The QMODE+ Session Review Indicator functionality should be used in a retrospective manner after PVI isolation. |

Table 64: Description of QMODE+ Preferences

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| The Respiration Adjustment setting in the active VISITAG™ Preset is ON/OFF. | Presents the current status of the Respiration Adjustment filter, set in the VISITAG tab of the Preferences dialog box.  The Respiration Adjustment setting can be used to minimize the respiration effect on the position of the QMODE+™ Sites. Catheter positions are adjusted and brought to the end-expiration phase location during the creation of the QMODE+™ Sites.   * If the Respiration Adjustment is set to ON: The location of the QMODE+™ Site will be either the closest position to the end-expiration phase, or the first catheter location during the RF session, depending on the timing of the end-expiration phase with respect to the RF session. If the tag is on the first catheter location, it is marked as Resp. Adjusted: No in the Cumulative Data window. * If the Respiration Adjustment is set to OFF: The location of the QMODE+™ Site will be at the first catheter location during the RF session. |
| Color According to | When selected, QMODE+™ Site coloring provides an indicator of the total energy applied. These QMODE+™ Sites will be displayed with a colored, textured surface, according to the total energy applied.  When not selected, or if the option is selected but all parameters are 0, standard QMODE+™ Site coloring is applied. |
| Total energy applied | REP 9626: V7 QMODE 2.0; REP4611 A-V2-11.2.1.4  The Total energy applied threshold allows you to see where less RF energy was applied during an ablation session.  The Total energy applied indicator does not indicate lesion creation or the quality of the lesion. Use your clinical judgement with indicator use and interpretation.  The default value is 0. |

Less RF energy can be due to one of the following:

* The ablation session was of a shorter duration
* Less power was applied during an ablation due to power titration

If the Total energy applied threshold is changed, the new value is retrospectively applied to the coloring of existing QMODE+™ Sites.

The settings are saved with the study.

If a new template is created, the System saves the Total energy applied value and clears the Color According to check box. If you select the check box again, the System resets the Total energy applied value to 0.

When using the Gated stability algorithm, QMODE+™ ablation sessions are marked as Focal, QMODE+ in the Type column of the RF sessions list. The active Preset for a QMODE+™ session is always the QMODE+ preset. This preset cannot be changed or replaced.

# CARTO VIZIGO™ Software

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/VIZIGO\_module\_EN.xml

## CARTO VIZIGO™ Software Overview

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/VIZIGO\_overview\_EN.xml

The **CARTO VIZIGO™ Software** allows visualization of the CARTO VIZIGO™ Bi-Directional Guiding Sheath in the CARTO™ 3 System when paired with a sensor-based focal ablation catheter.[[49]](#footnote-49) Sheath visualization aids in understanding the spatial relationship between the catheter and the sheath during navigation in the cardiac chambers.

![VIZIGO_overview.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Sheath visualization is made possible by four electrodes located along the sheath curve. Sheath electrodes cannot be used for point acquisition, signal display, or pacing.

An enhanced visualization mode, *paired visualization*, is available when a sensor-based focal ablation catheter with 6 electrodes is inserted in the sheath. This paired mode aligns the sensor-based visualization of the catheter with the ACL-based visualization of the sheath.[[50]](#footnote-50)

Only one CARTO VIZIGO™ Sheath can be visualized in a Study.

### Building the Matrix

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/VIZIGO\_vis\_acl\_EN.xml

Sheath visualization in the CARTO™ 3 System is based on Advanced Catheter Location (ACL) technology. This technology relies on a combination of magnetic field measurements and current measurements. The magnetic location data and current location data are calibrated to form the matrix when a sensor-based navigation catheter is moved through the chamber of interest.

The better the matrix, the better the visualization. To view the matrix, click Help > Show Visualization Matrix.

![vizigo_matrix.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 131: CARTO VIZIGO™ Sheath Visualization with Matrix

The sheath is not sensor based, so it cannot be used for building the matrix. If a sensor-based catheter is within the sheath, it must be extended outside the sheath before it can be used for building the matrix.

### Paired Visualization

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/VIZIGO\_pairing\_EN.xml

DOR chart, 4.1

When the system detects that a CARTO VIZIGO™ Sheath and a supported sensor-based focal ablation catheter are both connected, the System automatically pairs them. The paired sheath and catheter are visualized as one unit using a unified mechanical model.

DOR chart, 5.1, 5.2

If the paired catheter is disconnected, the sheath is unpaired. When the catheter is reconnected, pairing is restored automatically.

The system cannot detect which catheter is inserted in the sheath. This means that you must manually pair or unpair the sheath in certain situations.

DOR chart, 4.2, 7.2

* If a catheter supported for paired visualization is connected to the PIU while a diagnostic catheter is inserted in the sheath, you must unpair the sheath. After the sheath is unpaired, both catheters are visualized.

If you do not manually unpair the sheath, the System attempts to visualize the sheath paired with the ablation catheter.

DOR chart, 4.2.1

* If a supported catheter is inserted in the sheath after it was manually unpaired, you must pair it again.

By default, both the catheter and the sheath are displayed when they are paired. You can change the display settings.

See also:

* ["Sheath Visualization Options"](#target9000081)
* ["Pairing or Unpairing the Sheath"](#target9000082)
* ["Troubleshooting CARTO VIZIGO™ Sheath Visualization"](#target9000083)

### Interpolation Limit

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/VIZIGO\_limit\_EN.xml

DOR chart, 5.2

The mechanical model for visualization interpolates the position of the catheter shaft when the catheter extends beyond the sheath. If the catheter extends too far out of the sheath, visualization of the connecting shaft between the sheath and the catheter disappears. Visualization of the catheter and the sheath are maintained.

![vizigo_break_v7p3.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 132: Paired Visualization when Catheter Extends Beyond Interpolation Limit

## CARTO VIZIGO™ Software Setup

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/VIZIGO\_setup\_EN.xml

Standard CARTO™ 3 System connector cables (yellow or blue) are used to connect the CARTO VIZIGO™ Sheath to the PIU.

1. Connect the sheath to the connector cable before connecting the cable to the PIU.

Connecting the cable to the PIU first may lead to problems with the Auto-ID recognition.

1. Connect the cable to the appropriate socket (blue or yellow).

To ensure best sheath visualization, connect the connector cable attached to the CARTO VIZIGO™ sheath to one of the compatible PIU sockets based on the following order of recommendation:

1. REF/DECA, 20 POLE A, or 20 POLE B
2. DECA
3. QUAD B

![VIZIGO_PIU_connections.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 133: PIU Connections

A change in the connection status of the sheath is displayed in the Catheter Setup screen and in the Main Map Viewer within 10 seconds of making the change.

## Sheath Visualization Options

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/VIZIGO\_vis\_intro\_EN.xml

### Define Sheath Visualization

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/VIZIGO\_vis\_define\_v7\_EN.xml

When you connect a CARTO VIZIGO™ Bi-Directional Guiding Sheath, it is automatically identified by the system. The sheath icon appears in the Catheter Setup screen at the socket to which it is connected.

![VIZIGO_cath_icon_setup.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 134: Sheath Icon in Catheter Setup Screen

Click the sheath icon to display its visualization settings. Adjust the visualization settings as required.

![cath_setup_vizigo.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Table 66: Sheath Visualization Options

| Option | Description |
| --- | --- |
| Sheath Image | When selected, the sheath image is displayed in the Main Map Viewer and Additional Map Viewer.  If the sheath and catheter are paired, this setting interacts with the catheter's Tip Only setting as follows:   * Tip Only not selected: display of sheath, catheter and connecting shaft   ![VIZIGO_view_full.png](data:image/png;base64...)   * Tip Only selected: display of sheath and catheter, but no connecting shaft   The tip and the sheath are still aligned.  ![VIZIGO_view_tip.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| Label | When selected, the label is displayed at the distal end of the sheath in the Main Map Viewer and Additional Map Viewer.  ![VIZIGO_view_label_v7p3.png](data:image/png;base64...)  The default label is the connector label in the Catheter Setup screen. In the text box, you can enter a new label for the sheath (up to 3 characters).  The Label check box is available only when Sheath Image is selected. The text box is always available. |
| Shaft Color | Sets the color for the sheath. |
| Paired with | Shows whether the sheath is paired with a catheter for visualization.  By default, the sheath is paired with the catheter connected to the MAP socket if that catheter is supported for paired visualization.  If the paired catheter is disconnected, Paired with is automatically set to None.  If a catheter that is not supported for paired visualization is in the sheath, you must manually set Paired with to None. |

The new settings are applied immediately.

All the sheath visualization settings except for Paired with are saved in the template.
