Wait a few minutes for communication to be established between the PIU and the workstation.  3. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error 25  Failure in PIU Loc Rx card BIT. | Perform the following to determine if the problem was caused by the PIU, or by one of the catheters or their extension cables:  1. Wait a few minutes for communication to be established between the PIU and the workstation.  4. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department.  5. If the problem has disappeared, reconnect each cable and catheter, one by one, and check if the errors reappears after a particular cable or catheter connection. |

Wait a few minutes for communication to be established between the PIU and the workstation.  3. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |

Wait a few minutes for communication to be established between the PIU and the workstation.  4. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |

This may be caused by a defective catheter or its extension cable, or by a defective Loc Rx card in the PIU.  Perform the following to determine if the problem is caused by the PIU, or the catheter or the extension cable:  1. Disconnect the extension cable from the MAP socket. If the error message disappears, the catheter or its extension cable is defective. Verify that there is no moisture in the connectors of the catheter and the extension cable. Replace the faulty catheter or cable.  2. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department to report a PIU issue. |

Perform the following to determine if the problem is caused by the PIU, or the catheter or the extension cable:  1. Disconnect the extension cable from the REF/DECA socket. If the error message disappears, the catheter or its extension cable is defective. Verify that there is no moisture in the connectors of the catheter and the extension cable. Replace the faulty catheter or cable.  2. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department to report a PIU issue. |

Perform the following to determine if the problem is caused by the PIU, or the catheter or the extension cable:  1. Disconnect the extension cable from the 20 POLE A socket. If the error message has disappeared, the catheter or its extension cable is defective. Verify that there is no moisture in the connectors of the catheter and the extension cable. Replace the faulty catheter or cable.  2. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department to report a PIU issue. |
| Error\* 43  20 POLE B: defective catheter or cable. | The voltage supplied by the Rx card to the 20 POLE B socket is out of tolerance. This may be caused by a defective catheter or its cable or by a defective Rx card in the PIU.  Perform the following to determine if the problem is caused by the PIU, or the catheter or the extension cable:  1. Disconnect the extension cable from the 20 POLE B socket. If the error disappeared, the catheter or the extension cable is defective. Verify that there is no moisture in the connectors of the catheter and the extension cable. Replace the catheter or the cable.  2. If the error persists, the problem is in the PIU. Contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error 44  ULTRASOUND: defective catheter or cable. | Voltage supplied by the Loc Rx card to the ULTRASOUND socket exceeds the tolerance settings. This may be caused by a defective catheter or its cable, or by a defective Loc Rx card in the PIU.  Perform the following to determine if the problem is caused by the PIU, or the catheter or the extension cable:  1. Disconnect the extension cable from the ULTRASOUND socket. If the error message has disappeared, the catheter or its extension cable is defective. Verify that there is no moisture in the connectors of the catheter and the extension cable. Replace the faulty catheter or cable.  2. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department to report a PIU issue. |
| Alert 79  Force reading accuracy might be reduced. | The force catheter indicates a force reading with reduced accuracy.  To ensure accurate force readings, perform the following:  1. Verify that there is no other catheter or other metallic object in close proximity to the force catheter tip.  2. If the force reading is not near zero when the catheter is not in contact with tissue, perform zeroing. |
| Alert 80  Invalid force measurements. | Ensure that all four tip electrodes are outside the sheath. |
| Alert 81  Force catheter warm-up. | Temperature cannot be read from the force catheter. While the force catheter in its 15-minute warm-up phase, force measurement accuracy might be reduced.  After 15 minutes from catheter connection, perform zeroing again. |
| Alert 82  Force measurements might be inaccurate. | Force integrity is not calculated and therefore the force measurements received from the force catheter might be inaccurate.  If the problem persists, switch the PIU Power Supply off and on again. Wait until communication between the PIU and the workstation is established. |
| Alert 83  Invalid force measurements. | Force measurements received from the force catheter are invalid. It is possible to continue using the catheter; however, force measurement is disabled.  To use force data, perform the following until resolved:  1. Disconnect and reconnect the catheter.  2. Restart the application.  3. Switch the PIU power supply off and on again. Wait until communication between the PIU and the workstation is established. |
| Alert 84  Force reading accuracy is reduced. | The force catheter indicates a force reading with reduced accuracy.  The catheter is too close to the Location Pad. If necessary, continue the study without force data. |
| Alert 85  Force is not displayed due to high interference. | High interference is detected to the force integrity. Force data cannot be displayed.  The interference may be caused by another catheter or other metal object in a close proximity to the force catheter tip.  To receive force readings, move the interfering catheter farther away from the force catheter tip.  If no catheter is found near the force catheter tip, verify that the force catheter is not in contact with the tissue and perform zeroing. |
| Alert 86  Force catheter requires zeroing. | To ensure accurate force readings, verify that the force reading is near zero when the catheter is not in contact with tissue. If the force reading is not near zero when the catheter is not in contact with tissue, perform zeroing. |
| Alert 87  Force catheter may require zeroing. | To ensure accurate force readings, verify that the force reading is near zero when the catheter is not in contact with tissue. If the force reading is not near zero when the catheter is not in contact with tissue, perform zeroing. |
| Alert 88  Force reading accuracy might be reduced. | Interference is detected to the force integrity.  The interference may be caused by another catheter or other metal object in a close proximity to the force catheter tip.  To ensure accurate force readings, move the interfering catheter farther away from the force catheter tip.  If no catheter is found near the force catheter tip, verify that the force catheter is not in contact with the tissue and perform zeroing. |
| Alert 89  Force reading accuracy is reduced. | The force catheter indicates a force reading with reduced accuracy, due to high lateral force applied to the catheter tip. |
| Alert 90  MAP: test catheter detected. | The catheter connected to the MAP socket is detected as a test catheter. It is not for human use!  This catheter is intended for system testing by qualified service personnel. While the testing catheter is connected, maps are limited to 30 points. |
| Alert 91  REF/DECA: test catheter detected. | The catheter connected to the REF/DECA socket is detected as a test catheter. It is not for human use! If the error persists, perform the following steps until resolved:  1. Fully disconnect and reconnect the eco cable, connected to the MAP socket.  2. Replace the eco cable. |

| Alert 227  Pacing routed to micro-electrodes. Ablation disabled. | Ablation from tip electrode is disabled when tip micro-electrodes are selected for stimulation. |
| Error 228  Eco cable not connected to QUAD or DECA socket. | The eco cable is partially connected. In order to enable the eco cable functionality connect both of the eco cable’s connectors to the PIU.  If both connectors of the eco cable are connected to the PIU and the error persists, perform the following until resolved:  1. Fully disconnect and reconnect the eco cable to the PIU.  2. Replace the eco cable.  3. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Alert 229  Eco cable not connected to MAP socket. | The eco cable is partially connected. In order to enable the eco cable functionality connect both of the eco cable’s connectors to the PIU.  If both connectors of the eco cable are connected to the PIU and the error persists, perform the following until resolved:  1. Fully disconnect and reconnect the eco cable to the PIU.  2. Replace the eco cable.  3. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error 255  Unstable Body Reference location. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Ensure that the Back Patches' sensors are in place.  2. Ensure that the patient is at rest.  3. Ensure that neither Fluoroscope nor Table is moving.  This message will close when the problem is resolved. |
| Error 256  Patient Body Reference has moved. | The patient Body Reference (Back Patches) has changed position and cannot be compensated by the system. No new data can be added to existing maps unless the error is resolved.  Perform the following until resolved:  1. If the error occurred after a change in the Fluoroscope or patient table position, return the Fluoroscope or patient table to its previous position.  2. Verify that the patient is relaxed and lying flat.  3. If not resolved, click "Learn new Body Reference". Previous maps will no longer be valid for addition of new data unless "Continue" is clicked to accept the expected Map Shift. |
| Alert 257  Back Patch detached. | One or more of the Back Patches has partially detached or has insufficient contact with the skin.  Perform the following:  1. Open the Location Setup.  2. Identify the detached Patch.  3. Press the Patch to the skin, without moving it. |
| Error 258  Magnetic data streaming error. | Wait a few seconds. If the error does not disappear, perform the following until resolved:  1. Switch the Power Supply to the PIU off and on again. Wait a few minutes for communication to be established between the PIU and the workstation.  2. If the error reappears, restart the workstation application.  3. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error 260  Data synchronization error. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Switch the Power Supply to the PIU off and on again. Wait a few minutes for communication to be established.  2. Wiring to all patches is well connected. |

| Alert 318  MAP: catheter in reduced accuracy zone. | The catheter connected to the MAP socket is within the reduced accuracy zone.  If the catheter cannot be moved into the accuracy zone, be aware that accuracy will be reduced. |

If the catheter is placed within the mapping zone but the error persists, this may be due to severe metal interference, or due to a defective catheter or its extension cable. Replace the catheter extension cable.  3. Replace the catheter. |

| Error 370  Mapping catheter not connected. | No catheter is recognized at the socket selected for mapping. Perform the following until the Acquire button is enabled:  1. Verify that a mapping catheter is connected to the socket selected for mapping.  2. Change the selection of the mapping catheter socket.  3. Disconnect and reconnect the catheter and ensure good connection.  4. Replace the cable or the catheter. |
| Error 371  Cannot acquire points with mapping catheter. | None of the electrodes of the selected mapping catheter are available for mapping. Perform the following until the Acquire button is enabled:  1. Ensure that catheter visualization initialization has finished and that electrodes selected for mapping are out of sheath.  2. Ensure that the selected mapping catheter is visualized. If not, check that the Calibration Matrix is sufficient in the region of the catheter.  3. Disconnect and reconnect the catheter and ensure good connection.  4. Replace the cable or the catheter. |
| Error 372  Cannot acquire points with a non-mapping catheter. | A non-mapping catheter is connected to the socket selected for mapping. Points cannot be acquired using that catheter. Perform the following until Acquiring is enabled:  1. Replace the catheter with a mapping catheter.  2. Change the selection of the mapping catheter socket. |

| Alert 374  No ventricular Reference Annotations are detected. | Body Surface signals are low quality. Perform the following:  1. Verify good connection of ECG electrodes to the patient.  2. Verify proper connection of the ECG cable.  3. Inspect the precordial lead signals and verify that there is no noise. |
| Alert 375  No atrial Reference Annotations are detected. | The intracardiac signals are low quality. Perform the following:  1. Verify that the reference catheter is properly connected.  2. Verify that the bipolar reference signals are of good quality and include atrial activity. |
| Alert 376  Atrial Reference Annotation might be compromised. | Atrial Reference Annotation might be compromised due to low quality of Body Surface signals. Perform the following:  1. Verify good connection of ECG electrodes to the patient.  2. Verify proper connection of the ECG cable.  3. Inspect the precordial lead signals and verify that there is no noise. |
| Alert 377  No atrial Reference Annotations are detected. | Atrial activations are concurrent with ventricular activations. Perform the following:  1. Try repositioning the reference catheter in the chamber to improve the atrial signal timing.  2. Consider using a Body Surface Reference Channel, if applicable.  3. Use a different Reference Criterion. |
| Alert 379  Reference Annotation Algorithm is initializing. | The Reference Annotation calculation is in progress. Wait few seconds until calculation is complete. |
| Alert 382  Reference Annotations are momentarily unavailable | The Reference Annotation Algorithm is adjusting the detection threshold. Wait a few seconds until annotations are detected again. |
| Error 399  Ref. & Map annotations selected from same catheter. | Map points cannot be built. Select Reference and Mapping annotations from different catheters. |
| Alert 400  No Reference Annotations are detected. | Verify that the Reference Annotations are detected consistently in the Annotation Viewer. |
| Error 401  Map points cannot be acquired. | Perform the following until point Acquisition is enabled and the issue is resolved:  1. In the Map Setup, verify that Reference Annotation and Mapping Annotation are detected consistently.  2. Verify that the Patch unit and the mapping catheter are connected properly and detected by the system.  3. Verify that there is no severe distortion error related to the mapping catheter.  4. Verify that at least one mapping channel of the selected type (bipolar or unipolar) is recorded.  5. Switch the power supply to the PIU off and on again. Wait a few minutes for communication to be established between the PIU and the workstation. |
| Alert 402  MAP: magnetic distortion. | A distortion in the magnetic field has been detected near the catheter connected to the MAP socket. The location data received from this catheter may be inaccurate.  Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that metal objects have not been introduced into the field.  2. Verify that the Fluoroscopy Detector is not too close to patient's chest. If it is, raise its position.  3. Verify that the Fluoroscope tube is not too close to the Location Pad. If it is, raise the table, or change the Fluoroscope position so that the tube is farther away from the Location Pad.  4. Verify that the handle of the catheter or the eco cable, connected to the MAP socket, are at least 30 cm from the Location Pad. |
| Error\* 410  MAP: severe magnetic distortion. | A severe distortion in the magnetic field has been detected near the catheter connected to the MAP socket. Verify that the Fluoroscopy Detector is not too close to patient's chest. If it is, raise its position.  3. Verify that the Fluoroscope tube is not too close to the Location Pad. If it is, raise the table, or change the Fluoroscope position so that the tube is farther away from the Location Pad.  4. Verify that the handle of the catheter or the eco cable, connected to the MAP socket, are at least 30 cm from the Location Pad.  5. Replace the cable of the catheter.  6. Replace the catheter. |
| Alert 411  REF/DECA: magnetic distortion. | A distortion in the magnetic field has been detected near the catheter connected to the REF/DECA socket. If it is, raise the table, or change the Fluoroscope position so that the tube is farther away from the Location Pad.  4. Verify that the handle of the catheter and the eco adapter are at least 30 cm from the Location Pad. |
| Alert 412  Patch 1: magnetic distortion. | A distortion in the magnetic field has been detected near Back Patch 1. Mapping is not recommended under these conditions. Verify that the Patch unit is at least 50 cm from the Location Pad.  If the problem persists, replace the Patch Unit. |

| Error\* 428  20 POLE B: severe magnetic distortion. | A severe distortion in the magnetic field has been detected near the catheter connected to the 20 POLE B socket. The location data received from this catheter are not accurate.  Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that metal objects have not been introduced into the field.  2. Verify that the Fluoroscope tube is not too close to the Location Pad. If it is, raise the table, or change the Fluoroscope position so that the tube is farther from the Location Pad.  3. Verify that the Fluoroscopy Detector is not too close to patient's chest. If it is, raise its position.  4. Verify that the catheter's handle is at least 30 cm from the Location Pad.  5. Replace the cable of the catheter.  6. Replace the catheter. |

| Error\* 980  SPU: Connected to 20 POLE B in PIU instead of 20 POLE A. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that the extension cable is securely connected to the 20 POLE A socket on the PIU. Confirm the connection in the Catheter Setup screen.  2. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 981  SPU: Connected to 20 POLE A in PIU instead of 20 POLE B. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that the extension cable is securely connected to the 20 POLE B socket on the PIU. Confirm the connection in the Catheter Setup screen.  2. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 982  SPU: Synchronization error. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that the extension cables are securely connected to the 20 POLE A and 20 POLE B sockets. Confirm the connection in the Catheter Setup screen.  2. If pacing is active through the catheter connected to the SPU, stop pacing.  3. Wait 30 seconds. If the error remains, disconnect the catheter and reboot the SPU.  4. If the error persists, switch the PIU Power Supply off and on again. Please note that power reset is not recommended during procedure  5. Replace the extension cable between the 20 POLE A socket on the PIU and the To Pole A socket on the SPU.  6. Replace the extension cable between the 20 POLE B socket on the PIU and the To Pole B socket on the SPU.  7. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 983  SPU: ECG self-test failure. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Disconnect the catheter and reboot the SPU.  2. If the error persists, switch the PIU Power Supply off and on again. Please note that power reset is not recommended during procedure.  3. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 984  SPU: Version mismatch. | Disconnect the catheter and reboot the SPU.  If the problem re-occurs after SPU power reboot, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 985  SPU: Communication error. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that the SPU’s front LED is lit. If not, connect the Power Supply cable to the SPU.  2. Verify that the fiber-optic cable is securely connected to the PIU and the SPU.  3. Wait until the LED indicates that the SPU is ready.  4. If the error persists, switch the PIU Power Supply off and on again. Please note that power reset is not recommended during a procedure  5. Replace the fiber-optic cable.  6. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Alert 986  SPU: Temperature is high. | Verify that the SPU vent openings are not covered. |
| Error\* 987  SPU: Temperature reading is out of range. | DO NOT TOUCH SPU! MAY CAUSE BURN.  If the problem persists after SPU power reboot, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 988  SPU: 20 POLE B is not connected. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that the extension cable is securely connected to the 20 POLE B socket on the PIU. Confirm the connection in the Catheter Setup screen.  2. Replace the extension cable between the 20 POLE B socket on the PIU and the To Pole B socket on the SPU.  3. Disconnect the catheter and reboot the SPU.  4. If the error persists, switch the PIU Power Supply off and on. Please note that power reset is not recommended during a procedure.  5. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 989  SPU: Unsupported pacing channels. | Stop pacing.  Perform the following until resolved:  1.If there is pacing through the Recording system, define pacing channels as described in the Signal Processing Unit Instructions for Use and Release Notes.  2. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 991  SPU: Connected to REF/DECA in PIU instead of 20 POLE A. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that the extension cable is securely connected to the 20 POLE A socket on the PIU. Confirm the connection in the Catheter Setup screen.  2. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 992  SPU: Connected to REF/DECA in PIU instead of 20 POLE B. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that the extension cable is securely connected to the 20 POLE B socket on the PIU. Confirm the connection in the Catheter Setup screen.  2. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 993  SPU: Power card sensor reading is out of range. | Disconnect the catheter and reboot the SPU.  If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 994  SPU: Initialization not complete. | Disconnect the catheter and reboot the SPU.  If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 995  SPU: 20 POLE A is not connected. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that the extension cable is securely connected to the 20 POLE A socket on the PIU. Confirm the connection in the Catheter Setup screen.  2. Replace the extension cable between the 20 POLE A socket on the PIU and the To Pole A socket on the SPU.  3. Disconnect the catheter and reboot the SPU.  4. If the error persists, switch the PIU Power Supply off and on. Please note that power reset is not recommended during a procedure.  5. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Alert 996  SPU: Synchronization warning. | Verify that the extension cables are securely connected to the 20 POLE A and 20 POLE B sockets. Confirm the connection in the Catheter Setup screen.  If the problem persists for more than 10 minutes, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 997  SPU: Software installation incomplete. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Verify that the SPU’s front LED is lit. If not, connect the Power Supply cable to the SPU.  2. Verify that the fiber-optic cable is securely connected to the PIU and the SPU.  3. Wait until the LED indicates that the SPU is ready. If the error persists, disconnect the catheter and reboot the SPU.  4. If the error persists, switch the PIU Power Supply off and on again. Please note that power reset is not recommended during a procedure.  5. Restart the CARTO™ 3 Application.  6. Replace the fiber-optic cable.  7. If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 998  SPU: ECG data streaming error. | Disconnect the catheter and reboot the SPU.  If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Error\* 999  SPU: Self-test in progress. | Disconnect the catheter extender from the SPU.  Keep the catheter disconnected until the LED changes from blue to steady green. |
| Error 1003  The Patch Unit appears disconnected. | Verify that the Patch Unit is properly connected. This message will close as the Patch Unit is detected. |
| Error 1006  Back Patch sensor error. | The magnetic sensor of one of the Back Patches is defective. Replace the Back Patches cable, or the whole Patch unit. |
| Alert 1007  Chest Patch sensor error. | The magnetic sensor of one of the Chest Patches is defective. Replace the Chest Patches cable, or the whole Patch unit.  Note that this issue may affect ACL catheter visualization and ACCURESP™ Module data. |
| Alert 1008  Back Patch disconnected or fully detached. | One of the Back Patches is disconnected or fully detached from the patient.  Identify the disconnected patch in the Location Setup, and then perform the following until resolved:  1. Reconnect the wire to the patch.  2. Reattach the patch to the patient. |
| Alert 1009  Chest Patch disconnected or fully detached. | One of the Chest Patches is disconnected or fully detached from the patient.  Identify the disconnected patch in the Location Setup, and then perform the following until resolved:  1. Reconnect the wire to the patch.  2. Reattach the patch to the patient. |
| Error 1011  Back Patches cable not detected. | Verify that the cable for the Back Patches is properly connected to the Patch Unit. If the problem persists, replace the cable. |
| Alert 1012  Chest Patches cable not detected. | Verify that the cable for the Chest Patches is properly connected to the Patch Unit. If the problem persists, replace the cable. |
| Alert 1049  REF/DECA: catheter in reduced accuracy zone. | The catheter connected to the REF/DECA socket is within a reduced accuracy zone.  If there is no option to move the catheter into the accuracy zone, be aware that accuracy will be reduced. |
| Error\* 1050  REF/DECA: catheter out of mapping range. | The catheter connected to the REF/DECA socket appears to be out of the mapping range.  If the catheter is placed within the mapping zone but the error persists, this may be due to a severe metal interference, or due to a defective catheter or its extension cable. Perform the following until resolved:  1. Make sure that there is no severe metal interference to the catheter or to the Location Pad, from the Fluoroscope or other metal objects.  2. Replace the catheter extension cable.  3. Replace the catheter. |
| Error 1052  Back Patches are out of mapping range. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Ensure that the Back Patches' sensors are in place.  2. Open the Location Setup screen and move the patches into the mapping range, by adjusting the Location Pad or the patches.  3. Verify that the Fluoroscopy Detector is not too close to patient's chest. If it is, raise its position  4. Verify that the Fluoroscope tube is not too close to the Location Pad. If it is, raise the table or change the Fluoroscope position so that the tube is farther away from the Location Pad.  5. Replace the Patch Unit.  If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Alert 1053  Chest Patches are out of mapping range. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Ensure that the Chest Patches' sensors are in place.  2. Open the Location Setup screen and move the patches into the mapping range, by adjusting the Location Pad or the patches.  3. Verify that the Fluoroscopy Detector is not too close to patient's chest. If it is, raise its position.  4. Verify that the Fluoroscope tube is not too close to the Location Pad. If it is, raise the table or change the Fluoroscope position so that the tube is farther away from the Location Pad.  5. Replace the Patch Unit.  If the problem persists, contact Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Alert 1054  Chest Patch: partially detached or bad contact. | One or more of the Chest Patches has partially detached or has bad contact with the skin.  Perform the following:  1. Open the Location Setup.  2. Identify the detached Patch.  3. Press the Patch to the skin, without moving it. |
| Alert 1101  The hard disk is almost full. | When possible, exit the study and create more space by backing up patient data, and then deleting it from the hard disk drive. |
| Alert 1102  The hard disk is full. | When possible, exit the study and create more space by backing up patient data, and then deleting it from the hard disk drive. |
| Alert 1103  Memory has almost no free space. | Avoid acquiring more data to the study. System performance might be degraded. When possible, restart the CARTO™ 3 Application. |
| Alert 1104  There is not enough free memory. | Reboot the workstation. |
| Error 1108  Reference conflict. | Current Body Reference (Back Patches) location is different from the active map's Reference location. The Acquire button is disabled.  Points cannot be acquired to the active map. Open a new map, or select another map. |
| Alert 1201  ECG limb lead disconnection. | An ECG limb lead has a bad connection or is totally disconnected. Please check the four ECG limb connections. |
| Info 1210  Acquisition disabled. | Real-time point and FAM acquisition cannot be performed in Timeline mode. Switch to real-time mode to continue. |
| Info 1220  Continuous acquisition disabled. | When the CFAE Module is in use continuous acquisition is disabled. To enable continuous acquisition, disable the CFAE Module. |
| Alert 1221  Continuous acquisition disabled. | Continuous acquisition is disabled due to one of the following:  1. The number of points in the active map reached the limit for continuous acquisition.  2. The number of points in the study reached the limit for continuous acquisition.  To continue continuous point acquisition, either switch to another active map or start a new study. |
| Alert 1222  Continuous acquisition disabled. | Continuous acquisition is disabled due to an error related to the force catheter or the CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Module. |
| Alert 1402  No communication with Fluoroscopy system. | Verify that the communication cable between the CARTO™ 3 System and to the Fluoroscopy system is properly connected. |
| Alert 1403  Registration to Fluoroscopy system not completed. | Complete the registration process before continuing. |
| Alert 1404  Unsupported table or Fluoroscopy position. | The registration is invalid due to unsupported position of the Fluoroscopy system or the patient table. Importing new Fluoroscopy captures is disabled and location data may be inaccurate. Move the Fluoroscopy system or the patient table to the last used position. |
| Alert 1406  No communication with Fluoroscopy system. | Perform the following until resolved:  1. Check that the DICOM network cable is properly connected between the CARTO™ 3 System workstation and the DICOM network.  2. Check that the DICOM network cable is properly connected between the Fluoroscopy system and the DICOM network.  3. Verify that the DICOM configuration parameters are correct in both the CARTO™ 3 System and the Fluoroscopy system. |
| Alert 1500  Respiration gating training is required. | Start respiration gating training. |
| Alert 2600  Simultaneous ablation and pacing not allowed. | To enable ablation through these electrodes, deselect Stimulation Routing from them. |
| Alert 2601  End-expirium data missing. | The respiration-related data, required for proper operation of the CARTO VISITAG™ Module, is not available.  Perform the following until resolved:  1. Perform Respiration training using ACCURESP™ Module.  2. Disable the Position Respiration Adjustment option in CARTO VISITAG™ Module preferences. |
| Alert 2604  Force values missing. | The force data required for one or more of the current VISITAG™ Preferences is not available.  Perform the following until resolved:  1. Ensure that CARTO SMARTTOUCH™ Module license is active.  2. Ensure that a force catheter is connected and is used as the ablation catheter.  3. Ensure that the function is working properly and force graphs show valid data.  4. Disable the Force Over Time filter in CARTO VISITAG™ Module preferences. |
| Alert 2606  Stability+ training is active. | VISITAG™ Sites will not be created during this period due to unknown catheter stability.  Stability+ training may take up to 2 minutes. |
| Alert 2607  Stability+ data is invalid. | VISITAG™ Sites will not be created during this period due to unknown catheter stability.  The data required to use Stability+ is not valid.  Perform the following until resolved:  1. Confirm that the ablation catheter is in the mapping zone and that it is connected to the PIU or extension cable.  2. Verify that the patches are properly connected and positioned, and that they do not overlap on the patient's body, including ECG and ablation patches.  3. Verify that no metal objects have been introduced into the field.  Stability+ reset will start when the issue is resolved. |
| Alert 2608  Stability+ reset is active. | VISITAG™ Sites will not be created during this period due to unknown catheter stability.  Stability+ reset may take up to 20 seconds. |
| Error 6150  Ultrasound catheter sensor error. | The magnetic sensor of the catheter is disconnected. To continue, replace the catheter cable. If that does not resolve the problem, replace the catheter. |
| Alert 6951  Ultrasound catheter is in reduced accuracy zone. | If there is no option to move the catheter into the accuracy zone, be aware that accuracy will be reduced. |

# Troubleshooting

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Troubleshooting\_intro\_system\_EN.xml

Difficulties with the CARTO™ 3 System can be approached in various ways, depending on the nature of the problem.

* Users can determine the source of the difficulty based on various online messages that can appear on the main screen. Such messages could relate to:
  + Workstation (computer) and PIU communication
  + Catheter or patch connections
  + Catheter or patch location
* The System Status panel is displayed with all messages relating to system function.
* Users can follow the steps suggested herein to help determine the exact nature of the problem.

The Biosense Webster Service and Support Department can be contacted by telephone to provide assistance with any issues not covered in this chapter.

## Troubleshooting Tools

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ServiceTroubleshooting\_EN.xml

The Troubleshooting tools provide valuable information for investigating a problem or suspected problem in system functioning. When customer support staff are contacted about a service issue, they might instruct users to collect information from the Troubleshooting tools. In some cases, the collected information might help users implement a solution directly. If not, the collected information will help customer support staff prepare for an on-site service visit.

### Viewing Troubleshooting Information

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/OpenTroubleshooting\_EN.xml

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| 1. | In the Help menu, click Troubleshooting.  The Troubleshooting window opens, displaying the Communication Status pane.  ![help_troubleshooting_simulator_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...) |
| 2. | Review the communication status between the CARTO™ 3 Workstation and the following:   * PIU * DICOM network * EP Recording System * Ultrasound system   Communication status is indicated according to the key below:   | Status | Line Connecting Components | | --- | --- | | No physical connection | None | | Unsatisfactory communication | Red with ![help_troubleshooting_exclamation.png](data:image/png;base64...) | | Satisfactory communication | Green | |
| 3. | Review additional information by clicking the buttons at the bottom left of the window.  ![help_troubleshooting_simulator_all_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...)   |  |  | | --- | --- | | 3.1 | Click BIT Results to review results of built-in tests.  The Power-Up BIT tab displays results of tests performed when the system was switched on. The Ongoing BIT tab displays results of the most recent ongoing test.  Pass results are indicated by ![help_troubleshooting_check_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...). Fail results are indicated by ![help_troubleshooting_minus_v7.png](data:image/png;base64...).  The elapsed time since the test is displayed on the right of the pane.  For detailed results for a specific test, click the appropriate line. | | 3.2 | Click CALi Values to review calibration values.  To update the information in this pane, click Refresh. | | 3.3 | Click Values to review values related to various sensors.  The values in this pane are updated automatically in real time. | |
| 4. | To close any pane, click its corresponding button. |
| 5. | To close the Troubleshooting window, click Close. |

## Metal Interference

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Magnetic\_distortion\_troubleshooting\_7p3\_EN.xml

If the CARTO™ 3 System detects metal interference, a System message (info, alert, or error) specifies which catheter or patch is affected, and provides troubleshooting information. If the interference affects the patches or the mapping catheter, indication appears near the Acquisition panel indicating the severity of the interference (minor, moderate, or severe). Follow the instructions in the message to resolve the problem.

Table 78: Metal Interference Indication

| Level | Indication | Message | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Minor | ![horseshoe_yellow.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Info | Mapping is possible; however, accuracy may be affected.  Use your common clinical practice for real-time verification of catheter locations throughout the procedure (inspection of IC signals, direct imaging guidance such as fluoroscopy or ultrasound, etc.). Failure to do so might result in incorrect placement of the catheter. |
| Moderate | ![horseshoe_orange.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Alert |
| Severe | ![horseshoe_red.png](data:image/png;base64...) | Error | Acquisition is disabled and the catheter visualization may disappear.  Mapping is not possible under these conditions. |
| Alert | If the affected catheter is not the mapping catheter, the message is an alert. Mapping is possible; however, accuracy may be affected. |

The metal interference detection threshold for Chest Patches can be adjusted to be less sensitive. Consult your Biosense Webster representative for details[[51]](#footnote-51).

To see current metal values related to connected catheters or patches, click the magnet icon.

![Metal_values_window_8p2.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 173: Metal Values Window

|  | Normal  (White) | Low  (Yellow) | Suboptimal  (Orange) | Bad  (Red) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Back Patches (Patch 1,2,3)** | >0 to 5 | >5 to 8 | >8 to 25 | >25 |
| **Chest Patches (Patch 4,5,6)** | >0 to 35  or  >0 to 10 |  | >35 to 75  or  >10 to 75 | >75 |
| **Magnetic Sensor Based Catheters** | >0 to 5 | >5 to 8 | >8 to 25 | >25 |
| \* Value range when the Enhanced Sensitivity check box is selected in CAS Tools | | | | |

## Electrode View

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/troubleshooting\_electrode\_view\_EN.xml

If there are issues with visualization of diagnostic catheters (for example, a spline is not visualized), you can view the unprocessed (raw) electrode positions. This shows the unprocessed positions before the visualization algorithm is applied to display the full catheter. These electrode positions can be displayed even if the catheter or spline cannot be visualized.

To view the electrode positions, press CTRL + W. Electrodes are displayed as brown discs. If electrode numbering is enabled in the Catheter Setup screen, the numbers also appear on the disc.

## Patches View

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Patches\_View\_EN.xml

During a study, the CARTO™ 3 System keeps track of changes between the initial placement of the patches and their current location.

The CARTO™ 3 System may detect a change in the position of one or more of the Back Patches or Chest Patches. This change may be due to an actual change in the position of the patch, patient movement, movement of the Location Pad, or a distortion to the magnetic field.

In **Patches View**, the System shows the distance between the initial placement of the patches and their current location.

* Current patch position is indicated by the standard green (back) and yellow (chest) discs.
* The initial patch location is indicated with striped discs. If the change in position is large, the striped discs are colored red.
* The distance between the initial patch location and the current patch location is indicated in red text next to the current patch location.

To open **Patches View**, click the background of one of the map viewers and press CTRL + P. The view appears in the selected viewer. Press CTRL + P again to hide the view.

If there is a change to the Body Coordinate System (BCS), the new patch positions are considered the initial location.

![patches_view.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 174: Patches View in the Main Map Viewer

## ECG Noise

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/ECG\_Noise\_EN.xml

REP4611 D-V1-1.31.2, PL.8

To minimize ECG noise, the following guidelines should be followed.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Reducing Signal Noise | REP9626 1.9.5  ECG noise is typically generated as the result of improper connection of the body surface ECG patch to the patient. This noise is the most significant during ablation. To resolve this situation, verify proper connection. It is recommended to turn off the Notch filter for this verification. |
| Using Filters to Reduce Noise | Proper setting of the ECG filters enables ECG noise reduction. |
| Induced Noise (50 Hz and HF) | Keep all signal cables (for example, body surface cable, catheter cable, and RF generator cables) as far away as possible from AC fields (for example, Location Pad and cabling, monitors, isolation transformers, power supply, etc.). |
| Noise Generated by the Location Pad | If noise appears on the Recording System when the signals are clear on the CARTO™ 3 System, verify a proper connection between the PIU and the Recording System. If the noise continues, check if the noise is generated by the Location Pad by turning off the Location Pad. If the noise does appear to be generated by the Location Pad, contact the Biosense Webster Service and Support Department. |
| Active Right Leg (RL) | Using Unipolar ECG in a Recording System is possible only for catheters connected to the PIU. The Reference signal is either WCT or IC Reference, according to the CARTO™ 3 System configuration. |

## Communication Problems

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Communication\_Problems\_c3\_EN.xml

REP4611 PL.8b

A failure in communication between the workstation (computer) and the PIU is indicated by a message in the System Status panel that appears for more than a few seconds. If this message appears, any data acquired during the current procedure will not be lost. Once this message automatically disappears, mapping can be resumed.

## Repeater Monitor Display

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/trbl\_monitor\_c3-v4\_EN.xml

If the repeater monitor is on but there is no display, do one of the following:

* From the opening screen, click the System button and then click the Repeater Monitor On button.
* On the Tools menu, select the Repeater Monitor On option (during a CARTO™ 3 Study).

## Disconnecting the Patch Sensor Cable

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/PU\_Detaching\_service\_EN.xml

The CARTO™ 3 System supports the Patch Unit with plug-and-play functionality. Patch Unit or Patch Sensor cables can be replaced. Preserve Patch Sensor cable integrity by handling it with care.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![DxNoteCaution.png](data:image/png;base64...) | *CAUTION*  Handle the Patch Sensor cable with care and avoid pulling it forcefully. Pulling the Patch Sensor cable can strain or damage the cable, connectors, or devices it is connected to. Always disconnect the Patch Sensor cable gently and in a controlled manner. |

When disconnecting the Patch Sensor cable, always pull gently from its reference housing by grasping the sensor strain relief and not by pulling on the cable.

![PU_detach_both.png](data:image/png;base64...)

Figure 175: Patch Sensor Cable Handling

# Appendix: Clinical Data Summary

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/Summary\_Clinical\_Studies\_To\_Determine\_Catheter\_Tip\_Location\_EN.xml

**Summary of Clinical Studies Conducted for Alternative Imaging To Determine Catheter Tip Location**

## Zero/Low Fluoroscopy: A nested sub-study of REAL AF Registry

https://bwiildita.sharepoint.com/sites/prod/Topics/zero-low\_fluoroscopy\_v8p1\_EN.xml

A Real-World Evidence study (REAL AF Registry Sub-Study) evaluating the safety and acute effectiveness of Paroxysmal Atrial Fibrillation ablation with a zero/low fluoroscopy workflow using contemporary radiofrequency technologies (e.g. THERMOCOOL SMARTTOUCH™, THERMOCOOL SMARTTOUCH™ SF) and electroanatomical mapping technologies (e.g. CARTO™ 3 System, CARTO VIZIGO™ Guiding Sheath, PENTARAY™ NAV eco, Webster™ CS, DECANAV™) was performed. The table below summarizes the therapeutic and diagnostic devices used in the study. The primary safety (major procedural complications) and secondary acute effectiveness (achievement of electrical PV isolation) endpoints were met in the REAL AF Registry Sub-Study in which the zero/low fluoroscopy workflow was used for left atrial ablation of paroxysmal atrial fibrillation. The results of the REAL AF Registry Sub-Study support the use of alternative imaging to fluoroscopy to aid in the manipulation and positioning of the mapping catheters/sheaths to the area of interest during a left atrial ablation procedure.
